Canto 1 — Goloka-khaṇḍa

The English translation of the Garga Saṁhitā, an ancient Vaiṣṇava scripture narrating the life of Kṛṣṇa in seventeen cantos.

Inspired by Śrī Nārada, Garga Muni, who was a sage and the priest of the Yadu dynasty, performed several austerities and penances, and as a result he composed this sweet scripture.

Chapter 1 - Description of Śrī-Kṛṣṇa’s Glories

oṁ nārāyaṇaṁ namaskṛtya
naraṁ caiva narottamam
devīṁ sarasvatīṁ vyāsaṁ
tato jayam udīrayet 1.1.1

“Before reciting this Garga-saṁhitā, which is the very means of conquest, one should offer respectful obeisances unto the Personality of Godhead, Nārāyaṇa, unto Nara-Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi, the supermost human being, unto mother Sarasvatī, the goddess of learning, and unto Śrīla Vyāsadeva.”

śarad-vikaca-paṅkaja-śriyam atīva-vidveṣakaṁ
milinda-muni-sevitaṁ kuliśa-kañja-cihnāvṛtam
sphurat-kanaka-nūpuraṁ dalita-bhakta-tāpa-trayaṁ
calad-dyuti-pada-dvayaṁ hṛdi dadhāmi rādhā-pateḥ 1.1.2

“In my heart I place the splendid feet of Rādhā’s Lord, feet that eclipse the glory of autumn lotuses, are served by hosts of bumblebee sages, are marked with lightning and lotus and decorated with glistening golden anklets, and break the three sufferings of the devotees.”

vadana-kamala-niryadyasya pīyūṣam ādyaṁ
pibati jana-varo ’yaṁ pātu so ’yaṁ giraṁ me
badara-vana-vihāraḥ satyavatyāḥ kumāraḥ
praṇata-durita-hāraḥ sārṅga-dhanv-avatāraḥ 1.1.3

“May Satyavatī’s son Vyāsa, who enjoys pastimes at Badarikāsrama, who is the best of men, who drinks the transcendental nectar of the glory of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus face, and who is an incarnation of the Lord who carries the Śārṅga bow, protect my words.”

kadācin naimiṣāraṇye
śrī-gargo jñānināṁ varaḥ
āyayau śaunakaṁ draṣṭuṁ
tejasvī yoga-bhāskaraḥ 1.1.4

“Once Śrī Garga Muni, who was the best of the wise, very powerful, and a brilliant sun of yoga, went to see Śaunaka Muni.”

taṁ dṛṣṭvā sahasotthāya
śaunako munibhiḥ saha
pūjayām āsa padyādyair
upacārair vidhānataḥ 1.1.5

“Seeing him, Śaunaka and the sages at once stood up and worshiped him by washing his feet and presenting many offerings according to ancient tradition.”

śrī-śaunaka uvāca
satāṁ paryaṭanaṁ dhanyaṁ
gṛhināṁ śāntaye smṛtam
nṛṇām antas tamo-hārī
sādhur eva na bhāskaraḥ 1.1.6

Śrī Śaunaka said: “The auspicious travels of saintly devotees bring peace to they who stay at home. It is a saintly devotee, and not the sun, the removes the darkness in people’s hearts.”

tasmān me hṛdi sambhūtaṁ
sandehaṁ nāśaya prabho
katidhā śrī-harer viṣṇor
avatāro bhavaty alam 1.1.7

“Therefore, O lord, please destroy the doubt in my heart. How often does Lord Viṣṇu, who removes all that is inauspicious, descend to this world?”

śrī-garga uvāca
sādhu pṛṣṭaṁ tvayā brahman
bhagavad-guṇa-varṇanam
śṛṇvatāṁ gadatāṁ yad vai
pṛcchatāṁ vitanoti śam 1.1.8

Śrī Garga Muni said: “O brāhmaṇa, you have asked a worthy question. The description of the Lord’s transcendental qualities brings auspiciousness to they who hear, speak, and ask.”

atraivodāharantīmam
itihāsaṁ purātanam
yasya śravaṇa-mātreṇa
mahā-doṣaḥ praśamyati 1.1.9

“Of this they tell the story of an event in ancient history, simply by hearing of which great sin is removed.”

mithilā-nāgare pūrvaṁ
bahulāśvaḥ pratāpavān
śrī-kṛṣṇa-bhaktaḥ śāntātmā
babhūva nirahaṅkṛtiḥ 1.1.10

“In the city of Mithilā in ancient times lived a powerful king named Bahulāśva, who was peaceful at heart, free of false ego, and a great devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

ambarād āgatam dṛṣṭvā
nāradaṁ muni-sattamam
sampūjya cāsane sthāpya
kṛtāñjalir abhāṣata 1.1.11

“Seeing the best of sages Nārada decend from the sky, (the king) worshiped him, offered him a seat, and with folded hands addressed him.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
yo ’nādir ātmā puruṣo
bhagavān prakṛteḥ paraḥ
kasmāt tanuṁ samādhatta
tan me brūhi mahā-mate 1.1.12

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “O wise one, please tell me why the beginningless Supreme Person, who is beyond the material energy, places His transcendental form in this world?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
go-sādhu-devatā-vipra-
vedānāṁ rakṣaṇāya vai
tanuṁ dhatte hariḥ sākṣād
bhagavān ātma-līlayā 1.1.13

“To protect the cows, devotees, demigods, and Vedas, Lord Hari, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, placed His own transcendental form and pastimes in this world.”

yathā naṭaḥ sva-līlāyāṁ
mohito na paras tathā
anye dṛṣṭvā ca tan-māyāṁ
mumuhus te muhur muhuḥ 1.1.14

“As a magician is not bewildered by His own magic tricks, although others may be bewildered, so the Supreme Personality of Godhead is not bewildered by the magic tricks He does with His illusory potency, although others who see them are bewildered again and again.”

śri-bahulāśva uvāca
katidhā śrī-harer viṣṇor
avatāro bhavaty alam
sādhūnāṁ rakṣaṇārthaṁ hi
kṛpayā vadā māṁ prabho 1.1.15

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “How often does Lord Viṣṇu, who removes all that is inauspicious, descend to this world to protect the devotees? O lord, please kindly tell me.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
aṁśāṁśo ’ṁśas tathāveśaḥ
kalā pūrṇaḥ prakathyate
vyāsādyaiś ca smṛṭaḥ ṣaṣṭhaḥ
paripūrṇatamaḥ svayam 1.1.16

Śrī Nārada said: “In the Smṛti-śāstra the great sages who have Vyāsa as their leader explain that the Lord descends in six kinds of forms: 1. aṁśāṁśa (a part of a part), 2. aṁśa (a part), 3. āveśa (entrance into a jīva), 4. kalā (a full part), 5. pūrṇa (full), and 6. paripūrṇatama (most full).”

aṁśāṁśas tu marīcy-ādir
aṁśā brahmādayas tathā
kalāḥ kapila-kūrmādyā
āveśā bhārgavādayaḥ 1.1.17

“The aṁśāṁśa incarnations begin with Marīci, the aṁśā incarnations begin with Brahmā, the kalā incarnations begin with Kapila and Kūrmā, and the āveśa incarnations begin with Paraśurāma.”

pūrṇo nṛsiṁho rāmaś ca
śvetadvīpādhipo hariḥ
vaikuṇṭho ’pi tathā yajño
nara-nārāyaṇaḥ smṛtaḥ 1.1.18

“The Smṛti-śāstra explains that the pūrṇa incarnations are 1. Nṛsiṁha, 2. Rāma, 3. Lord Hari, the ruler of Śvetadvīpa, 4. Vaikuṇṭha, 5. Yajña, and 6. Nara- Nārāyaṇa.”

paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān svayam
asaṅkhya-brahmāṇḍa-patir
goloke dhāmni rājate 1.1.19

“The paripūrṇatama form of the Lord is Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the original Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself. He is the master of countless universes. He is splendidly manifest in the realm of Goloka.”

kāryādhikāraṁ kurvantaḥ
sad-aṁśāś te prakirtitāḥ
tat-kārya-bhāraṁ kurvantas
te ’ṁśāṁśā viditāḥ prabhoḥ 1.1.20

“The aṁśa incarnations are said to oversee the execution of the Lord’s mission (in the world). The aṁśāṁśa incarnations are understood to perform the work of executing those missions.”

yeṣām antar-gato viṣṇuḥ
kāryaṁ kṛtvā vinirgataḥ
nānāveṣāvatārāṁś ca
viddhi rājan mahā-mate 1.1.21

“O great soul, O king, please know that in the various āveśa incarnations Lord Viṣṇu enters (a jīva), executes His mission, and then departs.”

dharmaṁ vijñāya kṛtvā yaḥ
punar antaradhīyata
yuge yuge vartamānaḥ
so ’vatāraḥ kalā hareḥ 1.1.22

“Millennium after millennium Lord Hari’s kalā incarnations teach and establish the principles of religion, and then disappear.”

catur-vyūho bhaved yatra
dṛśyante ca rasā nava
ataḥ paraṁ ca vīryāṇi
sa tu pūrṇaḥ prakathyate 1.1.23

“The pūrṇa incarnations are said to include the catur-vyūha incarnations. They are said to be the places where heroic powers and the nine rasas are seen.”

yasmin sarvāṇi tejāṁsi
vilīyante sva-tejasi
taṁ vadanti pare sākṣāt
paripūrṇatamaṁ svayam 1.1.24

“Others say that the form of the Lord where all powers and glories enter is His paripūrṇatama form, the original form of the Lord Himself.”

pūrṇasya lakṣaṇaṁ yatra
taṁ paśyanti pṛthak pṛthak
bhāvenāpi janāḥ so ’yaṁ
paripūrṇatamaḥ svayam 1.1.25

“The transcendental qualities people see distributed among the Lord’s pūrṇa incarnations are all present in the Lord’s original, paripūrṇatama form.”

paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo nānya eva hi
eka-kāryārtham āgatya
koṭi-kāryaṁ cakāra ha 1.1.26

“The Lord’s original, paripūrṇatama form is Śrī Kṛṣṇa and no one else. Coming (to this world) to execute one mission, He executes millions of missions.”

pūrṇaḥ purāṇaḥ puruṣottamottamaḥ
parāt paro yaḥ puruṣaḥ pareśvaraḥ
svayaṁ sadānandamayaṁ kṛpākaraṁ
guṇākaraṁ taṁ śaraṇaṁ vrajāmy aham 1.1.27

“He is perfect and complete. He is the oldest. He is the most exalted of exalted persons. He is greater than the greatest. He is the Supreme Person. He is the master of the exalted. Of He who is full of bliss, who is jewel-mine of mercy, who is a jewel-mine of transcendental virtues, I take shelter.”

śrī-garga uvāca
tac chrutvā harṣito rājā
romāñcī prema-vihvalaḥ
pramṛśya netre ’śru-pūrṇe
nāradaṁ vākyam abravīt 1.1.28

Śrī Garga said: “Hearing this, the king became very happy. Overcome with love and the hairs of his body erect in ecstasy, he wiped his tear-filled eyes and spoke to Nārada.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
paripūrṇatamah sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo kena hetunā
āgato bhārate khaṇḍe
dvāravatyāṁ virājate 1.1.29

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “Why did Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original, paripūrṇatama form of the Lord, come to the land of Bhārata and gloriously appear in Dvārakā?”

tasya goloka-nāthasya
golokaṁ dhāma sundaram
karmāṇy aparimeyāni
brūhi brahman bṛhan-mune 1.1.30

“O brāhmaṇa, O great sage, please describe the countless deeds and beautiful Goloka realm of He who is the master of Goloka.”

yadā tīrthāṭanaṁ kurvan
chata-janma-tapaḥ-paraḥ
tadā sat-saṅgam etyāśu
śrī-kṛṣṇaṁ prāpnuyān naraḥ 1.1.31

“When a human being visits many holy places and devotedly performs a hundred years of austerities, he will attain the association of the devotees. Then he will attain Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself.”

śrī-kṛṣṇa-dāsasya ca dāsa-dāsaḥ
kadā bhaveyaṁ manasārdra-cittaḥ
yo durlabho deva-varaiḥ parātmā
sa me kathaṁ gocara ādi-devaḥ 1.1.32

“When, my heart melting with love, will I think “I am the servant of the servants of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s servants?” Will the Supreme Personality of Godhead, whom even the greatest demigods cannot attain, appear before me?”

dhanyas tvaṁ rāja-śārdūla
śrī-kṛṣṇeṣṭo hari-priyaḥ
tubhyaṁ ca darśanaṁ dātuṁ
bhakteśo ’trāgamiṣyati 1.1.33

“O tiger of kings, you are fortunate. Śrī Kṛṣṇa honors you. Lord Hari loves you. The Lord of the devotees will come to reveal Himself to you.”

Chapter 2 - Description of the Abode of Śrī Goloka

jihvāṁ labdhvāpi yaḥ kṛṣṇam
kīrtanīyaṁ na kīrtayet
labdhvāpi mokṣa-niśreṇiṁ
sa nārohati durmatiḥ 1.2.1

“A person who has a tongue but does not glorify glorious Śrī Kṛṣṇa is a fool. Even if he somehow approaches the staircase to liberation, he cannot climb it.”

atha te sampravakṣyāmi
śrī-kṛṣṇāgamanaṁ bhuvi
asmin vārāha-kalpe vai
yad bhūtaṁ tac chṛṇu prabho 1.2.2

“Now I will describe to you Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s coming to this earth during the Varāha-kalpa. O lord, please hear what happened.”

purā dānava-daityānām
narāṇāṁ khalu bhubhujām
bhūri-bhāra-samākrāntā
pṛthvī go-rūpa-dhāriṇī
anāthavad rudantīva
vedayantī nija-vyatham
kampayantī nijaṁ gātraṁ
brahmāṇaṁ śaraṇaṁ gatā 1.2.3-4

“In ancient times the earth, overcome with a burden of many daityas, dānavas, and human kings, assumed the form of a cow and, helplessly crying, trembling, and describing her troubles, took shelter of the demigod Brahmā.”

brahmāthāśvasya tāṁ sadyaḥ
sarva-deva-gaṇair vṛtaḥ
śaṅkareṇa samaṁ prāgād
vaikuṇṭhaṁ mandiraṁ hareḥ 1.2.5

“Brahmā comforted her and then, accompanied by Śiva and the demigods, went to Lord Hari’s palace in Vaikuṇṭha.”

natvā catur-bhujaṁ viṣṇuṁ
svābhiprāyaṁ jagāda ha
athodvignaṁ deva-gaṇaṁ
śrī-nāthaḥ prāha taṁ vidhim 1.2.6

“Brahmā bowed down before four-armed Lord Viṣṇu and explained why he had come. Then He who is the husband of the goddess of fortune spoke to Brahmā and the troubled demigods.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
kṛṣṇaṁ svayaṁ vigaṇitāṇḍa-patiṁ pareśaṁ
sākṣād akhaṇḍam atidevam atīva-līlam
kāryaṁ kadāpi na bhaviṣyati yaṁ vinā hi
gacchāśu tasya viṣadaṁ padam avyayaṁ tvam 1.2.7

The Blessed Lord said: “Without Kṛṣṇa, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of countless universes, perfect, above the demigods, and the enjoyer of transcendental pastimes, this problem will not be solved. You should go to His eternal and effulgent abode at once.”

śrī-brahmovāca
tvattaḥ paraṁ na jānāmi
paripūrṇatamaṁ svayam
yadi yo ’nyas tasya sākṣāl
lokaṁ darśaya naḥ prabho 1.2.8

Śrī Brahmā said: “I do not know anyone greater and more perfect than You. If there is such a person, please show Him to us, O Lord.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity ukto ’pi hariḥ pūrṇaḥ
sarvair deva-gaṇaiḥ saha
padavīṁ darśayām āsa
brahmāṇḍa-śikharopari
vāma-pādāṅguṣṭha-nakha-
bhinna-brahmāṇḍa-maṣṭake
śrī-vāmanasya vivare
brahma-drava-samākule 1.2.9-10

Śrī Nārada said: “When this was said, perfect Lord Hari showed all the demigods a spiritual river path flowing through the hole Śrī Vāmana made in the universe with His toenail.”

jala-yānena mārgeṇa
bahis te niryayuḥ surāḥ
kaliṅga-bimba-vac cedaṁ
brahmāṇḍaṁ dadṛśus tv adhaḥ 1.2.11

“Going by boat on this path, the demigods left the universe. They saw the universe was like a round kuṭaja fruit far below.”

indrāyaṇa-phalānīva
luṭhanty anyāni vai jale
vilokya vismitāḥ sarve
babhuvus cakitā iva 1.2.12

“When the saw the other universes were like many kuṭaja fruits bobbing in the water (of the Kāraṇa Ocean), they all became frightened and surprised.”

kotiśo yojanordhvaṁ vai
purāṇām aṣṭakaṁ gatāḥ
divya-prakara-ratnādi-
druma-vṛnda-manoharam 1.2.13

“Millions of yojanas higher they came to eight cities charming with glistening jewel-forest groves.”

tad-ūrdhvaṁ dadṛśur devā
virajāyās taṭaṁ śubham
taraṅgitaṁ kṣauma-śubhraṁ
sopānair bhāskaraṁ param 1.2.14

“Higher still the demigods saw the Virajā river’s splendid shore, which was splashed by waves white as silk and glorious with many staircases.”

taṁ dṛṣṭvā pracalantas te
tat puraṁ jagmur uttamam
asaṅkhya-koṭi-martaṇḍa-
jyotiṣāṁ maṇḍalaṁ mahat 1.2.15

“After seeing this, they entered a city splendid as countless millions of suns.”

dṛṣṭvā pratāditākṣās te
tejasā dharṣitāḥ sthitāḥ
namaskṛtvātha tat-tejo
dadhyau viṣṇv-ājñayā vidhiḥ 1.2.16

“As they looked at its splendor they became overpowered, their eyes struck by the light. By Lord Viṣṇu’s order, Brahmā offered obeisances to that light and meditated on it.”

taj-jyotir-maṇḍale ’paśyat
sākaraṁ dhāma śāntidam
tasmin mahādbhutaṁ dīrghaṁ
mṛṇāla-dhavalaṁ param
sahasra-vadanaṁ śeṣaṁ
dṛṣṭvā nemuḥ surās tataḥ 1.2.17

“In that circle of light he saw the form of a peaceful transcendental realm. When the demigods saw in that realm the very wonderful white lotus stem of thousand-faced Lord Śeṣa, they bowed down to offer respects to Him.”

tasyotsaṅge mahā-loko
goloko loka-vanditaḥ
yatra kālaḥ kalayatām
īśvaro dhāma-māninām
rājan na prabhaven māyā
manaś cittaṁ matir hy aham
na vikāro viśaty eva
na mahāṁś ca guṇāḥ kutaḥ 1.2.18-19

“On His head was the transcendental realm of Goloka, which is worshiped by all the worlds, and which is a realm where time, the conqueror of the proud, does not prevail, and where illusion, the material mind, heart, intelligence, and ego, material transformations and the mahat-tattva do enter. How can the modes of nature enter there?”

tatra kandarpa-lāvaṇyāḥ
śyāmasundara-vigrahāḥ
dvāri gantuṁ cābhyuditā
nyaṣedhan kṛṣṇa-pārṣadāḥ 1.2.20

“Their forms very beautiful, more beautiful than Kāmadeva himself, Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s associates stood at the entrance.”

śrī-devā ūcuḥ
loka-pālā vayaṁ sarve
brahma-viṣṇu-maheśvarāḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇa-darśanārthāya
śakrādyā āgatā iha 1.2.21

The demigods said: “We, Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, all the protectors of the planets, and the demigods headed by Indra, have come here to see Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tac chrutvā tad-abhiprāyaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇāya sakhī-janāḥ
ūcur deva-pratīhārāḥ
gatvā cāntaḥ-puraṁ param 1.2.22

Śrī Nārada said: “Having heard this, the doorkeepers, girls who were the Lord’s friends, went inside the palace and told Śrī Kṛṣṇa of (the visitors’) wish.”

tadā vinirgatā kācic
chatacandrānanā sakhī
pītāmbarā vetra-hastā
sāpṛcchad vāñhitaṁ surān 1.2.23

“Then a girl named Śatacandrānanā, who was dressed in yellow garments, who held a stick in her hand, and who was Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s friend, came from inside and asked the demigods what they wished.”

śrī-śatacandrānanovāca
kasyāṇḍasyādhipā devā
yūyaṁ sarve samāgatāḥ
vadatāśu gamiṣyāmi
tasmai bhagavate hy aham 1.2.24

Śrī Śatacandrānanā said: “All you demigods who have come here, of what universe are you the kings? Tell me and I shall go to the Lord at once.”

śrī-devā ūcuḥ
aho aṇḍāny utānyāni
nāsmābhir darśitāni ca
ekam aṇḍaṁ prajānīmo
’tho ’paraṁ nāsti naḥ śubhe 1.2.25

The demigods said: “We have not seen any other universes. We know only one universe. O beautiful one, there is no universe beyond ours.”

śrī-śatacandrānanovāca
brahmadeva luṭhantīha
koṭiśo hy aṇḍa-rāśayaḥ
teṣāṁ yūyam yathā devās
tathāṇḍe ’ṇḍe pṛthak pṛthak 1.2.26

Śrī Śatacandrānanā said: “O demigod Brahmā, millions of universes bob up and down (in the Kāraṇa ocean). As you are demigods in one universe, so in each each universe there are many demigods.”

nāma-grāmaṁ na jānītha
kadā vātra samāgatāḥ
jaḍa-buddhyā prahṛṣyadhve
gṛhān nāpi vinirgatāḥ 1.2.27

“You do not even know their names. When did you even meet them? Never leaving your own homes, you remain happy with stunted minds.”

brahmāṇḍam ekaṁ jānanti
yatra jātās tathā janāḥ
maṣakā ca yathāntaḥ-sthā
auḍumbara-phaleṣu vai 1.2.28

“You know one universe. Living entities are born in many universes, like mosquitoes in many uḍumbara fruits.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
upahāsyaṁ gatā devā
itthaṁ tūṣṇīṁ sthitāḥ punaḥ
cakitān iva tān dṛṣṭvā
viṣṇur vacanam abravīt 1.2.29

Śrī Nārada said: “Mocked in this way, the demigods were silent. Seeing them intimidated, Lord Viṣṇu spoke some words.”

śrī-viṣṇur uvāca
yasminn aṇḍe pṛṣṇigarbho
’vatāro ’bhūt sanātanaḥ
trivikrama-nakhodbhinne
tasminn aṇḍe sthitā vayam 1.2.30

Śrī Viṣṇu said: “We stay in the universe where eternal Lord Pṛśnigarbha descended, the universe broken by Lord Trivikrama’s toenail.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tac chrutvā taṁ ca saṁślāghya
śīghram antaḥ-puraṁ gatā
punar āgatya devebhyo
’py ājñām dattvā gatāḥ puram 1.2.31

Śrī Nārada said: “Hearing this, she praised Him for a long time, again entered the palace, returned, and gave the demigods permission (to enter. Then the demigods) entered the palace.”

atha deva-gaṇāḥ sarve
golokam dadṛśuḥ param
tatra govardhano nāma
giri-rājo virājate
vasanta-mālinībhiś ca
gopībhir go-gaṇair vṛtaḥ
kalpa-vṛkṣa-latā-saṅghai
rāsa-maṇḍala-maṇḍitaḥ
yatra kṛṣṇā nadī śyāmā
tolikā-koṭi-maṇḍitā
vaidurya-kṛta-sopānā
svacchanda-gatir uttamā 1.2.32-34

“Then the demigods saw Goloka, where the king of mountains, named Govardhana, which was filled with many cows and with gopīs garlanded with forest flowers, and which was decorated with rāsa-dance circles, is splendidly manifest, where the dark Yamunā river meandered as it wished, (its shores) made with lapis lazuli steps and decorated with millions of gazebos, . . .”

vṛndāvanaṁ bhrājamānaṁ
divya-druma-latākulam
citra-pakṣi-madhuvratair
vaṁśī-vaṭa-virājitam 1.2.35

“. . . where the forest of Vṛndāvana, filled with splendid trees and vines, and glorious with bees, colorful birds, and the banyan tree where (Kṛṣṇa) played the flute, was splendidly manifested, . . .”

puline śītalo vayur
manda-gāmī vahaty alam
sahasra-dala-padmānāṁ
rajo vikṣepayan muhuḥ 1.2.36

“. . . where, carrying the pollen of many thousand-petal lotuses, a cool breeze gently blew to the shore, . . .”

madhye nija-nikuñjo ’sti
dva-triṁśad-vana-samyutaḥ
prakara-parikhā-yuto
’ruṇākṣaya-vaṭājiraḥ 1.2.37

“. . . where in the middle is the Lord’s personal woodland of 32 forests, which is surrounded by walls and moats and endowed with a reddish courtyard of eternal banyan trees, . . .”

saptadhā padmarāgāgrā-
jira-kuḍya-vibhūṣitaḥ
koṭīndu-maṇḍalākārair
vitānair gulikā-dyutiḥ 1.2.38

“. . . a courtyard decorated with seven walls made of rubies and with a glory of pearls from awnings like many millions of moons, . . .”

patat-patākair divyābhaiḥ
puṣpa-mandira-vartmabhiḥ
jāta-bhramara-saṅgīto
matta-barhi-pika-svanaḥ 1.2.39

“. . . .where there were splendid flags moving (in the breeze), pathways that were flower palaces, and the singing of many bees and many maddened peacocks and cuckoos, . . .”

bālārka-kuṇḍala-dharāḥ
śata-candra-prabhāḥ striyaḥ
svacchanda-gatayo ratnaiḥ
paśyantyaḥ sundaraṁ mukham
ratnājireṣu dhāvantyo
hāra-keyūra-bhūṣitāḥ
kvāṇan-nūpura-kiṅkinyas
cūḍā-maṇi-virājitāḥ 1.2.40

“. . . where there were many women effulgent as a hundred moons, wearing sunrise earrings, walking about as they wished, noticing their beautiful faces reflected in the jewels, running in the jewel courtyard, decorated with necklaces, bracelets, and tinkling ankle-bells, and splendid with jewels in their hair, . . .”

koṭiśaḥ koṭiśo gāvo
dvāri dvāri manoharāḥ
śveta-parvata-saṅkāśā
divya-bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣitāḥ 1.2.42

“. . . where at door after door there were millions of millions of beautiful cows glorious as white mountains, decorated with glittering ornaments, . . .”

payasvinyas taruṇyaś ca
śīla-rūpa-guṇair yutaḥ
sa-vatsāḥ pīta-pucchāś ca
vrajantyo bhāvya-mūrtikāḥ 1.2.43

“. . . young, full of milk, endowed with good character, virtues, and beauty, their tails yellow, personifications of happiness as they walked about with their calves, . . .”

ghaṇṭa-mañjīra-samrāvāḥ
kiṅkinī-jāla-maṇḍitāḥ
hema-śṛṅgyo hema-tulya-
hāra-mālā-sphurat-prabhāḥ 1.2.44

“. . . decorated with a network of tiny bells, their bells and anklets tinkling, splendid with golden necklaces and flower garlands, their horns of gold, . . .”

pāṭalā haritās tamrāḥ
pītāḥ śyāmā vicitritāḥ
dhūmrāḥ kokila-varṇāś ca
yatra gāvas tv anekadhā 1.2.45

“. . . many different wonderful colors, some pink, some green, some red, some yellow, some black, some smokey, and some the color of cuckoos, . . .”

samudravad-dugdhadāś ca
taruṇī-kara-cihnikāḥ
kuraṅgavad-vilaṅghadbhir
govatsair maṇḍitāḥ śubhāḥ 1.2.46

“. . . giving oceans of milk, marked with the signs of youth, leaping like deer, beautiful, decorated with many calves, . . .”

itas tataś calantaś ca
go-ganeṣu mahā-vṛṣāḥ
dīrgha-kandhara-śṛṅgāḍhyā
yatra dharma-dhurandharāḥ 1.2.47

“. . . where, wandering among the cows were many great bulls opulent with long horns and necks and very pious, . . .”

gopālā vetra-hastāś ca
śyāmā vaṁśī-dharāḥ parāḥ
kṛṣṇa-līlāṁ pragāyanto
rāgair madana-mohanaiḥ 1.2.48

“. . . where were many handsome cowherd boys, carrying flutes, holding sticks in their hands, and with charming melodies singing of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes.”

itthaṁ nija-nikuñjaṁ taṁ
natvā madhye gatāḥ surāḥ
jyotiṣāṁ maṇḍalaṁ padmaṁ
sahasra-dala-śobhitam 1.2.49

“Bowing down to offer respects, the demigods entered the Lord’s personal forest grove, which was a thousand-petal lotus that was a great circle of light.”

tad-ūrdhvaṁ ṣoḍaśa-dalaṁ
tato ’ṣṭa-dala-paṅkajam
tasyopari sphurad dīrghaṁ
sopāna-traya-maṇḍitam 1.2.50

“Above that was a sixteen-petal lotus, above that an eight-petal lotus, and above that a long path decorated with three stairways.”

siṁhāsanaṁ paraṁ divyaṁ
kaustubhaiḥ khacitaṁ śubhaiḥ
dadṛśur devatāḥ sarvāḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇaṁ rādhayā yutam 1.2.51

“There all the demigods saw, sitting on a splendid throne studded with beautiful kaustubha jewels, and accompanied by Śrī Rādhā, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, . . .”

divyair aṣṭa-sakhī-saṅghair
mohiny-ādibhir anvitam
śrīdāmādyaiḥ sevyamānam
aṣṭa-gopāla-maṇḍalaiḥ 1.2.52

“. . . who was accompanied by eight splendid gopī friends headed by Mohinī and served by eight gopas headed by Śrīdāmā, . . .”

haṁsābhair vyajanāndola-
cāmarair vajra-muṣṭibhiḥ
koṭi-candra-pratīkāśaiḥ
sevitaṁ chatra-koṭibhiḥ 1.2.53

“. . . who was served with diamond-handled cāmaras glorious as swans and millions of parasols like millions of moons, . . .”

śrī-rādhikālaṅkṛta-vāma-bāhuṁ
svacchanda-vakrī-kṛta-dakṣiṇāṅghrim
vaṁśī-dharaṁ sundara-manda-hāsaṁ
bhrū-maṇḍalāmohita-kāma-rāśim 1.2.54

“. . . whose left arm was decorated by Śrī Rādhikā, whose right foot was bent as He wished, who held a flute, who smiled gently and gracefully, whose eyebrows enchanted hosts of Kāmadevas, . . .”

ghana-prabhaṁ padma-dalāyutekṣaṇaṁ
pralamba-bāhuṁ bahu-pīta-vāsasam
vṛndāvanonmatta-milinda-śabdair
virājitaṁ śrī-vana-mālayā harim 1.2.55

“. . . who was splendid as a raincloud, whose large eyes were lotus petals, whose arms were long, who wore great yellow garments, who was splendid with a beautiful forest garland filled with the sounds of intoxicated bees.”

kāñī-kalā-kaṅkana-nūpura-dyutiṁ
lasan-manohāri-mahojjvala-smitam
śrīvatsa-ratnottama-kuntala-śriyaṁ
kirīṭa-hārāṅgada-kuṇḍala-tviṣam 1.2.56

“. . . who was splendid with bracekets, anklets, and a belt, who had a glittering, charming, very glorious smile, who was handsome with the mark of Śrīvatsa and with a jewel placed in His curly locks of hair, and who was glorious with a crown, necklace, armlets, and earrings.”

Chapter 3 - Description of the Lord’s Appearance

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
mune deva mahātmānaṁ
kṛṣṇam dṛṣṭvā parāt param
agre kiṁ cakrire tatra
tan me brūhi kṛpāṁ kuru 1.3.1

“Śrī Bahulāśva said; O sage, O lord, what did they do when they saw the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is greater than the greatest? Please be kind and tell me.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
sarveṣaṁ paśyataṁ teṣāṁ
vaikuṇṭho ’pi haris tataḥ
utthāyāṣṭa-bhujaḥ sākṣāl
līno ’bhūt kṛṣṇa-vigrahe 1.3.2

Śrī Nārada said: “As everyone looked on, Lord Hari, the eight-armed master of Vaikuṇṭha, appeared and merged in the the body of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

tadaiva cāgataḥ pūrṇo
nṛsiṁhas cāṇḍa-vikramaḥ
koṭi-sūrya-pratīkāśo
līno ’bhūt kṛṣṇa-tejasi 1.3.3

“The perfect, ferocious, and powerful Lord Nrsiṁha, who was effulgent as millions of suns, merged in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence.”

rathe lakṣa-haye śubhre
sthitaś cāgatavāṁs tataḥ
śvetadvīpādhipo bhūmā
sahasra-bhuja-maṇḍitaḥ 1.3.4

“Then the master of Śvetadvīpa, who was decorated with a thousand arms, came in a splendid chariot drawn by a hundred thousand horses.”

śriyā yuktaḥ svāyudhāḍhyaḥ
pārṣadaiḥ pariṣevitaḥ
sampralīno babhūvāśu
so ’pi śrī-kṛṣṇa-vigrahe 1.3.5

“Opulent with many weapons, accompanied by the goddess of fortune, and served by many associates, He suddenly merged in the body of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”

tadaiva cāgataḥ sākṣād
rāmo rajīva-locanaḥ
dhanur-bāṇa-dharaḥ sītā-
śobhito bhrātṛbhir vṛtaḥ
daśa-koṭy-arka-saṅkāśe
cāmarair dolite rathe
asaṅkhya-vaṇarendrāḍhye
lakṣa-cakra-ghana-svane
lakṣa-dhvaje lakṣa-haye
śatakaumbhe sthitas tataḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇa-vigrahe pūrṇaḥ
sampralīno babhūva ha 1.3.6-8

“Then, holding a bow and arrows, glorious with Sītā, and accompanied by His brothers, lotus-eyed Lord Rāmacandra came in a golden chariot that was effulgent as a hundred million suns, (decorated) with swaying cāmaras, opulent with countless monkey-kings, rumbling with ten thousand wheels, (decorated with) ten thousand flags, and (pulled by) ten thousand horses, and merged in the body of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

tadaiva cāgataḥ sākṣād
yajño nārāyaṇo hariḥ
prasphurat-pralayāṭopa-
jvalad-agni-śikhopamaḥ
rathe jyotirmaye dṛśye
dakṣiṇāḍhyaḥ sureśvaraḥ
so ’pi līno babhūvāśu
śrī-kṛṣṇe śyāma-vigrahe 1.3.9-10

“Then Lord Yajña, who is known as Nārāyaṇa and Hari, who is glorious like a flame in the fire of cosmic devastation, who is the master of the demigods, and who is glorious with His consort Dakṣiṇa, came in a splendid and beautiful chariot and suiddenly merged in the dark form of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

tadā cāgatavān sākṣān
nara-nārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ
catur-bhujo viśālākṣo
muni-veṣo ghana-dyutiḥ
taḍit-koṭi-jaṭājutaḥ
prasphurad-dīpta-maṇḍalaḥ
munīndra-maṇḍalair divyair
maṇḍito ’khaṇḍita-vrataḥ
sarveṣāṁ paśyatāṁ teṣām
āścarya-manasā nṛpa
so ’pi līno babhūvāśu
śrī-kṛṣṇe śyāma-sundare 1.3.11-13

“Then Lord Nara-Nārāyaṇa, whose eyes were large, who had four arms, who was dressed as a sage, who was splendid like a dark cloud, whose matted hair was a lightning flash, who observed an unbroken vow (of celibacy), and who was surrounded by splendid multitudes of the kings of sages, suddenly merged in the handsome dark form of Lord Kṛṣṇa as everyone looked on, their hearts filled with wonder.”

paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ ca svayaṁ prabhum
jñātvā devaḥ stutiṁ cakruḥ
paraṁ vismayam āgataḥ 1.3.14

“Filled with wonder, and now understanding that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, the demigods offered many prayers.”

śrī-devā ūcuḥ
kṛṣṇāya pūrṇa-puruṣāya parāt parāya
yajñeśvarāya para-kāraṇa-kāraṇāya
rādhā-varāya paripūrṇatamāya sākṣād-
goloka-dhāma-dhiṣaṇāya namaḥ parasmai 1.3.15

The demigods said: “Obeisances to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, greater than the greatest, the master of sacrifices, the cause of causes, the lover of Rādhā, the most perfect, and the Lord who resides in Goloka.”

yogeśvaraḥ kila vadanti mahaḥ paraṁ tvāṁ
tatraiva sātvatatamāḥ kṛta-vigrahaṁ ca
asmābhir adya viditaṁ padayor dvayaṁ te
tasmai namo ’stu mahasāṁ pataye parasmai 1.3.16

“The masters of yoga say You are the Brahman effulgence, and the great devotees say You have a form. Today we understand Your feet. Obeisances to You, the master of the Brahman effulgence.”

vyaṅgyena vā na na hi lakṣaṇayā kadāpi
sphoṭena yac ca kavayo na viśanti mukhyāḥ
nirdeśya-bhāva-rahitaṁ prakṛteḥ paraṁ ca
tvāṁ brahma nirguṇam ajaṁ śaraṇaṁ vrajāmaḥ 1.3.17

“We take shelter of You, the unborn Supreme, who are free from the material modes, who are beyond the world of matter, and who cannot be described. The greatest philosophers cannot describe You, either directly, indirectly, or by the most oblique hint.”

tvāṁ brahma kecid avayanti pare ca kālaṁ
kecit prasāṅkhyam apare bhuvi karma-rūpam
pūrve ca yogam apare kila kartṛ-bhāvam
anyoktibhir na viditaṁ śaraṇaṁ gatāḥ smaḥ 1.3.18

“Some teach that You are the greatest, some that You are time, some that You are the sum total of everything, some that You are karma, some that You are yoga, and some that You are the first cause. We take shelter of You, who cannot be perfectly known by all their words.”

śreyas-karīṁ bhagavatas tava pāda-sevāṁ
hitvātha tīrtha-yajanādi tapaś caranti
jñānena ye ca viditā bahu-vighna-saṅghaiḥ
santāḍitāḥ kim u bhavanti na te kṛtārthāḥ 1.3.19

“O Lord, they who reject the auspicious service of Your feet, and instead visit holy places, perform sacrifices and austerities, and try to cultivate spiritual knowledge are beaten by a host of obstacles. How miserably will they fail.”

vijñāpyam adya kim u deva aśeṣa-sākṣī
yaḥ sarva-bhūta-hṛdayeṣu virājamānaḥ
devair namadbhir amalāśaya-mukta-dehais
tasmai namo bhagavate puruṣottamāya 1.3.20

“What request shall we place before You today? The pure-hearted devotees whose forms are not material offer respectful obeisances to You, the Supreme Personality of Godhead who, the witness of everything, stays in everyone’s heart.”

yo rādhikā-hṛdaya-sundara-candra-hāraḥ
śrī-gopikā-nayana-jīvana-mūla-hāraḥ
goloka-dhāma-dhiṣaṇa-dhvaja ādi-devaḥ
sa tvaṁ vipatsu vibudhān paripāhi pāhi 1.3.21

“O Lord, You are a necklace of moons Śrī Rādhā places over Her heart. You are the source of life for the gopīs’ eyes. You are a flag over Goloka. You are the original Supreme Personality of Godhead. O Lord, from all calamities please protect, please protect the demigods.”

vṛndāvaneśa girirāja-pate vrajeśa
gopāla-veṣa-kṛta-nitya-vihāra-līla
rādhā-pate śrutidharādhipate ’ddhara tvaṁ
govardhanoddharaṇa durdhara-dharma-dhārin 1.3.22

“O king of Vṛndāvana, O king of the mountains’ king, O king of Vraja, O king who enjoys cowherd-boy pastimes eternally, O king of Rādhā, O king of the great philosophers, O lifter of Govardhana Hill, O Lord who can do any impossible task, please save us!”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity ukto bhagavān sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo gokuleśvaraḥ
pratyāha praṇatān devān
megha-gambhīrayā girā 1.3.23

Śrī Nārada said: “Addressed in this way, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the king of Gokula, in a voice of thunder replied to the demigods prostrate before Him.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
he sura-jyeṣṭha he śambho
devāḥ śṛṇuta mad-vacaḥ
yādaveṣu ca janyadhvam
aṁśaiḥ strībhir mad-ājñayā 1.3.24

The Blessed Lord said: “O Śiva, O eldest of the demigods, O demigods, hear My words. By My order you and your wives should take birth, by your aṁśa expansions, in the family of the Yadus.”

ahaṁ cāvatariṣyāmi
hariṣyāmi bhuvo bharam
kariṣyāmi ca vaḥ kāryaṁ
bhaviṣyāmi yadoḥ kule 1.3.25

“I will also descend. I will remove the earth’s burden. I will perform the work you (request). I will take birth in the Yadus’ family.”

vedā me vacanaṁ viprā
mukhaṁ gāvas tanur mama
aṅgāni devatā yūyaṁ
sādhavo hy asavo hṛdi 1.3.26

“The Vedas are My words. The brāhmaṇas are My mouth. The cows are My body. You demigods are My limbs. My devotees are the life-breath in My heart.”

yuge yuge ca badhyeta
yadā pākhāṇḍibhir janaiḥ
dharmaḥ kratur dayā sakṣat
tad ātmānaṁ sṛjāmy aham 1.3.27

“Millennium after millennium, whenever the blasphemers stop religion, sacrifice, and mercy, I descend Myself.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktavantaṁ jagad-īśvaraṁ hariṁ
rādhā patim prāha viyoga-vihvalā
dāvāgninā dāva-lateva mūrchitā-
śru-kampa-romāñcita-bhāva-samvṛtā 1.3.28

Śrī Nārada said: “When Her husband, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the master of the universes, had spoken, Śrī Rādhā, overcome with fear of being separated from Him, and now like a flowering vine burning in a great forest fire, weeping, trembling, about to faint unconscious, and the hairs of her body erect, spoke to Him.”

śrī-rādhovāca
bhuvo bharaṁ hartum alaṁ bhuvaṁ vraja
kṛtaṁ paraṁ me śapathaṁ śṛṇuṣva tat
gate tvayi prāṇa-pate ca vigrahaṁ
kadācid atraiva na dhārayāmy aham 1.3.29

Śrī Rādhā said: “Go to the earth and remove its burden! Hear My great vow. O Lord of My life, when You are gone I will not keep my body (alive) in this place.”

yadātha me tvaṁ śapathaṁ na manyase
dvitīya-vāraṁ pravadāmi vāk-patham
prāṇaś ca me gantum atīva-vihvalaḥ
karpūra-dhūli-kaṇavad bhaviṣyati 1.3.30

“If You do not believe My words, I will say them a second time. (If You go) then My life, yearning to depart, will become like camphor dust.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
tvayā saha gamiṣyāmi
mā śokaṁ kuru rādhike
hariṣyāmi bhuvo bhāraṁ
kariṣyāmi vacas tava 1.3.31

The Blessed Lord said: “I will go with You. O Rādhā, don’t lament. I will remove the earth’s burden and I will keep Your word.”

śrī-rādhikovāca
yatra vṛndāvanaṁ nāsti
yatra no yamunā nadī
yatra govardhano nāsti
tatra me na manaḥ-sukham 1.3.32

Śrī Rādhā said: “My heart cannot be happy where there is no Vṛndāvana forest, no Yamunā river, and no Govardhana Hill.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
veda-nāga-krośa-bhūmiṁ
sva-dhamnaḥ śrī-hariḥ svayam
govardhanaṁ ca yamunāṁ
preṣayām āsa bhūpari 1.3.33

Śrī Nārada said: “Then Lord Kṛṣṇa sent to the earth Govardhana Hill, the Yamunā river, and a 47 krośa portion of His abode. Note: One krośa equals two miles.”

tadā brahmā deva-gaṇair
natvā natvā punaḥ punaḥ
paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ samuvāca ha 1.3.34

“Bowing and bowing again and again with the demigods, Brahmā addressed the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”

śrī-brahmovāca
ahaṁ kutra bhaviṣyāmi
kutra tvaṁ ca bhaviṣyasi
ete kutra bhaviṣyanti
kair gṛhaiḥ kaiś ca nāmabhiḥ 1.3.35

Śrī Brahmā said: “Where will I take birth? Where will You take birth? Where will the (demigods) take birth? In what homes? With what names?”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
vasudevasya devakyāṁ
bhaviṣyāmi paraḥ svayam
rohiṇyāṁ mat-kalā śeṣo
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 1.3.36

The Blessed Lord said: “I will take birth in Devakī from Vasudeva. My transcendental kalā expansion Śeṣa will take birth in Rohiṇī. Of this there is no doubt.”

śrīḥ sākṣād rukmiṇī bhaiṣmī
śivā jāmbavatī tathā
satyā ca tulasī bhūmau
satyabhāmā vasundharā 1.3.37

“The goddess of fortune will take birth as Bhīṣmaka’s daughter Rukmiṇī. Lord Śiva’s consort will take birth as Jāmbavatī. Tulasī will take birth as Satyā and Vasundharā as Satyabhāmā.”

dakṣiṇā lakṣmaṇā caiva
kālindī virajā tathā
bhadrā hrīr mitravindā ca
jāhnavī pāpa-nāśinī 1.3.38

“Dakṣiṇā will become Lakṣmaṇā, Virajā will become Kālindī, Hrī will become Bhadrā, and Jhnavī, who destroys sins, will become Mitravindā.”

rukmiṇyāṁ kāmadevaś ca
pradyumna iti viśrutaḥ
bhaviṣyati na sandehas
tasya tvaṁ ca bhaviṣyasi 1.3.39

“(Born) in Rukmiṇī, Kāmadeva will be known as Pradyumna. You will take birth as his son. Of this there is no doubt.”

nando droṇo vasuḥ sākṣād
yaśodā sā dharā smṛtā
vṛṣabhānuḥ sucandraś ca
tasya bharyā kalāvatī 1.3.40

“The Vasu Droṇa will become Nanda, and Dharā will be remembered as Yaśodā. Sucandra will take birth as Vṛṣabhānu, and his wife Kalāvatī will also take birth.”

Chapter 4 - Description of Questions About the Lord’s Appearance

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
nandopananda-bhavane
śrīdāmā subalaḥ sakhā
stoka-kṛṣṇo ’rjuno ’ṁśuś ca
nava-nanda-gṛhe vidhe 1.4.1

The Blessed Lord said: “My friends Śrīdāmā and Subala will take birth in the homes of Nanda and Upananda. Stoka-kṛṣṇa, Arjuna, and Aṁśu will take birth in the nine homes of Nanda’s relatives.”

viśālaṛṣabha-tejasvi-
devaprastha-varūthapāḥ
bhaviṣyanti sakhāyo me
vraje sad vṛṣabhānuṣu 1.4.2

“My friends Viśāla, Ṛṣabha, Tejasvī, Devaprastha, and Varūthapa will take birth among the six relatives of King Vṛṣabhānu.”

śrī-brahmovāca
kasya vai nanda-padavī
kasya vai vṛṣabhānutā
vada deva-pate sākṣād
upanandasya lakṣaṇam 1.4.3

Śrī Brahmā said: “Who is Nanda? Who is Vṛṣabhānu? O master of the demigods, please describe the nature of Upananda.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
gāḥ pālayanti ghoṣeṣu
sadā go-vṛttayo ’niśam
te gopālā mayā proktās
teṣāṁ tvaṁ lakṣaṇaṁ śṛṇu 1.4.4

The Blessed Lord said: “They are cowherd people. Day and night they protect the cows in the villages of cowherds. Listen and I will describe them.”

nandaḥ proktaḥ sa-gopālair
nava-lakṣa-gavāṁ patiḥ
upanandaś ca kathitaḥ
pañca-lakṣa-gavāṁ patiḥ 1.4.5

“The gopas say that Nanda is the master of 900,000 cows and Upananda is the master of 500,000 cows.”

vṛṣabhānuḥ sa ukto yo
daśa-lakṣa-gavāṁ patiḥ
gavāṁ koṭir gṛhe yasya
nanda-rājaḥ sa eva hi 1.4.6

“They say that Vṛṣabhānu is the master of a million cows and at King Nanda’s home are ten million cows.”

koṭy-ardhaṁ ca gavāṁ yasya
vṛṣabhānu-varas tu saḥ
etādṛśau vraje dvau tu
sucandro droṇa eva hi 1.4.7

“Vṛṣabhānuvara has five million cows, and Sucandra and Droṇa in Vraja each have that number.”

sarva-lakṣaṇa-lakṣyāḍhyau
gopa-rājau bhaviṣyataḥ
śata-candrānanānāṁ ca
sundarīṇāṁ su-vāsasām
gopīnāṁ mad-vraje ramye
śata-yūtho bhaviṣyati 1.4.8

“These two gopa kings will be rich with all virtues. In My Vraja will also be hundreds of groups of beautiful, beautifully dressed gopīs, their faces like hundreds of moons.”

śrī-brahmovāca
he dīna-bandho he deva
jagat-kāraṇa-kāraṇa
yūthasya lakṣaṇaṁ sarvaṁ
tan me brūhi pareśvara 1.4.9

Śrī Brahmā said: “O friend of the poor, O Lord, O cause of the universes’ causes, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, please completely describe these groups (of gopīs).”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
arbudaṁ daśa-koṭīnāṁ
munibhiḥ kathitaṁ vidhe
daśārbudaṁ yatra bhavet
so ’pi yūthaḥ prakathyate 1.4.10

The Blessed Lord said: “O Brahmā, the sages say that the number one hundred million is called one arbuda. In each group there are ten arbudas (one million).”

goloka-vāsinyaḥ kāścit
kāścid vai dvāra-pālikāḥ
śṛṅgāra-prakarā kāścit
kāścit kāścic chayyopakārakāḥ 1.4.11

“Some of the gopīs who live in Gokula are doorkeepers, some decorate (the divine couple), and some prepare the (divine couple’s) bed.”

pārṣadākhyās tathā kāścic
chrī-vṛndāvana-pālikāḥ
govardhana-nivāsinyaḥ
kāścit kuñja-vidhāyikāḥ 1.4.12

“Some of these gopī associates of the Lord protect the forest of Vṛndāvana, some reside by Govardhana Hill, and some decorate the forest groves.”

me nikuñja-nivāsinyo
bhaviṣyanti vraje mama
evaṁ ca yamunā-yūtho
jāhnavī-yūtha eva ca 1.4.13

“Some will reside in the forests of My Vraja. Some will reside by the Yamunā and some by the Ganges.”

ramāyā madhu-mādhavyā
virajāyās tathaiva ca
lalitāyā viśākhāyā
māyā-yūtho bhaviṣyati 1.4.14

“Ramā, Madhu-Mādhavī, Virajā, Lalitā, and Viśākhā will each lead a group.”

eta hy aṣṭa-sakhīnāṁ3 ca
sakhīnāṁ kila ṣoḍaśa
dvatriṁśac ca sakhīnāṁ tu
yūthā bhāvyā vraje vidhe 1.4.15

“O Brahma, in Vraja there will be groups led by eight gopī friends, by sixteen gopī friends, and by thirty-two gopī friends.”

śruti-rūpā ṛṣi-rūpā
maithilāḥ kauśalās tathā
ayodhyā-pura-vāsinyo
yajña-sītāḥ pulindakāḥ 1.4.16

“Some of the gopīs to be had been Personified Vedas, some great sages, some women in Mithilā, some women in Koṣalā, some women in Ayodhyā, some yajñ-sītās, and some Pulindā girls.”

yāsāṁ mayā varo datto
pūrve pūrve yuge yuge
tāsāṁ yūthā bhaviṣyanti
gopīnāṁ mad-vraje śubhe 1.4.17

“In previous millennia having received a benediction from Me, they will become various groups of gopīs in My beautiful Vraja.”

śrī-brahmovāca
etāḥ kathaṁ vraje bhāvyāḥ
kena pūṇyena kair varaiḥ
durlabhaṁ hi padaṁ tad vai
yogibhiḥ puruṣottama 1.4.18

Śrī Brahmā said: “How will they become (gopīs) in Vraja? O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of what pious deed or what benedictions will they attain this position even the great yogīs cannot attain?”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
śvetadvīpe ca bhūmānaṁ
śrutayas tuṣṭuvuḥ param
uṣatībhir girābhiś ca
prasanno ’bhūt sahasra-pāt 1.4.19

The Blessed Lord said: “When in Śvetadvīpa the personififed Vedas offered prayers to Him with eloquent words, the Lord’s form who has thousands of feet became pleased.”

śrī-harir uvāca
varaṁ vṛṇīta yūyaṁ vai
yan mano-vāñchitaṁ mahat
yeṣāṁ prasanno ’haṁ sākṣāt
teṣāṁ kiṁ durlabhaṁ bhuvi 1.4.20

Śrī Hari said: “Choose a benediction, whatever your heart desires. What is difficult to attain for they with whom I am pleased?”

śrī-śrutaya ūcuḥ
vāṅ-mano-gocarātītaṁ
tato na jñāyate tu tat
ānanda-mātram iti yad
vandantīha pura-vidaḥ
tad rūpaṁ darśayāsmākaṁ
yadi deyo varo hi naḥ 1.4.21

The personified Vedas said: “Because You are beyond the material mind and material words, we cannot perfectly understand You. If You wish to give us a benediction, then please directly show us Your transcendental form, which the scholars of the Purāṇas say is full of bliss.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
śrutvaitad darśayām āsa
svaṁ lokaṁ prakṛteḥ param
kevalānubhavānanda-
mātram akṣaram avyayam 1.4.22

The Blessed Lord said: “Hearing this, the Lord showed them His own abode, where there is only bliss and there are neither decay nor death, . . .”

yatra vṛndāvanaṁ nāma
vanaṁ kāma-dughair drumaiḥ
manorama-nikuñjāḍhyaṁ
sarvartu-sukha-samyutam 1.4.23

“. . . where there is the forest named Vṛndāvana, which is opulent with beautiful forests of trees that fulfill all desires, and which brings happiness in every season, . . .”

yatra govardhano nāma
su-nirjhara-darī-yutaḥ
ratna-dhātu-mayaḥ śrīmān
su-pakṣi-gaṇa-saṅkulaḥ 1.4.24

“. . . where there is the hill named Govardhana, which has beautiful caves and swiftly-moving streams, which is made of jewels and precious minerals, and which is filled with graceful birds, . . .”

yatra nirmala-pānīyā
kālindī saritāṁ varā
ratna-baddhobhaya-taṭī
haṁsa-padmādi-saṅkulā 1.4.25

“. . . where there is the Yamunā, which is the best of rivers, which has pure and delicious water, which is filled with lotuses, swans, and other flowers and birds, and which has shores made of precious jewels, . . .”

nānā-rāsa-rasonmattaṁ
yatra gopī-kadambakam
tat-kadambaka-madhya-sthaḥ
kiśorākṛtir acyutaḥ 1.4.26

“. . . and where the infallible Lord, whose form is that of a youth, stood among many gopīs intoxicated by tasting the nectar of many rāsa dances.”

darśayitveti ca prāha
bruta kiṁ karavāṇi vaḥ
dṛṭṭo madīyo loko ’yam
yato nāsti paraṁ varam 1.4.27

Having revealed this, the Lord said: “What more shall I do for you? Tell Me. You have seen My realm. There is no benediction better than (seeing it).”

śrī-śrutaya ūcuḥ
kandarpa-koṭi-lāvaṇye
tvayi dṛṭṭe manāṁsi naḥ
kāminī-bhāvam āsadya
smara-kṣubdhāny asaṁśayam 1.4.28

“When we see You in this way, more handsome than millions of Kāmadevas, our hearts become troubled with amorous desires and we yearn to become beautiful girls. Of this there is no doubt.”

yathā goloka-vāsinyaḥ
kāminītvena gopikāḥ
bhajanti ramaṇaṁ matvā
cikīrṣājani nas tathā 1.4.29

“We yearn to become beautiful gopīs in Goloka who worship You as their lover.”

śrī-harir uvāca
durlabho durghaṭaś caiva
yuṣmākaṁ su-manorathaḥ
mayānumoditāḥ samyak
satyo bhavitum arhati 1.4.30

Śrī Hari said: “Although your glorious desire is impossible and can never be attained, I will grant it. It will become true.”

āgamini viriñcau tu
jāte sṛṭṭy-artham udyate
kalpa-sārasvataṁ prāpya
vraje gopyo bhaviṣyatha 1.4.31

“When a new Brahmā is born to create (the universe), and the time of Sārasvata-kalpa arrives, you will become gopīs in Vraja.”

pṛthivyāṁ bhārate kṣetre
māthure mama maṇḍale
vṛndāvane bhaviṣyāmi
preyān vo rasa-maṇḍale 1.4.32

“On the earth, in Bhārata-varṣa, in the circle of Mathurā, in Vṛndāvana, in the rāsa-dance circle, I will become your beloved.”

jāra-dharmeṇa su-snehaṁ
su-dṛḍhaṁ sarvato ’dhikam
mayi samprāpya sarvā hi
kṛtākṛtyā bhaviṣyatha 1.4.33

“Passionately loving Me as your paramour, you will all become perfect.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
tāś ca gopyo bhaviṣyanti
pūrva-kalpa-varān mama
anyāsāṁ caiva gopīnāṁ
lakṣaṇaṁ śṛṇu tad vidhe 1.4.34

The Blessed Lord said: “Because of the previous kalpa’s benediction they will become gopīs. O Brahmā, now hear the description of the other gopīs.”

surāṇāṁ rakṣaṇārthāya
rākṣasānāṁ vadhāya ca
tretāyāṁ rāmacandro ’bhūd
vīro daśarathātmajaḥ 1.4.35

“To protect the devotees and kill the demons the Lord appeared in Tretā-yuga as Daśaratha’s son, the hero Rāmacandra.”

sītā-svayamvaraṁ gatvā
dhanur-bhaṅgaṁ cakāra saḥ
uvāha jānakīṁ sītāṁ
rāmo rājīva-locanaḥ 1.4.36

“Lotus-eyed Rāma went to Sītā’s svayamvara, broke the bow, and married Janaka’s daughter Sītā.”

taṁ dṛṭṭvā maithilāḥ sarvāḥ
purandhryo mumuhur vidhe
rahasy ūcur mahātmānaṁ
bhartā no bhava he ragho 1.4.37

“O Brahmā, when they saw Him, all the married women of Mithilā were overcome with desire. In a secluded place they all approached the Lord and said, “O descendent of Raghu, please become our husband.”

tā āha rāghavendraś ca
mā śocaṁ kuruta striyaḥ
dvāparānte kariṣyāmi
bhavatīnāṁ manoratham 1.4.38

“Rāma, the king of the Rāghavas, said to them, “O women, don’t be unhappy. At the end of Dvāpara-yuga and I will fulfill your desire.”

tīrthaṁ dānaṁ tapo śaucaṁ
samācarata tattvataḥ
śraddhayā parayā bhaktyā
vraje gopyo bhaviṣyatha 1.4.39

“With great faith and devotion honestly go on pilgrimages, give charity, perform austerities, and follow the rules of cleanliness. Then you will become gopīs in Vraja.”

iti tābhyo varaṁ datvā
śrī-rāmaḥ karuṇā-nidhiḥ
koṣalān prayayau dhanvī
tejasā jita-bhārgavaḥ 1.4.40

“After giving them this benediction, Śrī Rāma, who is an ocean of mercy and an expert bowman, with His great prowess defeated Paraśurāma and then went to the country of the Koṣalas.”

mārge ca kauṣalā naryo
rāmaṁ dṛṭṭvāti-sundaram
manasā vavrire taṁ vai
patiṁ kandarpa-mohanam 1.4.41

“Seeing on the path very handsome Lord Rāma, who is more charming than a host of Kāmadevas, the women of Koṣala in their hearts chose Him to be their husband.”

manasāpi varaṁ rāmo
dadau tābhyo hy aśeṣa-vit
manorathaṁ kariṣyāmi
vraje gopyo bhaviṣyatha 1.4.42

“In His heart all-knowing Rāma gave them this benediction: “You will become gopīs in Vraja, and then I will fulfill your desire.”

āgataṁ sītayā sārdhaṁ
sainyena raghu-nandanam
ayodhyā-pura-vāsinyaḥ
śrutvā draṣṭuṁ samāyayuḥ 1.4.43

“When He came with Sītā and with His army, the women of Ayodhyā went to see the joy of the Raghus.”

vīkṣya taṁ moham āpannā
mūrchitāḥ prema-vihvalāḥ
tepus tapas tāḥ sārāyū-
tīre rāma-dhṛta-vratāḥ 1.4.44

“Seeing Him, they become bewildered and fainted, overcome with love. With a vow to attain Lord Rāma they performed austerities on the Sarāyū’s bank.”

ākāśa-vāg abhūt tāsāṁ
dvāparānte manorathaḥ
bhaviṣyati na sandehaḥ
kālindī-tīra-je vane 1.4.45

“A voice from the sky (said) to them, “At the end of Dvāpara-yuga, in the forest by the Yamunā’s shore, (your) desire will be fulfilled. Of this there is no doubt.”

pitur vākyād yadā rāmo
daṇḍakākhyaṁ vanaṁ gataḥ
cacāra sītayā sārdhaṁ
lakṣmaṇena dhanuṣmatā 1.4.46

“Then, because of His father’s words, He went with Sītā and the expert bowman Lakṣmaṇa to the Daṇḍaka forest.”

gopālopāsakāḥ sarve
daṇḍakāraṇya-vāsinaḥ
dhyāyantaḥ satataṁ māṁ vai
rāsārthaṁ dhyāna-tat-parāḥ 1.4.47

“All the sages in the Daṇḍaka forest were worshipers of Lord Gopāla. To attain the rāsa dance they always meditated on Me.”

yeṣām āśramam āsadya
dhanur-bāṇa-dharo yuvā
teṣāṁ dhyāne gato rāmo
jaṭā-mukuṭa-maṇḍitaḥ 1.4.48

“He came to their āśrama as a youth carrying bow and arrows. Then He appeared in their meditation as a sage with matted hair.”

anyākṛtiṁ te taṁ vīkṣya
paraṁ vismita-mānasāḥ
dhyānād utthāya dadṛśuḥ
koṭi-kandarpa-sannibham 1.4.49

“Surprised at heart by seeing this other form, they rose from their meditation and saw Lord Rāma, who was like millions of Kāmadevas.”

ūcus te ’yaṁ nu gopālo
vaṁśī-vetre vinā prabhuḥ
itthaṁ vicārya manasā
nemuś cakruḥ stutiṁ parām 1.4.50

“They said, “He is Lord Gopāla, who has come without His flute and stick.” Thinking in this way in their hearts, they bowed down and offered eloquent prayers.”

varaṁ vṛṇīta munayaḥ
śrī-rāmas tān uvāca ha
yathā sītā tathā sarve
bhūyaḥ sma iti vādinaḥ 1.4.51

“Śrī Rāma said to them, “O sages, please choose a benediction.” They said, “As Sītā is, so let us all become.”

śrī-rāma uvāca
yathā hi lakṣmaṇo bhrātā
tathā prārthyo varo yadi
adyaiva sa-phalo bhāvyo
bhavadbhir mat-prasaṅgataḥ 1.4.52

Śrī Rāma said: “If you had asked this of My brother Lakṣmaṇa, I would arrange that today your wish would bear its fruit.”

sītā yatheti vākyena
durghaṭo durlabho varaḥ
eka-patnī-vrato ’haṁ vai
maryādā-puruṣottamaḥ 1.4.53

“This request, to become “as Sītā is” is impossible and can never be granted. I have vowed to accept only one wife, and I am the best of they who follow the rules of religion.”

tasmāt tu mad-vareṇāpi
dvāparānte bhaviṣyatha
manorathaṁ kariṣyāmi
bhavatāṁ vāñchitaṁ param 1.4.54

“For this reason, by My benediciton, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga you will become (as She). Then I will fulfill your wish.”

iti datvā varaṁ rāmas
tataḥ pañcavatīṁ gataḥ
parṇa-śālaṁ samāsādya
vana-vāsaṁ cakāra ha 1.4.55

“After giving this benediction, Rāma went to Pa 24cavatī. He went to a leaf-hut, and there He lived in the forest.”

tad-darśana-smara-rujaḥ
pulindyaḥ prema-vihvalāḥ
śrīmat-pāda-rajo dhṛtvā
prānāṁs tyaktuṁ samudyatāḥ 1.4.56

“When they saw Him, some aborigine girls became tortured with lust. Overcome with love, they grasped the dust of His feet, intent on then giving up their lives.”

brahmacāri-vapur bhūtvā
rāmas tatra samāgataḥ
uvāca prāṇa-santyāgaṁ
mā kūryata striyo vṛthā 1.4.57

“Assuming the form of a brahmacārī, Rāma went to them and said, “O women, do not give up your lives without good reason.”

vṛndāvane dvāparānte
bhavitā vo manorathaḥ
ity uktvā brahmacārī tu
tatraivāntarādhīyata 1.4.58

““In Vṛndāvana, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga, your desire will be fulfilled.” Saying this, the brahmacārī disappeared.”

atha rāmo vānarendrai
rāvaṇādīn niśācarān
jitvā laṅkām etya sītā-
puṣpakena puraṁ yayau 1.4.59

“Then, aided by the monkey-kings, Rāma defeated the nocturnal demons headed by Rāvaṇa, entered Laṅkā and, on a flower-chariot carrying Sītā, returned to His own city.”

sītāṁ tatyāja rājendro
vane lokāpavādataḥ
aho satām api bhuvi
bhavanaṁ bhūri-duḥkha-dam 1.4.60

“Then, because of the criticism of the ordinary people, Rāma, the king of kings, abandoned Sītā in the forest. Ah, this brought great pain to the saintly devotees in the world.”

yadā yadākarod yajñaṁ
rāmo rajīva-locanaḥ
tadā tadā svarṇamayīṁ
sītāṁ kṛtvā vidhānataḥ 1.4.61

“Whenever he performed a yajña, lotus-eyed Rāma, following the rules of religion, had made a golden Deity of Sītā.”

yajña-sītā-samūho ’bhūn
mandire rāghavasya ca
tāś caitanya-ghanā bhūtvā
rantuṁ rāmaṁ samāgataḥ 1.4.62

“In Rāma’s palace there were many of these yajña Deities of Sītā. (One day) they came to life and approached Rāma to enjoy (with Him).”

tā āha raghu-vaṁśendro
nāhaṁ gṛhnāmi he priyāḥ
tadocus tāḥ prema-parā
rāmaṁ daśarathātmajam 1.4.63

“To them Rāma, the king of the Raghu dynasty, said, O beloved wives, I cannot accept you”. Then, filled with love, they said to Rāma, the son of Daśaratha, . . .”

kathaṁ cāsmān na gṛhnāsi
bhajantīr maithilīḥ satīḥ
ardhāṅgī yajña-kāleṣu
satataṁ kārya-sādhinīḥ 1.4.64

“. . . “Why do You not accept us? We are all Sītā, the chaste daughter of the king of Mithilā. We are half of Your body. At the time of the yajñas we were Your assistants.”

dharmiṣṭhas tvaṁ śruti-dharo
’dharmavad bhāṣase katham
karaṁ gṛhitvā tyajasi
tataḥ pāpam avāpsyasi 1.4.65

“You are a pious person. You know the teaching of the Vedas. Why do You speak as one who is impious? First You take our hand and then You reject us. From this You will incur sin.”

śrī-rāma uvāca
samīcīnaṁ vacaḥ satyaṁ
yuṣmābhir gaditaṁ ca me
eka-patnī-vrato ’haṁ hi
rājarṣiḥ sītayaikayā 1.4.66

Śrī Rāma said: “Your words are right. What you say to Me is true. (Still,) I am religious king, and I have vowed to accept only Sītā as My wife.”

tasmād yūyaṁ dvāparānte
puṇye vṛndāvane vane
bhaviṣyathā kariṣyāmi
yuṣmākaṁ tu manoratham 1.4.67

“Therefore at the end of Dvāpara-yuga you will take birth in the spiritual land of Vṛndāvana, and there I will fulfill your desire.”

Chapter 5 - The Lord’s Appearance

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
ramā-vaikuṇṭha-vāsinyaḥ
śvetadvīpa-sakhī-janāḥ
ūrdhva-vaikuṇṭha-vāsinyas
tathājita-padāśrayāḥ
śrī-lokācala-vāsinyaḥ
śrī-sakhyo ’pi samudra-jāḥ
tā gopyo ’pi bhaviṣyanti
lakṣmī-pati-varād vraje 1.5.1-2

The Blessed Lord said: “The women of Ramā-Vaikuṇṭha, the women of Śvetadvīpa, the women of Urdhva-Vaikuṇṭha, the women who have taken shelter of Lord Ajita’s feet, the women of Lokācala, and the daughters of Varuṇa will become gopīs in Vraja because of a benediction offered by Lord Nārāyaṇa, the husband of Lakṣmī.”

kāścid divyā adivyāś ca
tathā tri-guṇa-vṛttayaḥ
bhūmi-gopyo bhaviṣyanti
puṇyair nānā-vidhaiḥ kṛtaiḥ 1.5.3

“Some women from the heavenly planets, some women not from the heavenly planets, and some women acting in the three modes will, because of various kinds of pious activities, become gopīs on the earth.”

yajñāvatāraṁ ruciraṁ
ruci-putraṁ divas-patim
mohitāḥ pratibhāvena
vīkṣya deva-jana-striyaḥ 1.5.4

“When the saw handsome Yajña-avatāra, who is the son of Ruci and the master of the heavenly planets, the demigoddesses were overcome with love for Him.”

tāś ca devala-vākyena
tapas tepur himācale
bhaktyā paramayā tā me
gopyo bhāvyā vraje vidhe 1.5.5

“On Devala Muni’s advice with great devotion to Me they performed austerities in the Himalayas. O Brahmā, they will become gopīs in Vraja.”

antarhite bhagavati
deve dhanvantarau bhuvi
auṣadhyo duḥkham āpannā
niṣphalā bhārate ’bhavan 1.5.6

“When Lord Dhanvantari disappeared from the earth, all the medicines in Bhārata-varṣa became unhappy. They all became useless and ineffective.”

siddhy-arthaṁ tās tapas tepuḥ
striyo bhūtvā manoharāḥ
catur-yuge vyatīte tu
prasanno ’bhūd dhariḥ param 1.5.7

“To regain their powers they transformed themselves into beautiful women and performed austerities. When four yugas had passed Lord Hari became pleased with them.”

varaṁ vṛṇīta cety uktam
śrutvā nāryo mahā-vane
taṁ dṛṭṭvā moham āpannā
ūcur bhārtā bhavātra naḥ 1.5.8

“Seeing the Lord in a great forest, they became completely enchanted by Him. Hearing Him say, “Please choose a benediction,” they said, “Please become our husband here”.”

śrī-harir uvāca
vṛndāvane dvāparānte
latā bhūtvā manoharāḥ
bhaviṣyatha striyo rāse
kariṣyāmi vacaś ca vaḥ 1.5.9

Śrī Hari said: “In Vṛndāvana, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga, you will become flowering vines and then you will change into women in the arena of the rāsa dance. I promise this to you.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
bhakti-bhāva-samāyuktā
bhūri-bhāgyā varāṅganāḥ
latā-gopyo bhaviṣyanti
vṛndāraṇye pitāmaha 1.5.10

The Blessed Lord said: “In Vṛndāvana they will become beautiful and very fortunate flowering-vine gopīs filled with love (for Me).”

jālandharyaś ca yā nāryo
vīkṣya vṛndā-patiṁ harim
ūcur vayaṁ hariḥ sākṣād
asmākaṁ tu varo bhavet 1.5.11

“When the women of the Jālandhara country saw Lord Hari, the husband of Vṛndā, they said (to themselves), “May Lord Hari be our chosen husband”.”

ākāśa-vāg abhūt tāsāṁ
bhajatāśu ramā-patim
yathā vṛndā tathā yūyaṁ
vṛndāraṇye bhaviṣyatha 1.5.12

“Then a voice from the sky said to them, “Worship Lord Nārāyaṇa, the husband of Ramā, and in Vṛndāvana you will become like Vṛndā.”

samudra-kanyāḥ śrī-matsyaṁ
hariṁ dṛṭṭvā ca mohitāḥ
tā hi gopyo bhaviṣyanti
śrī-matsyasya varād vraje 1.5.14

“When Varuṇa’s daughters saw Lord Hari as Śrī Matsya, they became bewildered with love for Him. By Lord Matsya’s benediction they will become gopīs in Vraja.”

āsīd rājā pṛthuḥ sākṣān
mamāṁśaś caṇḍa-vikramaḥ
jitvā śatrūn nṛpa-śreṣṭho
dharāṁ kāmān dudoha saḥ 1.5.14

“There was a powerful king named Pṛthu who was a partial incarnation of Me. After defeating His enemies, He, the best of kings, milked many desirable things from the earth.”

barhiṣmati-bhavās tatra
pṛthuṁ dṛṭṭvā pura-striyaḥ
atreḥ samīpam āgatya
tā ūcur moha-vihvalāḥ 1.5.15

“When the women of Barhiṣmati City saw Lord Pṛthu they became overwhelmed with love for Him.” Approaching Atri Muni, they said:

ayaṁ tu rāja-rājendraḥ
pṛthuḥ pṛthula-vikramaḥ
kathaṁ varo bhaven no vai
tad vada tvaṁ mahā-mune 1.5.16

“O great sage, please tell us how powerful Pṛthu, the king of the kings of kings, may become our husband.”

śrī-atrir uvāca
go-dohaṁ kurutāśv adya
pṛthvīyaṁ dhāraṇāmayī
sarvaṁ dāsyati vo durgaṁ
manoratha-mahārṇavam 1.5.17

Śrī Atri Muni said: “In meditation make an offering of milk to her and the earth will give you a great and impassable ocean of fulfilled desires.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
manorathaṁ praduduhur
manaḥ-pātreṇa tāś ca gām
tasmād gopyo bhaviṣyanti
vṛndāraṇye pitāmaha 1.5.18

The Blessed Lord said: “Using their thoughts as a milk-pail, they milked their desire from the cow-earth. In this way they will become gopīs in Vṛndāvana, O Brahmā.”

kāma-senā-mohanārthaṁ
divyā apsaraso varāḥ
nārāyaṇasya sahasā
babhuvur gandha-mādane 1.5.19

“To bewilder Kāmadeva’s army, on Mount Gandhamādana Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi manifested a host of the most beautiful divine Apsarā girls.”

bhārtṛ-kāmāś ca tā āha
siddho nārāyaṇo muniḥ
manoratho vo bhavitā
vraje gopyo bhaviṣyatha 1.5.20

“When they wished Him as their husband, perfect Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi said to them, “You will become gopīs in Vraja. Then your desire will be fulfilled.”

striyaḥ sutala-vāsinyo
vāmanaṁ vīkṣya mohitāḥ
tapas taptvā bhaviṣyanti
gopyo vṛndāvane vidhe 1.5.21

“Brahmā. When the women of Sutalaloka saw Lord Vāmana, they fell in love with Him and (to attain Him) they performed austerities. They will become gopī in Vraja, O Brahmā.”

nāgendra-kanyakāḥ śeṣaṁ
bhejur bhaktyā varecchayā
saṅkarṣaṇasya rāsārthaṁ
bhaviṣyanti vraje ca tāḥ 1.5.22

“Snake princesses that, desiring Him as their husband, worshiped Lord Śeṣa, will take birth in Vraja (to attain) Lord Balarāma’s rāsa dance.”

kaśyapo vasudevaś ca
devakī cāditiḥ parā
śūraḥ prāṇo dhruvaḥ so ’pi
devako ’vatariṣyati 1.5.23

“Kaśyapa will descend as Vasudeva, exalted Aditi as Devakī, Prāṇa Vasu as Śūrasena, and Dhruva Vasu as Devaka.”

vasuś caivoddhavaḥ sākṣād
dakṣo ’krūro dayā-paraḥ
hṛdīko dhanadaś caiva
kṛtavarmā tv apāṁ patiḥ 1.5.24

“Vasu will decsned as Uddhava, Dakṣa as kind Akrūra, Kuvera as Hṛdīka, and Varuṇa as Kṛtavarmā.”

gadaḥ pracīnabarhiś ca
maruto hy ugrasena ut
tasya rakṣaṁ kariṣyāmi
rājyaṁ datvā vidhānataḥ 1.5.25

“Pracīnabarhi will descend as Gada and Maruta as Ugrasena. I will give the kingdom to Ugrasena and I will protect him.”

yuyudhānaś cāmbarīṣaḥ
prahlādaḥ satyakis tathā
kṣīrābdhiḥ śāntanuḥ sākṣād
bhīṣmo droṇo vasūttamaḥ 1.5.26

“Ambarīṣa will descend as Yuyudhāna, Prahlāda as Satyaki, Kṣīrābdhi as Śantanu, and Droṇa Vasūttama as Bhīṣma.”

śālyaś caiva divodāso
dhṛtarāṣṭro bhago raviḥ
pāṇḍuḥ puṣā satāṁ śreṣṭho
dharmo rājā yudhiṣṭhiraḥ 1.5.27

“Divodāsa will decsend as Śālya, Bhaga as Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Puṣā as Pāṇḍu, and King Dharma, the best of the devotees, as Yudhiṣṭhira.”

nakulaḥ sahadevaś ca
smṛtau dvāv aśvinī-sutau
dhātā bāhlīkavīraś ca
vahnir droṇaḥ pratāpavān 1.5.29

“The two Aśvinī-kumāras will descend as Nakula and Sahadeva. Dhātā will descend as heroic Bāhlīka, and powerful Vahni as Droṇācārya.”

duryodhanaḥ kaler aṁśo
’bhimanyuḥ soma eva ca
drauṇiḥ sākṣāc chivasyāpi
rūpaṁ bhūmau bhaviṣyati 1.5.30

“A partial incarnation of Kali will descend as Duryodhana. Soma will descend as Abhimanyu. Lord Śiva will place His own form on the earth as Droṇācārya’s son Aśvatthāmā.”

itthaṁ yadoḥ kauravāṇām
anyeṣāṁ bhū-bhujāṁ nṛṇām
kule kule bhavantaś ca
svāṁśaiḥ stribhir mad-ājñayā 1.5.31

“In this way, by My order (the demigods) and their wives will take birth in the families of the Yadus, Kurus, and other human kings.”

ye ye ’vatārā me pūrvaṁ
teṣāṁ rājñyo ramāṁśakāḥ
bhaviṣyā rāja-rājñīṣu
sahasrāṇi ca ṣoḍaśa 1.5.32

“Whenever in the past I incarnated in the world, the goddess of fortune would also incarnate as My queen. This time she will incarnate as My sixteen-thousand queens.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā śrī-haris tatra
brahmāṇaṁ kamalāsanam
divya-rūpāṁ bhagavatīṁ
yogamāyām uvāca ha 1.5.33

Śrī Nārada said: “After speaking these words to the demigod Brahmā, who sits on a lotus throne, Śrī Hari spoke to goddess Yogamāyā, whose form is spiritual.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
devakyāḥ saptamaṁ garbhaṁ
sannikṛṣya mahā-mate
vasudevasya bharyāyāṁ
kaṁsa-trāsa-bhayāt punaḥ
nanda-vraje sthitāyāṁ ca
rohiṇyāṁ sanniveśaya
nanda-patnyāṁ bhava tvaṁ vai
kṛtvedaṁ karma cādbhutam 1.5.34-35

The Blessed Lord said: “O noble-hearted one, take the seventh embryo from Vasudeva’s wife Devakī and place it in Rohiṇī, who is staying in Vraja out of fear of Kaṁsa. When you have done that wonderful deed, you yourself become an embryo in Nanda’s wife.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
śrutvā brahmā deva-gaṇair
natvā kṛṣṇaṁ parāt param
bhūmim āśvāsya vāṇībhiḥ
sva-dhāma nijam āyayau 1.5.36

Śrī Nārada said: “Hearing this, Brahmā and the demigods bowed down before Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is greater than the greatest, and, comforted the earth-goddess, and each went to his own abode.”

paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ viddhi maithila
kaṁsādīnāṁ vadhārthāya
prāpto ’yaṁ bhūmi-maṇḍale 1.5.37

“O king of Mithilā, please understand that Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who went to the earth to kill the demons headed by Kaṁsa, is the original Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

roma-mātraṁ tanau jihvā
bhavantītthaṁ yadā nṛpa
tadāpi śrī-hares tasya
varṇyate na guṇo mahān 1.5.38

“O king, If I had as many tongues as there are hairs on my body, I could still not properly describe Śrī Hari’s great and glorious qualities.”

Chapter 6 - Description of Kaṃsa’s Strength

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
kaḥ kaṁso ’yaṁ purā daityo
mahā-bala-parākramaḥ
tasya janmāṇi karmāṇi
brūhi devarṣi-sattama 1.6.1

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “Who was this very powerful demon Kaṁsa? O great sage, please describe his births and deeds.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
samudra-mathane pūrvaṁ
kālanemir mahāsuraḥ
yuyudhe viṣṇunā sārdhaṁ
yuddhe tena hato balāt 1.6.2

Śrī Nārada said: “At the time of churning the ocean a great demon named Kālanemi fought with Lord Viṣṇu and was violently killed by Him.”

śukreṇa jīvitas tatra
sañjīvinyā sva-vidyayā
punar viṣṇuṁ yoddhu-kāma
udyogaṁ manasākarot 1.6.3

“When with his sañjīvinī science Śukrācārya revived him, in his heart Kālanemi yearned to fight again with Lord Viṣṇu.”

tapas tepe tadā daityo
mandarācala-sannidhau
nityaṁ dūrvā-rasaṁ pītvā
bhajan devaṁ pitāmaham 1.6.4

“On Mount Mandara he performed severe austerities, drinking dūrvā-grass juice and worshiping the demigod Brahmā.”

divyeṣu śata-varṣeṣu
vyatīteṣu pitāmahaḥ
asthi-śeṣaṁ sa-vālmīkaṁ
varaṁ brūhīty uvāca tam 1.6.5

“When a hundred celestial years had passed all that remained of him was bones in an anthill. Then Brahmā said to him, Ask for a benediction,” and Kālanemi spoke a reply.”

kālanemir uvāca
brahmāṇḍe ye sthitā devā
viṣṇu-mūlā mahā-balāḥ
teṣāṁ hastair na me mṛtyuḥ
pūrṇānām api mā bhavet 1.6.6

Kālanemi said: “May my death not come from the hands of any or all of the very powerful demigods, who have Lord Viṣṇu as their root.”

śrī-brahmovāca
durlabho ’yaṁ varo daitya
yas tvayā prarthitaḥ paraḥ
kālāntare te praptaḥ syān
mad-vākyaṁ na mṛṣā bhavet 1.6.7

Śrī Brahmā said: “O demon, the benediction you request is difficult to attain. Still, you have now attained it. My words are not false.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ugrasenasya patnyāṁ kau
janma lebhe ’suraḥ punaḥ
kaumāre ’pi mahā-mallaiḥ
satataṁ sa yuyodha ha 1.6.8

Śrī Nārada said: “In Ugrasena’s wife the demon attained another demonic birth. Even in childhood he would regularly fight with great wrestlers.”

jarāsandho māgadhendro
dig-jayārthī vinirgataḥ
yamunā-nikaṭe tasya
śibiro ’bhūd itas tataḥ 1.6.9

“Wishing to conquer all directions, Jarāsandha, the king of Magadha, set his royal camp at different places by the Yamunā’s shore.”

dvipaḥ kuvalayāpīḍaḥ
sahasra-dvipa-sattva-bhṛt
babhañja śṛṅkhala-bandhaṁ
dudrāva śibirān madī 1.6.10

“Then the elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa, who was strong as thousands of other elephants, became angry, broke his chains, and ran from the royal camp.”

nipātayan sa śibirān
mandirān bhūbhṛtas taṭān
raṅga-bhūmyām ājagāma
yatra kaṁso ’pi yudhyati 1.6.11

“It ran from the royal camp to the mountains and entered an arena where Kaṁsa was fighting.”

palāyiteṣu malleṣu
kaṁsas taṁ ca samāgatam
śuṇḍā-daṇḍe saṅgṛhītvā
pātayām āsa bhū-tale 1.6.12

“When wrestlers fled and the elephant approached him, Kaṁsa grabbed its trunk and threw it to the ground.”

punar gṛhītvā hastābhyāṁ
bhrāmayitvograsenajaḥ
jarāsandhasya senāyāṁ
cīkṣepa daśa-yojanam 1.6.13

“Again grabbing it, Kaṁsa threw it into Jarāsandha’s army eighty miles away.”

tad adbhutaṁ balaṁ dṛṭṭvā
prasanno magadheśvaraḥ
asti-prāptī dadau kanye
tasmai kaṁsaya taṁ dvipam 1.6.14

“Pleased by seeing this amazing strength, the king of Magadha gave his two daughters, Asti and Prāpti, as well as that elephant, to Kaṁsa.”

aśvārbhudaṁ hasti-lakṣaṁ
rathānāṁ ca tri-lakṣakam
ayutaṁ caiva dāsīnāṁ
pāribarhaṁ jarā-sutaḥ 1.6.15

“For a wedding present Jarāsandha gave a hundred million horses, a hundred thousand elephants, three hundred thousand chariots, and ten thousand maidservants.”

dvandva-yodhī tataḥ kaṁso
bhuja-vīrya-madotkaṭaḥ
māhiṣmatīṁ yayau vīro
’thaikākī caṇḍa-vikramaḥ 1.6.16

“Then, very proud of the strength of his arms, and eager to fight, powerful Kaṁsa went, all alone, to Māhiṣmatī City.”

cāṇūro muṣṭikaḥ kūṭaḥ
śalas tośalakas tathā
māhiṣmatī-pateḥ putrā
mallā yuddha-jayaiṣiṇaḥ 1.6.17

“Cāṇūra, Muṣṭika, Kūṭa, Śāla, and Tośala, who were the sons of the king of Māhiṣmatī, were wrestlers very eager to defeat their opponents in fighting.”

kaṁsas tān āha sāmnāpi
duryuddhaṁ karavāmahe
ahaṁ dāso bhaveyaṁ vo
bhavanto jayino yadi 1.6.18

“Kaṁsa calmly said to them: “Let us fight. If you win, I will become your servant.”

ahaṁ jayī ced bhavato
dāsān sarvān karomy alam
sarveṣāṁ paśyatāṁ teṣāṁ
nāgarāṇāṁ mahātmanām
iti pratijñāṁ kṛtvātha
yuyudhe tair jayaiṣibhiḥ 1.6.19

““If I win, I will make you all my servants.” All these great fighters looked at him and agreed. Then Kaṁsa fought with them and they tried to defeat him.”

yadāgataṁ sa cāṇūraṁ
gṛhītvā yādaveśvaraḥ
bhū-pṛṭṭhe pothayām āsa
śabdam uccaiḥ samuccaran 1.6.20

“When Cāṇūra rushed at him, Kaṁsa made a great sound and threw him to the ground.”

tadāyāntaṁ muṣṭikākhyaṁ
muṣṭibhir yudhi durgamam
ekena muṣṭinā taṁ vai
pātayām āsa bhū-tale 1.6.21

“When Muṣṭika rushed at him with ferocious punches, with one punch Kaṁsa knocked him to the ground.”

kūṭaṁ samāgataṁ kaṁso
gṛhītvā pādayoś ca tam
pātayitvā bhūmi-madhye
cīkṣepa gagane balāt 1.6.22

“When Kūṭa rushed at him, Kaṁsa knowcked him to the ground, grabbed his feet, and violently threw him into the sky.”

bhujam āsphoṭya dhāvantaṁ
śalaṁ nītvā bhujena saḥ
pātayitvā punar nitvā
bhūmau taṁ vicakarṣa ha 1.6.23

“When Śala, waving his arms, rushed at him, Kaṁsa grabbed an arm, pulled him down, and dragged him about.”

atha tośalakaṁ kaṁso
gṛhītvā bhujayor balāt
nipatya bhūmav utthāpya
cīkṣepa daśa-yojanam 1.6.24

“Then Kaṁsa grabbed Tośala, knocked him to the ground, and, by the power of his arms, threw him eighty miles away.”

dāsa-bhāve ca tān kṛtvā
taiḥ sārdhaṁ yādavādhipaḥ
mad-vākyena jagāmāśu
pravarṣaṇa-giriṁ varam 1.6.25

“Then Kaṁsa made them all his servants and, on my advice, went with them to great Mount Pravaraṣaṇa.”

tasmai nivedyābhiprāyaṁ
yuyudhe vānareṇa saḥ
dvividenāpi viṁśatyā
dinaiḥ kaṁso hy aviśramam 1.6.26

“After presenting his proposal before him, Kaṁsa fought with the gorilla Dvivida for twenty days without becoming tired.”

dvivido girim utpatya
cīkṣepa tasya mūrdhani
kaṁso giriṁ gṛhītvā ca
tasyopari samākṣipat 1.6.27

“Dvivida uprooted a mountain and threw it on Kaṁsa’s head. Kaṁsa also grabbed a mountain and threw it on Dvivida.”

dvivido muṣṭinā kaṁsaṁ
ghātayitvā nabho gataḥ
dhāvan kaṁsas ca taṁ nitvā
pātayām āsa bhū-tale 1.6.28

“Dvivida punched Kaṁsa and jumped into the sky. Kaṁsa chased him and threw him to the ground.”

mūrchitas tat-prahāreṇa
paraṁ kaṣmalatāṁ yayau
kṣīṇa-sattvas cūrṇitāsthir
dāsa-bhāvaṁ gatas tadā 1.6.29

“In this way Dvivida was knocked unconscious. Dispirited, weakened, and his bones broken, he became Kaṁsa’s servant.”

tenaivātha gataḥ kaṁsa
ṛṣyāmūka-vanaṁ tataḥ
tatra keśī mahā-daityo
haya-rūpī ghana-svanaḥ 1.6.30

“Then Kaṁsa went to Ṛṣyamūka forest, where there was a great demon named Keśī, who had the form of a horse whinnying like thunder.”

muṣṭibhir ghātayitvā taṁ
vaśī-kṛtvāruroha tam
itthaṁ kaṁso mahā-vīryo
mahendrākhyaṁ giriṁ yayau 1.6.31

“Punching it again and again, Kaṁsa subdued the horse. Mounting it, very powerful Kaṁsa rode to Mount Mahendra.”

śata-vāraṁ cojjahāra
girim utpatya daitya-rāṭ
punas tatra sthitaṁ rāmaṁ
krodha-samrakta-locanam
pralayārka-prabhaṁ dṛṭṭvā
nanāma śirasā munim
punaḥ pradakṣiṇī-kṛtyo
tad-aṅghryor nipapāta ha 1.6.32

“A hundred times the demon-king Kaṁsa uprooted the mountain, held it high, and set it again in its place. When he saw Lord Paraśurāma, who was as effulgent as the sun at the time of cosmic dissolution, and whose eyes were now red with anger, Kaṁsa bowed his head before the sage, circumambulated Him, and fell at His feet.”

tataḥ śānto bhārgavo ’pi
kaṁsaṁ prāha mahogra-dṛk
he kīṭa karkaṭī-dimbha
tuccho ’si maṣako yathā 1.6.34

“Pacified by this, Paraśurāma, his eyes very fierce, said to Kaṁsa, “O worm! O baby crab! You are insignificant as a mosquito.”

adyaiva tvāṁ hanmi duṣṭa-
kṣatriyaṁ vīra-māninam
mat-samīpe dhanur idaṁ
lakṣa-bhāra-samaṁ mahat 1.6.35

“Today I will kill you, who are a wicked kṣatriya falsely proud of his strength. This great bow by My side weighs a hundred thousand bhāras.”

idaṁ ca viṣṇunā dattaṁ
sambhave traipure yudhi
śambhoḥ karād iha prāptaṁ
kṣatriyāṇāṁ vadhāya ca 1.6.36

“This bow was given by Lord Viṣṇu in the battle for Tripura City. To kill the kṣatriyas I received it from Lord Śiva’s own hand.”

yadi cedaṁ tanoṣi tvaṁ
tadā ca kuśalaṁ bhavet
ced asya karṣaṇaṁ na syād
ghātayiṣyāmi te balam 1.6.37

“If you can draw this bow it will be good for you. If you cannot draw it I will destroy your strength.”

śrutvā vacas tadā daityaḥ
ko-daṇḍaṁ sapta-tālakam
gṛhītvā paśyatas tasya
sajjaṁ kṛtvātha līlayā 1.6.38

“Hearing these words, the demon Kaṁsa picked up the bow made of seven palm trees. As Paraśurāma watched, Kaṁsa easily strung it.”

ākṛṣya karṇa-paryantaṁ
śata-vāraṁ tatāna ha
pratyañcāsphoṭanenaiva
ṭaṅkāro ’bhūt taḍit-svanaḥ 1.6.39

“A hundred times he drew the bowstring back to his ear and released it with a thunderous sound.”

nanāda tena brahmāṇḍaṁ
sapta-lokair bilaiḥ saha
vicelur dig-gajās tārā
’patan bhū-khaṇḍa-maṇḍalam 1.6.40

“That sound echoed in the universe of seven planetary systems and outer space. It made the elephants that support the directions tremble and it made the stars fall to the earth.”

dhanuḥ saṁsthāpya tat kaṁso
natvā natvāha bhārgavam
he deva kṣatriyo nāsmi
daityo ’haṁ te ca kiṅkaraḥ 1.6.41

“Putting the bow back in its place, and bowing again and again to Lord Paraśurāma, Kaṁsa said, “O Lord, I am not a kṣatriya. I am a demon, and I am Your servant.”

tava dāsasya dāso ’haṁ
pāhi māṁ puruṣottama
śrūtvā prasannaḥ śrī-rāmas
tasmai prādād dhanuś ca tat 1.6.42

““I am the servant of Your servant. O Supreme Person, please protect me.” Hearing this, Lord Paraśurāma was pleased with him and gave him that bow.”

śrī-jāmadagny uvāca
yat ko-daṇḍaṁ vaiṣṇavaṁ tad
yena bhaṅgī-bhaviṣyati
paripūrṇatamenātra
so ’pi tvāṁ ghātayiṣyati 1.6.43

Śrī Paraśurāma said: “Whoever breaks this bow of Lord Viṣṇu will kill you.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha natvā muniṁ kaṁso
vicaran sa mahotkaṭaḥ
na ke ’pi yuyudhus tena
rājānaś ca baliṁ daduḥ 1.6.44

Śrī Nārada said: “Then Kaṁsa bowed down before the sage Paraśurāma. As Kaṁsa thought of all this, he became filled with pride. No king could fight with him. They only brought him gifts.”

samudrasya taṭe kaṁso
daityaṁ nāmnā hy aghāsuram
saprakāraṁ ca phūtkārair
lelihānaṁ dadarśa ha 1.6.45

“On the seashore Kaṁsa saw a a demon named Aghāsura who, licking its tongue and hissing, had a serpent’s body.”

āgacchantaṁ daśantaṁ ca
gṛhītvā taṁ nipatya saḥ
cakāra sva-gale hāraṁ
nirbhayo daitya-rāḍ balī 1.6.46

“When the serpent approached and bit him, the fearless and very powerful demon-king Kaṁsa jumped on it and became like a necklace squeezing its neck.”

prācyāṁ tu vaṅga-deśeṣu
daityo ’riṣṭo mahā-vṛṣaḥ
tena sārdhaṁ sa yuyudhe
gajenāpi gajo yathā 1.6.47

“In the east a great bull-demon named Ariṣṭa fought with Kaṁsa as one elephant fights with another elephant.”

śṛṅgābhyāṁ parvataṁ nītvā
cīkṣepa kaṁsa-mūrdhani
kaṁso giriṁ saṅgṛhītvā
prākṣipat tasya mastake 1.6.48

“With its horns it uprooted a mountain and threw it at Kaṁsa’s head. Then Kaṁsa took a mountain and threw it at Ariṣṭa’s head.”

jaghāna muṣṭināriṣṭaṁ
kaṁso vai daitya-puṅgavaḥ
mūrchitaṁ taṁ vinirjitya
tenodīcīṁ diśaṁ gataḥ 1.6.49

“With a single punch the great demon Kaṁsa made Ariṣṭa fall unconscious. After this victory Kaṁsa went to the north.”

prāgjyotiṣeśvaraṁ bhaumaṁ
narakākhyaṁ mahā-balam
uvāca kaṁso yuddhārthī
yuddhaṁ me dehi daitya-rāṭ 1.6.50

“There, eager to fight, Kaṁsa said to Narakāsura, Prāgjyotiṣa City’s very powerful king, who was a son of the earth-goddess, “O king of the demons, please give me a fight.”

ahaṁ dāso bhaveyaṁ vo
bhavanto jayino yadi
ahaṁ jayī ced bhavato
dāsān sarvān karomy aham 1.6.51

“If you are victorious, I will become your servant. If I am victorious I will make you my servant.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
pūrvaṁ pralambo yuyudhe
kaṁsenāpi mahā-balaḥ
mṛgendreṇa mṛgendro vā
udbhaṭena yathodbhaṭaḥ 1.6.52

“First a very powerful demon named Pralambāsura fought with Kaṁsa. They fought as one great lion fights with another great lion.”

malla-yuddhe gṛhītvā taṁ
kaṁso bhūmau nipatya ca
punar gṛhītvā cīkṣepa
prāgjyotiṣa-purodare 1.6.53

“Kaṁsa wrestled him to the ground and threw him into Prāgjyotiṣa City.”

āgato dhenuko nāmnā
kaṁsaṁ jagrāha roṣataḥ
nodayām āsa dūreṇa
balaṁ kṛtvātha dāruṇam 1.6.54

“Then a demon named Dhenuka came and grabbed Kaṁsa. With fearsome strength Kaṁsa angrily threw him far away.”

kaṁsas taṁ nodayām āsa
dhenukaṁ śata-yojanam
nipatya cūrṇayām āsa
tad-aṅga-muṣṭibhir dṛḍhaiḥ 1.6.55

“Kaṁsa threw Dhenuka eight hundred miles, jumped on him, and crushed him with many hard punches.”

tṛṇāvarto bhauma-vākyāt
kaṁsaṁ nītvā nabho gataḥ
tatraiva yuyudhe daitya
ūrdhvaṁ vai lakṣa-yojanam 1.6.56

“On Narakāsura’s order a demon named Tṛṇāvarta pulled Kaṁsa into the sky and fought with him eight hundred thousand miles in outer space.”

kaṁso ’nanta-balaṁ kṛtvā
daityaṁ nītvā tadāmbarāt
bhūmyāṁ sampātayām āsa
vamantaṁ rudhiraṁ mukhāt 1.6.57

“With limitless strength Kaṁsa dragged the demon from outer space to the earth and made him vomit blood from his mouth.”

tuṇḍenātha grasantaṁ ca
bakaṁ daityaṁ mahā-balam
kaṁso nipātayām āsa
muṣṭinā vajra-ghātinā 1.6.58

“When a powerful and demonic duck named Bakāsura tried to swallow him with its beak, Kaṁsa made it fall with a single punch hard as a thunderbolt.”

utthāya daityo balavān
sita-pakṣo ghana-svanaḥ
krodha-yuktaḥ samutpatya
tīkṣna-tuṇḍo ’grasac ca tam 1.6.59

“Lifting itself up, the white-winged demon made a sound like thunder and angrily swallowed Kaṁsa with its sharp beak.”

nigīrṇo ’pi sa vajrāṅgas
tad-gale rodha-kṛc ca yaḥ
sadyaś caccharda taṁ kaṁsaṁ
kṣata-kaṇṭho mahā-bakaḥ 1.6.60

“When he was swallowed, Kaṁsa became angry and his body became like a thunderbolt. Its throat breaking, the great duck Bakāsura spat Kaṁsa out at once.”

kaṁso bakaṁ saṅgṛhītvā
pātayitvā mahī-tale
karābhyāṁ bhrāmayitvā ca
yuddhe taṁ vicakarṣa ha 1.6.61

“Kaṁsa grabbed Bakāsura and threw it to the ground. As they fought, Kaṁsa grabbed it with both hands, whirled it around, and dragged it here and there.”

tat-svasāraṁ pūtanākhyāṁ
yoddhu-kāmām avasthitām
tām āha kaṁsaḥ prahasan
vākyaṁ me śṛṇu pūtane 1.6.62

“Then Bakāsura’s sister, who was named Pūtanā, came and wished to fight. Kaṁsa laughed and said to her, “Hear my words, O Pūtanā.”

striyā sārdham ahaṁ yuddhaṁ
na karomi kadācana
bakāsuraḥ syān me bhrātā
tvaṁ ca me bhaginī bhava 1.6.63

“I will never fight with a woman. Bakāsura has become my brother, so you should be my sister.”

Chapter 7 - Description of the Conquest of All Directions

atha kaṁsaḥ pralambādyair
anyaiḥ pūrva-jitaiś ca taiḥ
śambarasya puraṁ prāgāt
svābhiprayaṁ nivedayan 1.7.1

“Then, accompanied by Pralamba and the other demons he had defeated before, Kaṁsa went to the city of Śambara and made his offer.”

śambaro hy ati-vīryo ’pi
na yuyodha sa tena vai
cakāra sauhṛdaṁ kaṁse
sarvair ati-balaiḥ saha 1.7.2

“Although he was very powerful, Śambara would not fight. Instead, he made friends with Kaṁsa and all his very powerful allies.”

tri-śṛṅga-śikhare śete
vyomo nāmāsuro balī
kaṁsa-pāda-prabuddho ’bhūt
krodha-samrakta-locanaḥ 1.7.3

“A very strong demon named Vyoma slept on the summit of Mount Trikūṭa. When Kaṁsa awakened him with a kick, his eyes became red with rage.”

kaṁsaṁ jaghāna cotthāya
prabalair dṛḍha-muṣṭibhiḥ
tayor yuddham abhūd ghoraṁ
itaretara-muṣṭibhiḥ 1.7.4

“He stood up and attacked Kaṁsa with hard punches. Then they fought a terrible battle with their fists.”

kaṁsasya muṣṭibhiḥ so ’pi
niḥsattvo ’bhūd bhramāturaḥ
bhṛtyaṁ kṛtvātha taṁ kaṁsaḥ
prāptaṁ māṁ praṇanāma ha 1.7.5

“Kaṁsa’s punches weakened him and made him dizzy. Kaṁsa made Vyoma his servant. Then Kaṁsa came to me and bowed down to offer respects.”

he deva yuddha-kaṅkṣāsti
kva yāmi tvaṁ vadāśu me
provāca taṁ tadā gaccha
daityaṁ bāṇaṁ mahā-balam 1.7.6

“He said, “O lord, I yearn to fight. Where should I go?” I said to him, “Go to the very powerful demon Bāṇāsura.”

preritaś ceti kaṁsākhyo
mayā yuddhi-didṛkṣuṇā
bhuja-vīrya-madonnaddhaḥ
śonitākhyaṁ puraṁ yayau 1.7.7

“Thus sent by me, Kaṁsa, who was filled with pride in the strength of his arms and was looking for a fight, went to the city named Śonitā.”

bāṇāsuras tat-pratijñāṁ
śrūtvā kruddho hy abhūd bhṛśam
tatāḍa lattāṁ bhū-madhye
jagarja ghanavad balī 1.7.8

“When he heard Kaṁsa’s offer, powerful Bāṇāsura became very angry. He kicked the ground and roared like thunder.”

ā-jānu-bhūmi-gaṁ lattāṁ
pātālāntam upāgatam
kṛtvā tam āha bāṇas tu
pūrvaṁ cainaṁ samuddhara 1.7.9

“That kick pushed his leg through the ground up to his knee as his foot reached Pātālaloka. Then Bāṇāsura said, Please pull me up as I was before.”

śrūtvā vacaḥ karābhyāṁ taṁ
ujjahāra madotkaṭaḥ
pracaṇḍa-vikramaḥ kaṁsaḥ
khara-daṇḍaṁ gajo yathā 1.7.10

“Hearing these words, with both hands proud and powerful Kaṁsa pulled him up as an elephant pulls up a lotus.”

tayā coddhṛtayotkhātā
lokāḥ sapta-talā dṛḍhāḥ
nipetur girayo ’nekā
vicelur dṛḍha-dig-gajāḥ 1.7.11

“By this pulling the seven Tala planets were devastated, many great mountains fell, and the sturdy elephants holding the directions stumbled.”

yoddhuṁ tam udyataṁ bāṇaṁ
