dṛṭṭvāgatya vṛṣadhvajaḥ
sarvān sambodhayām āsa
provāca bali-nandanam 1.7.12

Seeing that Bāṇāsura was very eager to fight, Lord Śiva arrived, greeted everyone, and to Bali Mahārāja’s son, Bāṇāsura, said:”

kṛṣṇaṁ vināparaṁ cainaṁ
bhūmau ko ’pi na jeṣyati
bhārgaveṇa varaṁ dattaṁ
dhanur asmai ca vaiṣṇavam 1.7.13

“Except for Lord Kṛṣṇa, no one in the world can defeat him. Lord Paraśurāma gave him that benediction and also gave him Lord Viṣṇu’s own bow.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā sauhṛdaṁ hṛdyaṁ
sadyo vai kaṁsa-bāṇayoḥ
cakāra parayā śāntyā
śivaḥ sākṣān maheśvaraḥ 1.7.14

Śrī Nārada said: “By speaking these words Lord Śiva, who is the Supreme Lord Himself, brought peace between Kaṁsa and Bāṇāsura and made them great friends.”

atha kaṁso dik-pratīcyāṁ
śrūtvā vatsaṁ mahāsuram
tena sārdhaṁ sa yuyudhe
vatsa-rūpeṇa daitya-rāṭ 1.7.15

“Hearing of a great demon named Vatsāsura who lived in the west, the demon-king Kaṁsa fought with him, a demon who had the form of a calf.”

pucche gṛhītvā taṁ vatsaṁ
pothayām āsa bhū-tale
vaśī-kṛtvātha taṁ śīghraṁ
mleccha-deśāṁs tato yayau 1.7.16

“Kaṁsa grabbed the calf Vatsāsura by the tail and threw it to the ground, in this way bringing it under his dominion. Then he quickly went to the countries of the barbarian mlecchas.”

man-mukhāt kālayavanaḥ
śrūtvā daityaṁ mahā-balam
niryayau sammukhe yoddhuṁ
rakta-smaśrur gadā-dharaḥ 1.7.17

“Hearing about him from my mouth, red-bearded Kālayavana picked up a club and went before the powerful demon Kaṁsa to fight with him.”

kaṁso gadāṁ gṛhītvā svaṁ
lakṣa-bhāra-vinirmitam
prākṣipad yāvanendrāya
siṁha-nādam athākarot 1.7.18

“Kaṁsa picked up his own club, which weighed a hundred thousand bhāras, threw it at the barbarian king, and then roared like a lion.”

gadā-yuddham abhūt tatra
ghoraṁ hi kaṁsa-kālayoḥ
visphuliṅgān kṣarantyau dve
gade cūrṇī-babhūvatuḥ 1.7.19

“Then Kaṁsa and Kālayavana fought a terrible duel with clubs, a duel where, throwing many sparks, the clubs were ground to powder.”

kaṁsaḥ kālaṁ saṅgṛhītvā
pātayām āsa bhū-tale
punar gṛhītvā niṣpatya
mṛta-tulyaṁ cakāra ha 1.7.20

“Kaṁsa grabbed Kālayavana, threw him to the gound, jumped on him, and attacked him so violently he almost died.”

bāṇa-varṣaṁ prakurvantīṁ
senāṁ tāṁ yavanasya ca
gadayā pothayām āsa
kaṁso daityādhipo balī 1.7.21

“powerful. When Kālayavana’s army showered him with arrows, the powerful demon-king Kaṁsa knocked it to the ground with his club.”

gajāṁs turaṅgān sa-rathān
vīrān bhūmau nipātya ca
jagarja ghanavad vīro
gadā-yuddhe mṛdhāṅgane 1.7.22

“When he knocked down the elephants, horses, chariots and warriors in the club battlefield, powerful Kaṁsa roared like thunder.”

tataś ca durdruvur mlecchāḥ
tyaktvā svaṁ svaṁ raṇaṁ param
bhītān palāyitān mlecchān
na jaghānātha nīti-vit 1.7.23

“Then the barbarians fled the battlefield. Aware of the code of chivalry, Kaṁsa did not attack the frightened, fleeing barbarians.”

ucca-pādo dīrgha-jānuḥ
stambhorur laghimā kaṭiḥ
kapaṭa-vakṣaḥ pīnāṁsaḥ
puṣṭaḥ prāṁśur bṛhad-bhujaḥ
padma-netro bṛhat-keśo
’ruṇa-varṇo ’sitāmbaraḥ
kirīṭī kuṇḍalī hārī
padma-mālī layārka-ruk
khaḍgī niṣaṅgi kavacī
mudgarāḍhyo dhanur-dharaḥ
madotkaṭo yayau jetuṁ
devān kaṁso ’marāvatīm 1.7.24-26

“Lifting his feet and knees, his thighs like pillars, his waist slender, His chest a great door, his shoulders broad, His arms massive, His eyes lotus flowers, his hair long, his complexion ruddy, his garments black, wearing a helmet, earrings, necklace, and lotus garland, effulgent as the sun at the time of cosmic dissolution, wielding a sword, shield, quiver of arrows, and club, proud Kaṁsa went to Amarāvatī to conquer the demigods.”

cāṇūra-muṣṭikāriṣṭa-
śala-tośala-keśibhiḥ
pralambena bakenāpi
dvividena samāvṛtaḥ
tṛṇāvartāgha-kūṭaiś ca
bhauma-bāṇākhya-śambaraiḥ
vyoma-dhenuka-vatsaiś ca
rurudhe so ’marāvatīm 1.7.27

“Aided by Cāṇūra, Muṣṭika, Ariṣṭa, Śala, Tośala, Keśī, Pralamba, Baka, Dvivida, Tṛṇāvarta, Agha, Kūṭa, Narakāsura, Bāṇa, Śambara, Vyoma, Dhenuka, and Vatsa, he besieged the city of Aamarāvatī.”

kaṁsādīn āgatān dṛṭṭvā
śakro devādhipaḥ svarāṭ
sarvair deva-gaṇaiḥ sārdhaṁ
yoddhuṁ kruddho viniryayau 1.7.29

“Seeing the demons headed by Kaṁsa had come, Indra, the sovereign king of the demigods became angry and, accompanied by a great host of demigods, went to fight with them.”

tayor yuddham abhūd ghoraṁ
tumulaṁ roma-harṣaṇam
divyaiś ca śastra-sampātair
bāṇais tīkṣnaiḥ sphurat-prabhaiḥ
śastrāndhakāre sañjāte
rathārūḍho maheśvaraḥ
cīkṣepa vajraṁ kaṁsāya
śata-dhāraṁ taḍid-dyuti 1.7.30-31

“Then they fought a terrible, tumultuous battle that made hairs stand up. When hosts of sharp and glistening arrows and divine weapons created a blinding darkness, King Indra, riding on a chariot, threw his thunderbolt, flowing with a hundred streams and glittering with lightning, at Kaṁsa.”

mudgareṇāpi tad-vajraṁ
taṭādāśu mahāsuraḥ
papāta kuliśaṁ yuddhe
chinna-dhāraṁ babhūva ha 1.7.32

“With his club the great demon Kaṁsa struck the thunderbolt. Its streams of lightning broken, it fell in the battle.”

tyaktvā vajraṁ tadā vajrī
khaḍgaṁ jagrāha roṣataḥ
kaṁsaṁ mūrdhni taṭādāśu
nādaṁ kṛtvātha bhairavam 1.7.33

“Abandoning the thunderbolt, Indra took up a sword and angrily struck Kaṁsa on the head, making a terrible sound.”

sa kṣato nābhavat kaṁso
mālā-hata iva dvipaḥ
gṛhītvā sa gadāṁ gurvīṁ
aṣṭa-dhātu-mayīṁ dṛḍhām 1.7.34

“Kaṁsa remained unhurt, like an elephant struck by a flower garland. Then he took a great and heavy club made of eight metals.”

lakṣa-bhāra-samāṁ kaṁsaś
cīkṣependrāya daitya-rāṭ
tāṁ samāpatatīṁ vīkṣya
jagrāhāśu purandaraḥ 1.7.35

“Then the demon-king Kaṁsa threw that club weighing a hundred thousand bhāras at Indra. Seeing it coming, Indra caught it.”

tataś cīkṣepa daityāya
gadāṁ namuci-sūdanaḥ
cacāra yuddhe vidalann
arīn mātali-sārathiḥ 1.7.36

“Indra, the killer of Namuci, threw the club back at the demon. Then Indra, cutting his enemies to pieces, and his chariot driven by Mātali, went into the battle.”

kaṁso gṛhītvā parighaṁ
taṭādāṁse ’sura-dviṣaḥ
tat-prahāreṇa devendraḥ
kṣaṇaṁ murchām avāpa ha 1.7.37

“Taking an iron club, Kaṁsa struck Indra on the shoulder. Because of that blow, Indra, the king of the demigods and enemy of the demons, became momentarily unconscious.”

kaṁsaṁ marud-gaṇāḥ sarve
gṛḍhra-pakṣaiḥ sphurat-prabhaiḥ
bāṇaughaiś chādayām āsuḥ
pravṛt-sūryam ivāmbudaḥ 1.7.38

“Then all the Maruts coverd Kaṁsa with a great and splendid flood of gṛdhra-feathered arrows like a monsoon cloud covering the sun.”

doḥ-sahasra-yuto vīras
cāpāṁs ṭāṅkārayan muhuḥ
tadā tān kālayām āsa
bāṇair bāṇāsuro balī 1.7.39

“Making a great sound as he worked many bows with his thousand arms, the powerful warrior Bānaāsura drove them back with a host of arrows.”

bāṇaṁ ca vasavo rudrā
ādityā ṛbhavaḥ surāḥ
jaghnur nānā-vidhaiḥ śastraiḥ
sarvato ’driṁ yathā gajāḥ 1.7.40

“Surrounding him as a host of elephants might surround a mountain, the Vasus, Rudras, Adityas, Ṛbhus, and Suras attacked Bāṇāsura with a great variety of weapons.”

tato bhaumāsuraḥ prāptaḥ
pralambādy-asurair nadan
tena nādena devās te
nipetur mūrchitā raṇe 1.7.41

“Then, assisted by Pralamba and the other demons, Narakāsura came and made a great sound. That sound made the demigods fall unconscious on the battleground.”

utthāyāśu tadā sākro
gajam āruhya rakta-dṛk
nodayām āsa kaṁsāya
mattam airāvataṁ gajam
aṅkuśāsphalanāt kruddhaṁ
pātayantaṁ padair dviṣaḥ
śuṇḍā-daṇḍasya phūtkarair
mārdayantam itas tataḥ
sravan-madaṁ catur-dantaṁ
himādrim iva durgamam
nadantaṁ srṅkhalaṁ śuṇḍāṁ
cālayantaṁ muhur muhuḥ
ghaṇṭāḍhyaṁ kiṅkinī-jāla-
ratna-kambala-maṇḍitam
go-sūtra-caya-sindūra-
kastūrī-pātra-bhṛn-mukham 1.7.42-45

“Quickly rising, and his eyes now red, Indra mounted his furious four-tusked elephant Airāvata, now provoked by the striking of the goad, trumpeting with its trunk, crushing its enemies under its moving feet, its rut flowing, unapproachable as a mountain of snow, jingling its chains, moving its trunk again and again, opulent with bells, decorated with a jewel saddle and a network of tinkling ornaments, and its face decorated with pictures and designs drawn in musk, sindūra and gomūtra, and made it charge Kaṁsa.”

dṛḍhena muṣṭinā kaṁsas
taṁ taṭāda mahā-gajam
dvitīya-muṣṭinā śakraṁ
sa jaghāna raṇāṅgane 1.7.46

“With a hard punch Kaṁsa struck the great elephant and with a second punch he struck Indra in that battleground.”

tasya muṣṭi-prahāreṇa
dūre śakraḥ papāta ha
jānūbhyāṁ dharaṇīṁ spṛṣṭvā
gajo ’pi vihvalo ’bhavat 1.7.47

“With that punch Indra fell far away and the elephant was wounded and fell, its knees touching the ground.”

punar utthāya nāgendro
dantaiś cāhatya daityapam
śuṇḍā-daṇḍena coddhṛtvā
cīkṣepa lakṣa-yojanam 1.7.48

“Again standing, the king of elephants attacked the demon-king Kaṁsa with its tusks, picked him up with its trunk, and threw him eight-hundred thousand miles.”

patito ’pi sa vajrāṅgaḥ
kiñcid-vyākula-mānasaḥ
sphurad-oṣṭho ’ti-ruṣṭāṅgi
yuddha-bhūmiṁ samāyayau 1.7.49

“His body hard as a thunderbolt, when he fell he was only a little upset in his heart. Angry, and his lips trembling, he returned to the battleground.”

kaṁso gṛhītvā nāgendraṁ
sannipatya raṇāṅgane
niṣpīḍya śuṇḍāṁ tasyāpi
dantāṁs cūrṇī-cakāra ha 1.7.50

“Kaṁsa grabbed the elephant-king Airāvata, threw him onto the battleground, strangled its trunk, and broke its tusks into pieces.”

atha cairāvato nāgo
dudrāvāśu raṇāṅganāt
nipātayan mahā-vīrān
devadhānīṁ purīṁ gataḥ 1.7.51

“Knocking over many great heroes, the elephant Airāvata fled the battleground and went to the capitol of the demigods.”

gṛhītvā vaiṣṇavaṁ cāpaṁ
sajjaṁ kṛtvātha daitya-rāṭ
devān vidrāvayām āsa
bāṇaughaiś ca dhanuḥ-svanaiḥ 1.7.52

“Taking the bow of Lord Viṣṇu and stringing it, with a flood of arrows accompanied by the twanging sound of the bow, the demon-king Kaṁsa made the demigods flee.”

tataḥ surās tena nihanyamānā
vidudruvur dīna-dhiyo diśaṁ te
kecid raṇe mukta-śikhā babhūvur
bhītāḥ sma itthaṁ yudhi vādinas te 1.7.53

“As Kaṁsa was attacking and killing them, the dispirited demigods fled in all directions. Some, their helmets lost, screamed in terror.”

kecit tathā prāñjalayo ’ti-dīna-vat
sannyasta-śastrā yudhi mukta-kacchakāḥ
sthātuṁ raṇe kaṁsa-nṛ-deva-sammukhe
gatepsitāḥ kecid atīva-vihvalāḥ 1.7.54

“Some, dropping their weapons and armor, humbly surrendered with folded hands. Others, very troubled, had lost all desire to stand before King Kaṁsa.”

Chapter 8 - Description of Śrī Rādhikā’s Birth

śrī-garga uvāca
śrutvā tadā śaunaka bhakti-yuktaḥ
śrī-maithilo jñāna-bhṛtaṁ variṣṭhaḥ
natvā punaḥ prāha muniṁ mahantaṁ
devarṣi-vāryaṁ hari-bhakti-niṣṭhaḥ 1.8.1

Śrī Garga Muni said: “O Śaunaka, when Bahulāśva, the king of Mithilā, who was filled with faith and devotion to Lord Hari and who was the best of the wise, heard this, he again bowed down and spoke to the great sage Nārada, the best of the devarṣis.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
tvayā kulaṁ kau viśadī-kṛtaṁ me
śubhaṁ harer yad yaśasāmalena
śrī-kṛṣṇa-bhakta-kṣaṇa-saṅgamena
jano ’pi sat syād bahunā kim u svit 1.8.2

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “By giving it the narrations of Lord Hari’s pure glories you purified my materialistic family and made it auspicious. By a moment’s touch with Lord Kṛṣṇa’s devotee even an ordinary person will become a saint. What more need I say?”

śrī-rādhayā pūrṇatamas tu sākṣād
gatvā vraje kiṁ caritaṁ cakāra
tad brūhi me deva ṛśe ṛśīśa
tri-tāpa-duḥkhāt paripāhi māṁ tvam 1.8.3

“What did the Supreme Personality of Godhead do when He came with Śrī Rādhā to Vraja? O Lord, O great sage, please tell me. O king of the sages, please save me from the three miseries.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
dhanyaṁ kulaṁ yan niminā nṛpena
śrī-kṛṣṇa-bhaktena parat-pareṇa
pūrṇī-kṛtaṁ yatra bhavān prajātaḥ
śuktau hi muktā-bhavanaṁ na citram 1.8.4

Śrī Nārada said: “The family of your birth is fortunate for it was made perfect by King Nimi, who was a very great devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is not surprising that a pearl is born in an oyster.”

atha prabhos tasya pavitra-līlāṁ
su-maṅgalāṁ saṁśṛṇutāṁ parasya
abhūt satāṁ yo bhuvi rakṣaṇārthaṁ
na kevalaṁ kaṁsa-vadhāya kṛṣṇaḥ 1.8.5

“Please hear the purifying pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The Lord did not only come to kill Kaṁsa. He also came to protect the devotees.”

athaiva rādhā vṛśabhānu-patnyām
āveśya rūpaṁ mahasaḥ parākhyam
kalindajā-kula-nikuñja-deśe
su-mandire sāvatatāra rājan 1.8.6

“Then, placing Her glorious transcendental form in (the womb of) King Vṛṣabhānu’s wife, Śrī Rādhā descended into a great palace in a garden by the Yamunā’s shore.”

ghanāvṛte vyomni dinasya madhye
bhadre site nāga-tithau ca some
avākiran deva-gaṇāḥ sphuradbhis
tan-mandire nandanajaiḥ prasūnaiḥ 1.8.7

“In the month of Bhādra (August-September), on a Monday that was the eighth day of the bright fortnight of the moon, at midday, when the sky was covered with clouds, (to celebrate Rādhā’s descent) the demigods scattered flowers that had blossomed in the Nandana gardens.”

rādhāvatāreṇa tadā babhūvur
nadyo ’malāmbhaś ca diśaḥ praseduḥ
vavuś ca vātā aravinda-rāgaiḥ
su-śītalāḥ sundara-manda-yānāḥ 1.8.8

“Because of Rādhā’s descent the rivers became very pure and clear, the directions became auspicious and happy, and graceful, gentle, cooling breezes carried the pollen of lotus flowers.”

sutāṁ śarac-candra-śatābhirāmāṁ
dṛṭṭvātha kīrtir mudam āpa gopī
śubhaṁ vidhāyāśu dadau dvijebhyo
dvi-lakṣam ānanda-karaṁ gavāṁ ca 1.8.9

“Gazing at her daughter beautiful as hundreds of moons, the gopī Kīrti became happy. To bring auspiciousness she gave two hundred thousand cows in charity to please the brāhmaṇas.”

preṅkhe khacid-ratna-mayūkha-pūrṇe
suvarṇa-yukte kṛta-candanāṅge
andolitā sā vavṛdhe sakhī-janair
dine dine candra-kaleva bhābhiḥ 1.8.11

“By friends rocked in a golden cradle-swing splendid with jewels, like the waxing moon day by day She grew.”

yad-darśanaṁ deva-varaiḥ su-durlabhaṁ
yajñair na vāptaṁ jana-janma-koṭibhiḥ
sa-vigrahaṁ taṁ vṛśabhānu-mandire
lalanti lokā lalanā-pralālanaiḥ 1.8.12

“She whose form is transcendental, who the demigods cannot see, and who cannot be attained by performing yajñas for millions of births, the women in King Vṛṣabhānu’s palace played with, fondled, caressed, and loved.”

śrī-rāsa-raṅgasya vikāśa-candrikā
dīpāvalīr yā vṛśabhānu-mandire
goloka-cūḍā-maṇi-kaṇṭha-bhūśaṇāṁ
dhyātvā parāṁ tāṁ bhuvi paryaṭāmy aham 1.8.13

“Meditating on Her, who is a moon shining in the rāsa-dance arena, who is a host of bright lamps in King Vṛṣabhānu’s palace, and who is the central jewel in the necklace of Goloka, I wander on this earth.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
vṛśabhāno raho-bhāgyaṁ
yaśā rādhā sutābhavat
kalāvatyā sucandreṇa
kiṁ kṛtaṁ pūrva-janmani 1.8.14

“It is King Vṛṣabhānu’s glory and secret good fortune that Rādhā became his daughter. What did Kalāvatī and Sucandra do in their previous birth?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
nṛga-putro mahā-bhāgaḥ
sucandro nṛpatīśvaraḥ
cakravartī harer aṁso
babhūvātīva-sundaraḥ 1.8.15

Śrī Nārada said: Sucandra, the very fortunate and handsome son of King Nṛga, was the king of many other kings. He was an aṁśa-avatāra of Lord Hari.”

pitṛṇāṁ manasī-kanyās
tisro ’bhuvan manoharāḥ
kalāvatī ratnamālā
menakā nāma nāmataḥ 1.8.16

“The Pitṛs had three mind-born daughters named Kalāvatī, Ratnamālā, and Menakā.”

kalāvatīṁ sucandrāya harer aṁsāya
dhīmate vaidehāya ratnamālāṁ
menakāṁ ca himādraye pāribarheṇa
vidhinā svecchabhiḥ pitaro daduḥ 1.8.17

“Voluntarily, along with wedding gifts, and following the rules of Vedic tradition, the Pitṛs gave Kalāvatī to intelligent Sucandra, who was an aṁśa-avatāra of Lord Hari, Ratnamālā to the king of Videha, and Menakā to the king of the Himalayas.”

sītābhūd ratnamālāyāṁ
menakāyāṁ ca pārvatī
dvayoś caritraṁ viditaṁ
purāṇeṣu mahā-mate 1.8.18

“From Ratnamālā, Sītā was born, and from Menakā, Pārvatī was born. O noble-hearted one, their activities are known in the Purāṇas.”

sucandro ’tha kalāvatyā
gomatī-tīraje vane divyair
dvādaśabhir varśais
tatāpa brahmaṇas tapaḥ 1.8.19

“To please the demigod Brahmā, Sucandra and Kalāvatī performed austerities for twelve celestial years in a forest by the Gomatī’s shore.”

atho vidhis tam āgatya
varaṁ brūhīty uvāca ha
śrutvā valmīka-deśāc ca
niryayau divya-rūpa-dhṛk 1.8.20

“Then Brahmā came to that place and said, ‘Ask a benediction.’ Hearing this, Sucandra, now having a splendid celestial body, emerged from the anthill.”

taṁ natvovāca me bhūyād
divyaṁ mokṣaṁ parat-param
tac chrutvā duḥkhitā sādhvī
vidhiṁ prāha kalāvatī 1.8.21

“He bowed down and said, “May I have the highest spiritual liberation.” Hearing this, saintly Kalāvatī became unhappy and said to Brahmā:”

patir eva hi nārīṇāṁ daivataṁ paramaṁ smṛtam
yadi mokṣam asau yāti tadā me kā gatir bhavet 1.8.22

“‘For women their husband is the ultimate object of worship. If (my) husband attains liberation, then what will become of me?”

etad vinā na jīvāmi yadi mokṣaṁ pradāsyasi
tubhyaṁ śāpaṁ pradāsyāmi pati-vīkṣepa-vihvalā 1.8.23

“I cannot live without him. If you give him liberation, then I, tormented by the loss of my husband, will curse you.”

śrī-brahmovāca
tvac-chāpād bhaya-bhīto ’haṁ me varo ’pi mṛśā na hi
tasmāt tvaṁ prāṇa-patinā sārdhaṁ gaccha tripiśṭapam 1.8.24

Brahmā said: ‘I fear your curse. Still, my benediction cannot go in vain. Therefore please go with your husband to the heavenly material planets.”

bhuktvā sukhāni kālena yuvāṁ bhūmau bhaviśyathaḥ
gaṅgā-yamunayor madhye dvāparānte ca bhārate 1.8.25

“After enjoying many happinesses there, in time, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga, you will both take birth in Bhārata-varṣa, in the country between the Gaṅgā and Yamunā.”

yuvayo rādhikā sākṣāt paripūrṇatama-priyā
bhaviśyati yadā putrī tadā mokṣaṁ gamiśyathaḥ 1.8.26

“From you both Śrī Rādhā, the beloved of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, will be born. When She becomes your daughter you will both attain liberation’.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
itthaṁ brahma-vareṇātha divyenāmogha-rūpiṇā
kalāvatī-sucandrau ca bhūmau tau dvau babhūvatuḥ 1.8.27

Śrī Nārada said: In this way because of Brahmā’s infallible spiritual benediction Kalāvatī and Sucandra were born on the earth.”

kalāvatī kānyakuñje bhalandana-nṛpasya ca
jāti-smarā hy abhūd divyā yajña-kuṇḍa-samudbhavā 1.8.28

“Then, remembering her previous birth, glorious Kalāvatī was born in King Bhalandana’s yajña-pond in Kānyakubja.”

sucandro vṛśabhānv-ākhyaḥ
surabhānu-gṛhe ’bhavat
jāti-smāro gopa-varaḥ
kāmadeva ivāparaḥ 1.8.29

“Then, remembering his previous birth, Sucandra, who was now a great gopa, like another Kāmadeva, and now named Vṛṣabhānu, was born in the home of King Surabhānu.”

sambandhaṁ yojayām āsa
nanda-rājo mahā-matiḥ
tayoś ca jāti-smarayor
icchator icchayā dvayoḥ 1.8.28

“King Nanda arranged the marriage of these two, who both desired it, and who both remembered their previous birth.”

Chapter 9 - Description of Vasudeva’s Wedding

śrī-nārada uvāca
tatraikadā śrī-mathurā-pure vare
purohitaḥ sarva-yadūttamaiḥ kṛtaḥ
surecchayā garga iti pramāṇikaḥ
samāyayau sundara-rāja-mandiram 1.9.1

Śrī Nārada said: “One day the great scholar Garga Muni, whom all the Yadus had made their chief priest, by his own wish went in Mathurā City to the king’s handsome palace, . . .”

hīrā-khacid-dhema-lasat-kapāṭakaṁ
dvipendra-karṇāhata-bhrṅga-nāditam
ibha-sravan-nirjhara-gaṇḍa-dhārayā
samardritaṁ mandapa-khaṇḍa-maṇḍitam 1.9.2

“. . . which had great glistening golden doors studded with diamonds, which was filled with the sounds of bees and the flapping of elephants’ ears, which was wet with streams of ichor flowing from the elephants, and which was decorated with many pavilions, . . .”

mahodbhaṭair vīra-janaiḥ sa-kañcukair
dhanur-dharaiś carma-kṛpāṇa-pāṇibhiḥ
ratha-dvipāśva-dhvajinī-balādibhiḥ
su-rakṣitaṁ maṇḍala-maṇḍalībhiḥ 1.9.3

“. . . and which was well protected by great heroic soldiers wearing armor, carrying bows, holding swords and shields in their hands, and by the great power of an army riding on chariots, elephants, and horses.”

dadarśa gargo nṛpadevam āhukaṁ
śvaphalkinā devaka-kaṁsa-sevitam
śrī-śakra-siṁhāsana unnate pare
sthitaṁ vṛtaṁ cchātra-vitāna-cāmaraiḥ 1.9.4

“There Garga Muni saw emperor Ugrasena who was accompanied Akrūra, served by Devaka and Kaṁsa, and surrounded by parasols, awnings, and cāmaras as he sat on Indra’s great throne”

dṛṭṭvā muniṁ taṁ sahasāsanāśayād
utthāya rājā praṇanāma yādavaiḥ
saṁsthāpya sampūjya subhadra-pīṭhake
stutvā parikramya nataḥ sthito ’bhavat 1.9.5

“Seeing the sage, the emperor at once rose from his throne, offered respectful obeisances with the Yādavas, placed the sage on the throne, worshiped him, glorified him, circumambulated him, and bowed down before him.”

dattvāśiṣaṁ garga-munir nṛpāya vai
papraccha sarvaṁ kuśalaṁ nṛpādiṣu
śrī-devakaṁ prāha mahā-manā ṛṣir
mahaujasaṁ nīti-vidaṁ yadūttamam 1.9.6

“Garga Muni blessed the king and asked about the welfare of the king and his associates. Then the saintly sage spoke to powerful and wise Śrī Devaka, the best of the Yādavas.”

śrī-garga uvāca
śauriṁ vinā bhuvi nṛpeṣu varas tu nāsti
cintyo mayā bahu-dinaiḥ kila yatra tatra
tasmān nṛ-deva vasudeva-varāya dehi
śrī-devakīṁ tava sutāṁ vidhinodvahasva 1.9.7

Śrī Garga Muni said: “After thinking for many days I have decided that in this world no king is better than Vasudeva. Therefore, O king, please give your daughter Devakī in marriage to Vasudeva.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
kṛtvā tadaiva puri niścaya-nāgavalliṁ
śrī-devakaḥ sakala-dharma-bhṛtāṁ variṣṭhaḥ
gargecchayā tu vasudeva-varāya putrīṁ
kṛtvātha maṅgalam alaṁ pradadau vivāhe 1.9.8

Śrī Nārada said: “After offering (the sage) betelnuts to show his assent, Śrī Devaka, who was the best of all pious men, performed the auspicious ceremonies and gave his daughter in marriage to Vasudeva according to Garga Muni’s desire.”

kṛtodvahaḥ śaurir atīva-sundaraṁ
rathaṁ prayāne samalaṅkṛtaṁ hayaiḥ
sārdhaṁ tayā devaka-rāja-kanyayā
samāruhat kañcana-ratna-śobhayā 1.9.9

“When the marriage ceremony was over Vasudeva, accompanied by King Devaka’s daughter, who was beautiful with gold and jewels, climbed a very graceful decorated chariot drawn by many horses.”

svasuḥ priyaṁ kartum atīva kaṁso
jagrāha raśmīṁs calatāṁ hayānām
uvāha vāhāṁs caturāṅginībhir
vṛtaḥ kṛpā-sneha-paro ’tha śaurau 1.9.10

“To please his sister, Kaṁsa took the reins of the moving horses. Kind and affectionate to Vasudeva, and surrounded by an army, he drove the chariot.”

dāsī-sahasraṁ tv ayutaṁ gajanānāṁ
sat-pāribarhaṁ niyutaṁ hayānām
lakṣaṁ rathānāṁ ca gavāṁ dvi-lakṣaṁ
pradād duhitre nṛpa-devako vai 1.9.11

“King Devaka gave to his daughter a wedding present of a thousand maidservants, ten thousand elephants, a million horses, a hundred thousand chariots, and two hundred thousand cows.”

bheri-mṛdaṅgoddhara-gomukhānāṁ
dhundhurya-vīnānaka-veṇukānāṁ
mahat-svano ’bhūc calatāṁ yadūnāṁ
prayāna-kāle pathi maṅgalaṁ ca 1.9.12

“At the time of the Yādavas’ departure, on the path was a great auspicious sound of many bheris, mṛdaṅgas, uddharas, gomukhas, dhundhuryas, vīṇās, anakas, and veṇus.”

ākāśa-vāg āha tadaiva kaṁsaṁ
tvām aṣṭamo hi prasavo ’ñjasāsyāḥ
hantā na jānāsi ca yāṁ ratha-sthāṁ
rāśmiṁ gṛhītvā vahase ’budhas tvam 1.9.13

“Then a voice from the sky said to Kaṁsa: You are a fool. You do not know that the eighth son of the girl riding in the chariot you drive, holding the reins, will kill you.”

kusaṅga-niṣṭho ’ti-khalo hi kaṁso
hantuṁ svasāraṁ dhiṣaṇaṁ cakāra
kace gṛhitvā sita-khaḍga-pāṇir
gata-trapo nirdaya ugra-karmā 1.9.14

“Wicked, shameless, merciless Kaṁsa, whose friends were demons and whose deeds were horrible, threw his sister to the ground, grabbed her by the hair, and grasped a sharp sword in his hand to kill her.”

vaditra-kārā rahitā babhūvur
agre sthitāḥ syus cakitā hi paścāt
sarveṣu vā śveta-mukheṣu satsu
śauris tam āhāśu satāṁ variṣṭhaḥ 1.9.15

“The musicians dropped their instruments. They who were in front went to the rear. When all faces turned white, Vasudeva, the best of devotees, spoke to Kaṁsa.”

śrī-vasudeva uvāca
bhojendra bhoja-kula-kīrti-karas tvam eva
bhaumādi-māgadha-bakāsura-vatsa-bāṇaiḥ
ślāghyā guṇās tava yudhi pratiyoddhu-kāmaiḥ
sa tvaṁ kathaṁ tu bhaginīm asinātra hanyāt 1.9.16

Śrī Vasudeva said: “O king of the Bhojas, you are the glory of the Bhoja dynasty. Narakāsura, Jarāsandha, Bakāsura, Vatsāsura, and Bāṇāsura, who all yearned to fight with you, praise your virtues in battle. Why are you now on the verge of killing with a sword your own sister?”

jñātvā striyaṁ kila bakīṁ pratiyoddhu-kāmāṁ
yuddhaṁ kṛtaṁ na bhavatā nṛpa-nīti-vṛttyā
sā tu tvayāpi bhaginīva kṛtā praśāntyai
sākṣād iyaṁ tu bhaginī kim u te vicārāt 1.9.17

“Following the royal code of chivalry you would not fight with Pūtanā, a woman who yearned to fight with you, and to pacify her you treated her as if she were your sister. This girl is your real sister. Why do you wish to act in this way?”

udvāha-parvaṇi gatā ca tavānujā ca
bālā suteva kṛpaṇā śubhadā sadaiṣā
yogyo ’si nātra mathurādhipa hantum etāṁ
tvaṁ dīna-duḥkha-haraṇe kṛta-citta-vṛttiḥ 1.9.18

“On this, her festive wedding-day, you should be kind to your younger sister and treat her as a daughter. O king of Mathurā, you should not kill her. You should fix your heart on remving the sufferings of the distressed.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
nāmanyathetthaṁ pratibodhito ’pi
ku-saṅga-niṣṭho ’ti-khalo hi kaṁsaḥ
tadā hareḥ kāla-gatiṁ vicārya
śauriḥ prasannaḥ punar āha kaṁsam 1.9.19

Śrī Nārada said: “Although given this good instruction, wicked Kaṁsa, whose friends were demons, did not take it seriously. Then, reflecting on the ways of Lord Hari’s time, Vasudeva, now cheerful, again spoke to Kaṁsa.”

śrī-vasudeva uvāca
nāsyās tu te deva bhayaṁ kadācid
yad deva-vāṇyā kathitaṁ ca tac chṛṇu
putrān dadāmīti yato bhayaṁ syān
mā te vyathāsyāḥ prasava-prajātāt 1.9.20

Śrī Vasudeva said: “O lord, you never need to fear this girl. Hear what the divine voice said. I will give you the sons that frighten you. Don’t be afraid of the sons that may be born from her.”

Chapter 10 - Description of the Birth of Lord Balarāma

śrī-nārada uvāca
bhītah palāyate nāyaṁ
yoddhārah kaṁsa-noditah
ayutaṁ śastra-samyuktā
rurudhuh śauri-mandiraṁ 1.10.1

“Frightened Vasudeva could not flee, for ten thousand armed soldiers sent by Kaṁsa surrounded Vasudeva’s palace.”

śaurih kālena devakyām
aṣṭau putrān ajījanat
anuvarṣaṁ cātha kanyām
ekāṁ māyāṁ sanātanīm 1.10.2

“In the course of time, year after year, Vasudeva begat in Devakī eight sons and a daughter who was the eternal goddess Māyā.”

kīrtimantaṁ sutaṁ hy ādau
jātam ānakadundubhih
nītvā kaṁsa-sabhām etya
dadau tasmai parārtha-vit 1.10.3

“Vasudeva, who knew the ultimate goal of life, went to Kaṁsa’s assembly and offered his first son Kirrtimān.”

satya-vākya-sthitaṁ śauriṁ
dṛṣṭvā kaṁso ghṛṇī tv abhūt
duhkhaṁ sādhur na sahate
satye kasya kṣamā na hi 1.10.4

“Seeing that Vasudeva had kept his promise and his word was true, Kaṁsa became softened with kindness. In truth, what suffering will a saint not bear? What will he not tolerate?”

kaṁsa uvāca
eṣa bālo yātu gṛham
etasmān na hi me bhayam
yuvayor aṣṭamaṁ garbhaṁ
haniṣyāmi na saṁsayah 1.10.5

Kaṁsa said: “This child may go home. I do not fear him. I will kill your eighth son. Of this there is no doubt.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity ukto vasudevas tu
sa-putro gṛham āgatah
satyaṁ nāmanyata manāg
vākyaṁ tasya durātmanah 1.10.6

Śrī Nārada said: “Told this, Vasudeva went home with his son. However, he did not think the demon would make his words true.”

tadāmbarād āgataṁ māṁ
natvā pūjyograsenajah
papraccha devābhiprāyaṁ
prāvocaṁ taṁ nibodha me 1.10.7

“Then I came there from the sky. Kaṁsa bowed before me, worshiped me, and asked me about the demigods’ plans. I said to him, “Learn this from me.”

nandādyā vasavah sarve
vṛṣabhānv-ādayah surāh
gopyo veda-ṛcādyāś ca
santi bhūmau nṛpeśvara 1.10.8

““O king of kings, all the Vasus have become the people that have Nanda as their leader. The suras have become the people that have Vṛṣabhānu as their leader. A group that has the Vedic hymns as its leader has become gopīs. They are all now on the earth.”

vasudevādayo devā
mathurāyāṁ ca vṛṣṇayah
devaky-ādyāh striyah sarvā
devatāh santi niścayāh 1.10.9

“The devas have become the Vṛṣṇis headed by Vasudeva and all the demigoddesses have become their wives, headed by Devakī.”

sapta-vāra-prasaṅkhyānād
aṣṭamāh sarva eva hi
te hantuh saṅkhyayāyaṁ vai
devāṇāṁ vāmato gatih 1.10.10

“Depending on how they are counted, any of Vasudeva’s sons can be the eighth one. Any of them can be the one that will kill you. That is the crookedness of the demigods.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā taṁ mayi gate
kṛte daitya-vadhodyame
kaṁsah kopa-vṛtah sadyo
yadūn hantuṁ mano dadhe 1.10.11

Śrī Nārada said: “After I had gone, Kaṁsa became filled with anger at this plan (of the demigods) to kill the demons. In his heart he decided to kill all the Yādavas.”

vasudevaṁ devakīṁ ca
baddhvātha nigaḍair dṛḍhaih
mamarda taṁ śilā-pṛṣṭhe
devakī-garbhajaṁ śiśum 1.10.12

“Kaṁsa tightly bound Vasudeva and Devakī in chains. Then he crushed Devakī’s son against a stone surface.”

jāti-smaro viṣṇu-bhayāj
jātaṁ jātaṁ jaghāna ha
iti duṣṭa-vibhāvāc ca
bhūmau bhūtaṁ na saṁśayam 1.10.13

“Remembering his previous birth, out of his demonic nature and out of fear of Lord Viṣṇu he killed each son as it was born. Of this there is no doubt.”

ugrasenas tadā kruddho
yādavendro nṛpeśvarah
vārayām āsa kaṁsākhyaṁ
vasudeva-sahāya-kṛt 1.10.14

“Angry at this, emperor Ugrasena, who was the master of the Yādavas, stopped Kaṁsa and helped Vasudeva.”

kaṁsasya durābhiprāyaṁ
dṛṣṭvottasthur mahā-bhaṭāh
ugrasenānugā rakṣaṁ
cakrus te khaḍga-pāṇayah 1.10.15

“Seeing Kaṁsa’s demonic intentions, with swords in their hands, Ugrasena’s soldiers protected (Vasudeva and Devakī).”

ugrasenānugān dṛṣṭvā
kaṁsa-vīrāh samutthitāh
taih sārdham abhavad yuddhaṁ
sabhā-maṇḍapa-madhyatah 1.10.16

“Seeing Ugrasena’s soldiers, Kaṁsa’s soldiers rose against them and fought a great battle with them in the midst of the assembly.”

dvāra-deśe ’pi vīrāṇāṁ
yuddhaṁ jātaṁ parapsparam
khaḍga-prahārair ayutaṁ
janānāṁ nidhanaṁ gatam 1.10.17

“The soldiers fought a great battle in the doorway. By the blows of their swords ten thousand people died.”

kaṁso gṛhītvātha gadāṁ
pituh senāṁ mamarda ha
kaṁsasya gadayā spṛṣṭah
kecic chinna-lalāṭakāh
bhinna-pādā bhinna-mukhāś
chinnāṁsās chinna-bāhavah
adho-mukhā ūrdhva-mukhāh
sa-śastrāh patitāh kṣaṇāt 1.10.18-19

“Then Kaṁsa took his club and crushed his father’s army. Touched by his club, in a moment many fell with their weapons, their faces down or up, and their foreheads, legs, faces, shoulders, or arms broken.”

vamanto rudhiraṁ vīrā
mūrchitā nidhanaṁ gatāh
sabhā-maṇḍapam āraktaṁ
dṛśyate kṣataja-sravāt 1.10.20

“Many soldiers vomited blood, fell unconscious, and died. The assembly hall was seen to be red with streams of blood from their wounds.”

itthaṁ mahotkaṭah kaṁsah
sa nipātyodbhaṭān ripūn
krodhāḍhyo rāja-rājendro
jagrāha pitaraṁ khalah 1.10.21

“In this way Kaṁsa fell upon the enemy soldiers. Then, rich with anger, the demonic king of the kings of kings grabbed his father.”

nṛpāsanāt saṅgṛhītvā
baddhvā pāśaiś ca taṁ khalah
tan-mitraiś ca nṛpaih sārdhaṁ
kārāgāre rurodha ha 1.10.22

“The demon dragged him from the royal throne, bound him with ropes, and with his royal friends locked him in a prison cell.”

madhūnāṁ śūrasenānāṁ
deśānāṁ sarva-sampadām
siṁhāsane copaviśya
svayaṁ rājyaṁ cakāra ha 1.10.23

“Sitting on the throne, Kaṁsa then personally ruled the opulent kingdoms of the Madhus and Śūrasenas.”

pīḍitā yādavāh sarve
sambandhasya miṣais tvaram
catur-diśo ’ntaraṁ deśān
viviśuh kāla-vedinah 1.10.24

“Harassed by Kaṁsa, all the Yādavas fled to other countries on the preText of visiting relatives and waited for a better time.”

devakyāh saptame garbhe
harṣa-soka-vivardhane
vrajaṁ praṇīte rohiṇyām
anante yogamāyayā
aho garbhah kva vigata
ity ūcur māthurā janāh 1.10.25-26

“When Yogamāyā carried Devakī’s seventh fetus, which brought her both joy and grief, to Vraja and placed it in Rohiṇī’s womb, the people of Mathurā said, “Where did the child go?”

atha vraje pañca-dineṣu bhādre
tithau ca ṣaṣṭhyāṁ ca site budhe ca
uccair grahaih pañcabhir āvṛte ca
lagne tulākhye dina-madhya-deśe
sureṣu varṣatsu su-puṣpa-varṣaṁ
ghaneṣu muñcatsu ca vāri-bindūn
babhūva devo vasudeva-patnyāṁ
vibhāsayan nanda-gṛhaṁ sva-bhāsā 1.10.27-28

“Then, in Vraja, after five days, in the month of Bhādra (August-September), on the sixth day of the bright fortnight, when Mercury, five exalted planets, and the constellation Libra were on the horizon, at midday, as the demigods showered a beautiful rain of flowers and the clouds sprinkled drops of water, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, illuminating Nanda’s home with His splendor, was born from Vasudeva’s wife (Rohiṇī).”

nando ’pi kurvan chiśu-jāta-karma
dadau dvijebhyo niyutaṁ gavāṁ ca
gopān samāhūya su-gāyakānāṁ
rāvair mahā-maṅgalam ātanoti 1.10.29

“Nanda performed the child’s birth ceremony and gave in charity a million cows to the brāhmaṇas. Then he called the gopas and observed a very auspicious festival with the music of many singers and instrumentalists.”

dvaipāyano devala-devarāta-
vaśiṣṭha-vācaspatibhir mayā caāgatya tatraiva samāsthito ’bhūt
padyādibhir nanda-kṛtaih prasannah 1.10.30

“Coming there with Devala, Devarāta, Vaśiṣṭha, Bṛhaspati, and myself (Nārada), Vyāsadeva was pleased when Nanda washed his feet and worshiped Him in many ways.”

śrī-nanda uvāca
sundaro bālakah ko ’yaṁ
na dṛśyo yat-samah kvacit
kathaṁ pañca-dināj jātas
tan me brūhi mahā-mune 1.10.31

Śrī Nanda said: “Who is this handsome boy, whose equal cannot be seen anywhere? How is it He was born after only five days (in the womb)? O great sage, please tell me.”

śrī-vyāsa uvāca
aho bhāgyaṁ tu te nanda
śiśuh śeṣah sanātanah
devakyāṁ vasudevasya
jāto ’yaṁ mathurā-pure 1.10.32

Śrī Vyāsa said: “Nanda, you are very fortunate. This child is eternal Ananta Śeṣa. In Mathurā City He was conceived by Vasudeva in Devakī.”

kṛṣṇecchayā tad-udarāt
praṇīto rohiṇīṁ śubhām
nanda-rāja tvayā dṛśyo
durlabho yoginām api 1.10.33

“By Lord Kṛṣṇa’s wish He was brought to beautiful Devakī. O King Nanda, you may see Him even though the great yogīs cannot.”

tad-darśanārthaṁ prāpto ’haṁ
vedavyāso mahā-munih
tasmāt tvaṁ darśayāsmākaṁ
śiśu-rūpaṁ parāt param 1.10.34

“I, Vedavyāsa, have come here to see Him. Therefore, please show Him, the Supreme Person who is greater than the greatest and who now displays the form of an infant, to us.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha nandah śiśuṁ śeṣaṁ
darśayām āsa vismitah
dṛṣṭvā preṅkha-sthitaṁ prāha
natvā satyavatī-sutah 1.10.35

Śrī Nārada said: “Then Nanda showed them the child Aannta Śeṣa. Gazing at the child in the cradle, Vyāsa, struck with wonder, bowed down, and spoke.”

śrī-vyāsa uvāca
devādhideva bhagavan
kāma-pāla namo ’stu te
namo ’nantāya śeṣāya
sākṣād-rāmāya te namah 1.10.36

Śrī Vyāsa said: “O master of the demigods, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, O Lord who grants desires, obeisances to You! Obeisances to You, who are Lord Aananta Śeṣa! Obeisances to You, who are directly Lord Rāma!”

dharā-dharāya pūrṇāya
sva-dhāmne sīra-pāṇaye
sahasra-śirase nityaṁ
namah saṅkarṣaṇāya te 1.10.37

“Eternal obeisances to You, Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa, the maintainer of the earth, perfect and complete, effulgent and glorious, holding a plow in Your hand, and having a thousand heads!”

revatī-ramaṇa tvaṁ vai
baladevo ’cyutāgrajah
halāyudhah pralamba-ghnah
pāhi māṁ puruṣottama 1.10.38

“You are Baladeva, Revatī’s husband and the infallible Supreme Personality of Godhead’s elder brother. You are armed with a plow. You are Pralambāsura’s killer. O Supreme Person, please save me!”

balāya balabhadrāya
tālāṅkaya namo namah
nīlāmbarāya gaurāya
rauhiṇeyāya te namah 1.10.39

“Obeisances! Obeisances to You, who are known as Bala and Balabhadra, and who carry a palm-tree flag! Obeisances to You, Rohiṇī’s fair-complexioned son dressed in blue garments!”

dhenukārir muṣṭikārih
kūṭārir balvalāntakah
rukmy-arih kūpakarṇārih
kumbhaṇḍāris tvam eva hi 1.10.40

“You are the enemy of Dhenuka, Muṣṭika, Kūṭa, Rukmī, Kūpakarṇa, and Kumbhaṇḍa. You put an end to Balvala.”

kālindī-bhedano ’si tvaṁ
hastināpura-karṣakah
dvividārir yādavendro
vraja-maṇḍala-maṇḍanah 1.10.41

“You divided the Yamunā and dragged Hastināpura. You are the enemy of Dvivida. You are the king of the Yādavas and the decoration of the circle of Vraja.”

kaṁsa-bhrātṛ-prahātāsi
tīrtha-yātrākarah prabhuh
duryodhana-guruh sākṣāt
pāhi pāhi prabho jagat 1.10.42

“You are the killer of Kaṁsa’s brothers, a pilgrim who goes to holy places, the Supreme Master, and the teacher of Duryodhana. O Lord, please protect, please protect the world!”

jaya jayācyuta deva parāt para
svayam ananta dig-anta-gataṁ yaśah
sura-munīndra-phaṇīndra-varāya te
musaline baline haline namah 1.10.43

“O infallble Lord, O greater than the greatest, O Lord Ananta, O Lord whose fame is everywhere, glory, glory to You! Obeisances to You, who hold a plow and a club and are the master of the demigods, great sages, and regal serpents!”

iha paṭhet satataṁ stavanaṁ tu yah
sa tu hareh paramaṁ padam āvrajet
jagati sarva-balaṁ tv ari-mardanaṁ
bhavati tasya jayah sa-janaṁ dhanam 1.10.44

“A person who regularly reads or recites these prayers attains Lord Hari’s supreme abode. Even in this world he attains all strength. He crushes his enemies. For him are victory, wealth, and followers.”

Chapter 11 - Description of Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra’s Birth

paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān svayam
viveśa vasudevasya
manaḥ pūrvaṁ parāt paraḥ 1.11.1

“Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is greater than the greatest, personally entered Vasudeva’s heart.”

suryendu-vahni-saṅkāśo
vasudevo mahā-manāḥ
babhūvātyanta-mahasā
sākṣād yajña ivāparaḥ 1.11.2

“The great soul Vasudeva shone with a glory like the sun, moon, and fire. He was like a double of the Supreme Lord, the master of all sacrifices.”

devakyām āgate kṛṣṇe
sarveṣām abhayaṅkare
rarāja tena sā gehe
ghane saudaminī yathā 1.11.3

“When Lord Kṛṣṇa, who gives fearlessness to everyone, entered Devakī, she shone as if a great lightning flash had entered her home.”

tejovatīṁ ca tāṁ vīkṣya
kaṁsaḥ prāha bhayāturaḥ
prāpto ’yaṁ prāṇa-hantā me
pūrvam eṣā na cedṛśī 1.11.4

“Seeing her so effulgent, Kaṁsa became frightened. He said, “Now the destroyer of my life has come. She was not like this before.”

jāta-mātraṁ haniṣyāmīty
uktvāste bhaya-vihvalaḥ
paśyan sarvatra ca hariṁ
pūrva-śatruṁ vicintayan 1.11.5

““As soon as He is born I will kill Him.” Saying this, looking in all directions, and thinking of his former enemy Lord Hari, he became filled with fear.”

aho vairānubandhena
sākṣāt kṛṣṇo ’pi dṛśyate
tasmād vairaṁ prakurvanti
kṛṣṇa-prāpty-ārtham āsurāḥ 1.11.6

“Even by hating Him one is able to see Kṛṣṇa. For this reason, in order to attain the association of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the demons hate Him.”

atha brahmādayo devā
munīndrair asmad-ādibhiḥ
śauri-gehopari prāptāḥ
stavaṁ cakruḥ praṇamya tam 1.11.7

“Then the demigods headed by Brahmā and the great sages headed by me went to Vasudeva’s home, bowed down before Lord Kṛṣṇa, and offered many prayers.”

śrī-devā ūcuḥ
yaj-jāgarādiṣu bhaveṣu paraṁ hy ahetur
hetuḥ svid asya vicaranti guṇāśrayena
naitad viśanti mahad-indriya-deva-saṅghās
tasmai namo ’gnim iva vistṛta-visphuliṅgāḥ 1.11.8

The demigods said: “Taking shelter of the modes of nature, the philosophers wonder, “Is the Supreme the origin of consciousness and other states of being, or is not their origin?” As sparks come from a fire (but do not enter it), so the mahat-tattva, senses, and demigods (come from Him but) do not enter Him. Obeisances to Him, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

naiveṣituṁ prabhur ayaṁ balināṁ balīyān
māyā na śabda uta no viṣayī-karoti
tad brahma pūrṇam amṛtaṁ paramaṁ praśāntaṁ
śuddhaṁ parāt parataraṁ śaraṇaṁ gatāḥ smaḥ 1.11.9

“The strongest of the strong cannot conquer Him. The potency of illusion cannot conquer Him. The Vedas cannot bring Him within their realm of perception. Of Him, the perfect, complete, eternal, peaceful, and pure Supreme Personality of Godhead, we take shelter.”

aṁśāṁśakāṁśaka-kalādy-avatāra-vṛndāir
āveśa-pūrṇa-sahitaiś ca parasya yasya
sargādayaḥ kila bhavanti tam eva kṛṣṇaṁ
pūrṇat paraṁ tu paripūrṇatamaṁ natāḥ smaḥ 1.11.10

“With His aṁśa-avatāra, aṁśāṁśa-avatāra, kalā-avatāra, āveśa-avatāra, pūrṇa-avatāra, and many other avatāras, He creates (maintains, and destroys the material universes). To Him, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is greater than the pūrṇa-avatāra, we offer our respectful obeisances.”

manvantareṣu ca yugeṣu gatāgateṣu
kalpeṣu cāṁśa-kalayā sva-vapur bibharṣi
adyaiva dhāma paripūrṇatamaṁ tanoṣi
dharmaṁ vidhāya bhuvi maṅgalam ātanoṣi 1.11.11

“In manvantaras, yugas, and kalpas past and future You manifest Your own form in partial incarnations, but now You manifest Your original and most perfect form. Establishing the real religion, You bring auspiciousness to the earth.”

yad durlabhaṁ viśada-yogibhir apy agamyaṁ
gamyaṁ dravadbhir amalāśaya-bhakti-yogaiḥ
ānanda-kānda caratas tava manda-yānaṁ
pādāravinda-makaranda-rajo dadhāmaḥ 1.11.12

“O blissful Lord, we place (in our hearts) the nectar-pollen gently blowing from Your lotus feet as You walk, rare pollen not attained by even the purest yogīs but only by they whose pure hearts melt with love for You.”

pūrvaṁ tathātra kamanīya-vapuṣmayaṁ tvāṁ
kandarpa-koṭi-śata-mohanam adbhutaṁ ca
goloka-dhāma-dhiṣaṇa-dyutim ādadhānaṁ
rādhā-patiṁ dharaṇi-dhurya-dhanaṁ dadhāmaḥ 1.11.13

“(In our hearts) we place wonderful You, whose handsome form is more charming than a hundred million Kāmadevas, who are the glory of the realm of Goloka, who are the master of Rādhā and the wealth of the earth.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
natvā hariṁ tadā devā
brahmādyā munibhiḥ saha
gāyantas taṁ praśaṁsantaḥ
sva-dhāmāni yayur mudā 1.11.14

Śrī Nārada said: “Bowing down to Lord Hari and singing His praises, the demigods headed by Brahmā went with the sages to their own abodes.”

atha maithila-rājendra
janma-kāle hareḥ sati
ambaraṁ nirmalaṁ bhūtaṁ
nirmalāś ca diśo daśa 1.11.15

“O great king of Mithilā, at the time of Lord Hari’s birth the sky and the ten directions became very pure.”

ujjvalās tārakā jātāḥ
prasannaṁ bhūmi-maṇḍalam
nadā nadyaḥ samudrāś ca
prasannāpaḥ sarovarāḥ 1.11.16

“The stars were bright, the earth happy, and the rivers, streams, oceans, and lakes filled with pure waters.”

sahasra-dala-padmāni
śata-patrāṇi sarvataḥ
vicakāni marut-sparśaiḥ
patad-gandhi-rajāṁsi ca 1.11.17

“The breezes’ touch spreading their fragrant pollen, thousand-petal hundred-leaf lotuses bloomed everywhere.”

teṣu nedur madhukarā
nadantaś citra-pakṣiṇaḥ
śītalā manda-yānāś ca
gandhāktā vayavo vavuḥ 1.11.18

“Bees hummed, colorful birds sang, and fragrant cooling breezes gently blew.”

ṛddhā janapadā grāmā
nāgarā maṅgalāyanāḥ
devā viprā nagā gāvo
babhūvuḥ sukha-samvṛtāḥ 1.11.19

“The countries became wealthy, the towns and villages auspicious, and the demigods, brāhmaṇas, trees, and cows happy.”

deva-dundubhayo nedur
jaya-dhvani-samākulāḥ
yatra tatra mahā-rāja
sarveṣāṁ maṅgalaṁ param 1.11.20

“The drums of the demigods were filled with sounds of victory. Everywhere was great auspiciousness.”

vidyādharāś ca gandharvāḥ
siddha-kinnara-cāraṇāḥ
jaguḥ sunāyakā devās
tuṣṭuvuḥ stutibhiḥ param 1.11.21

“The Vidyādharas, Gandharvas, Siddhas, Kinnaras, and Cāraṇas sang, and the great demigods offered prayers to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

nanṛtur divi gandharvā
vidyādharyo mudānvitāḥ
pārijātaka-mandāra-
mālatī-sumanāṁsi ca
mumucur deva-mukhyāś ca
garjantaś ca ghanā jale
bhādre budhe kṛṣṇa-pakṣe
dhatrarkṣe harṣaṇe vṛṣe
karṇe ’ṣṭamyām ardha-rātre
nakṣatreśa-mahodaye
andhakārāvṛte kāle
devakyāṁ śauri-mandire
āvirāsīd dhariḥ sākṣād
āraṇyām adhvare ’gni-vat 1.11.22-24

“The happy Gandharvas and Vidyādharas danced in heaven, the great demigods threw many pārijāta, mandāra, mālatī, and sumanaḥ flowers, and the clouds thundered and sprinkled water. Then, in the month of Bhādra (August-September), during the eighth tithi of the dark fortnight, when Mercury, Taurus, and the Rohiṇī star were on the horizon, and the planets were in harṣaṇa-yoga, in the middle of the night, when everything was covered with darkness and the moon was rising, in Vasudeva’s palace Lord Hari appeared from Devakī as in a yajña fire appears from the araṇi wood.”

sphurad-akṣa-vicitra-hāriṇaṁ
vilasat-kaustubha-ratna-dhāriṇam
paridhi-dyuti-nūpurāṅgadaṁ
dhṛta-bālārka-kirīṭa-kuṇḍalam
calad-adbhuta-vahni-kaṅkanaṁ
taḍid-ūrjita-guṇa-mekhalāñcitam
madhubhṛd-dhvani-padma-mālinaṁ
nava-jāmbūnada-divya-vāsasam
sa-taḍid-ghana-divya-saubhagaṁ
cala-nīlālaka-vṛnda-bhṛn-mukham
calad-aṁśu tamo-haraṁ paraṁ
śubhadaṁ sundaram ambujekṣaṇam
kṛta-pātra-vicitra-maṇḍanaṁ
satataṁ koṭi-manoja-mohanam
paripūrṇatamaṁ parāt paraṁ
kala-veṇu-dhvani-vādya-tat-param
tam avekṣya sutaṁ yadūttamo
hari-janmotsava-phulla-locanaḥ
atha vipra-janeṣu cāśu vai
niyutaṁ san manasā gavāṁ dadau 1.11.25-29

“As he gazed at his son, who wore a glittering and colorful jewel necklace, a glittering Kaustubha gem, splendid armlets and anklets, earrings and a helmet like the rising sun, bracelets like wonderful flickering flames, a belt splendid as lightning, a lotus garland filled with the buzzing of bees, and splendid garments of new gold, and who was splendid as a dark cloud with lightning, His face with moving black locks of hair, His splendor removing the darkness, auspicious, handsome, His eyes lotus flowers, decorated with wonderful and colorful pictures and designs, more charming than ten million Kāmadevas, and who was the Supreme Personality of Godhead, greater than the greatest and fond of sweetly playing the flute, then Vasudeva, the best of the Yādavas, his eyes blossomed wide with the festive joy of seeing the birth of Lord Hari, in his heart at once gave a million cows in charity to the brāhmaṇas.”

harim ānakadundubhiḥ stavaiḥ
stavanaṁ taṁ praṇipatya vismitaḥ
akarod udita-prabhūdayo
gata-bhīḥ sūti-gṛhe kṛtāñjaliḥ 1.11.30

“Standing before the Lord in the maternity-room, astonished Vasudeva bowed down. Now fearless, with folded hands he glorified the Lord with many prayers.”

śrī-vasudeva uvāca
eko yaḥ prakṛti-guṇair anekadhāsi
hartā tvaṁ janaka utāsya pālakas tvam
nirliptaḥ sphāṭika ivādya deha-varṇais
tasmai śrī-bhuvana-pate namāmi tubhyam 1.11.31

“Although You are one, the modes of material nature make You (seem to be) many. You are the creator, maintainer, and destroyer (of the worlds). As crystal is colorless, You are untouched by material qualtities. O master of the worlds, I bow down before You.”

edhaḥsu tv anala ivātra vartamāno
yo ’nta-stho bahir api cāmbaraṁ yathā hi
adharo dharaṇir ivāsya sarva-sākṣī
tasmai te nama iva sarva-go nabhasvān 1.11.32

“Your are inside, like fire in fuel. You are outside, like the sky. You are the maintainer, like the earth. You see everything. You are present everywhere, like the air. Obeisances to You!”

bhū-bhārodbhaṭa-haraṇārtham eva
jāto go-deva-dvija-nija-vatsa-pālako ’si
gehe me bhuvi puruṣottamottamas tvaṁ
kaṁsān māṁ bhuvana-pate prapāhi pāpāt 1.11.33

“You are the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, the protector of the cows, demigods, brāhmaṇas, and Your own children, who has taken birth in my home on earth in order to remove the great armies that now burden the earth. O master of the worlds, please protect me from sinful Kaṁsa. Note: The “children” here are the Vaiṣṇavas.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ śyāma-sundaram
jñātvā natvātha taṁ prāha
devakī sarva-devatā 1.11.34

Śrī Nārada said: “Understanding that handsome and dark Śrī Kṛṣṇa was the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, Devakī, who was equal to all the demigods, bowed before Him and spoke.”

śrī-devaky uvāca
he kṛṣṇa he vigaṇitāṇḍa-pate pareśa
goloka-dhāma-dhiṣaṇa-dhvaja ādi-deva
pūrṇeśa pūrṇa paripūrṇatama prabho māṁ
tvaṁ pāhi pāhi parameśvara kaṁsa-pāpāt 1.11.35

Śrī Devakī said: “O Kṛṣṇa, O master of countless universes, O Supreme Lord, O flag of Goloka-dhāma, O original Lord, O master of the perfect, O perfect one, O most perfect original Supreme Personality of Godhead, O Supreme Controller, please protect, please protect me from Kaṁsa’s atrocities!”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tac chrutvā bhagavān kṛṣṇaḥ
paripūrṇatamaḥ svayam
sa-smito devakīṁ śauriṁ
prāha sa vṛjinārdanaḥ 1.11.36

Śrī Nārada said: “Hearing this, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, the destroyer of sins, smiled and spoke to Vasudeva and Devakī.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
iyaṁ ca pṛśniḥ pati-devatā ca
tvaṁ pūrva-sarge sutapā prajārthī
brahmājñayā divya-tapo yuvābhyāṁ
kṛtaṁ paraṁ nirjala-bhojanābhyām 1.11.37

The Blessed Lord said: “In the previous creation she was chaste Pṛśni, who worshiped her husband as if he were a god, and you were Sutapā, who desired to create children. By Brahmā’s order you both performed great and splendid austerities, fasting from food and drink.”

kāleṣu manvantarake vyatīte
tapaḥ paraṁ tat tapasaḥ prajārthi
tadā prasanno yuvayor abhūvaṁ
varaṁ paraṁ brūta mayā tadoktam 1.11.38

“When in time the manvantara had passed, your austerity to attain children continued. Then I became pleased with you and I said, “Ask a benediction.”

śrutvā yuvābhyāṁ kathitaṁ tadaiva
bhūyas sutas tvat-sādṛśaḥ kilāvayoḥ
tathāstu coktvātha gate mayi prajā-
patī hy abhūta sva-kṛtena dam-patī 1.11.39

“Hearing this, you both said, “May we have a son like You.” I said, “So be it,” and left. Then you became a prajāpati couple.”

na mat-samaḥ ko ’pi suto jagaty alaṁ
vicārya tad vām abhavaṁ pareśvaraḥ
śrī-pṛśnigarbho bhuvi viśrutaḥ punar
dvitīya-kāle ’ham upendra-vāmanaḥ 1.11.40

“Thinking, “No one is like Me,” I, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, became your son, famous in the world as Śrī Pṛśnigarbha. Then a second time I again became your son as Indra’s younger brother Vāmana.”

tathābhavaṁ hy adyatane parāt paro
nītvātha māṁ prāpaya nanda-mandiram
ato na bhūyād bhayam augrasenataḥ
sutaṁ samādāya sukhī bhaviṣyathaḥ 1.11.41

“Now I, the Supreme Lord, greater than the greatest, have again become your son. Take Me to Nanda’s palace. Don’t fear Kaṁsa. Take (Nanda’s) daughter and you will become happy.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tuṣṇīṁ bhūtvā haris tatra
tad bhūyaḥ paśyatos tayoḥ
dṛśyaṁ hy aprakaṭaṁ kṛtvā
bālo ’bhūt kau yathā nataḥ 1.11.42

Śrī Nārada said: “Now silent, Lord Hari became invisible and, as an actor does, reappeared as an infant on the ground.”

preṅkhe dhṛtvātha taṁ śaurir
yāvad gantuṁ samudyataḥ
tāvad vraje nanda-patynāṁ
yogamāyājani svataḥ 1.11.43

“As Vasudeva was placing the infant in a cradle and was about to depart, goddess Yogamāyā was born in Vraja from Nanda’s wife.”

tayā śayāne viśvasmin
rakṣakeṣu svapatsu ca
dvārā udghaṭitāḥ sarvāḥ
prasphuṭac-chrṅkhalārgalāḥ 1.11.44

“Goddess Yogamyā put all the guards to sleep, unfastened the shackles and locks, and opened all the doors.”

nirgate vasudeve ca
mūrdhni śrī-kṛṣṇa-śobhite
sūryodaye yathā sadyas
tamo-nāśo ’bhavat svataḥ 1.11.45

“When, his head decorated with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Vasudeva left, the darkness became at once destroyed, as if the sun had suddenly risen.”

ghaneṣu vyomni varṣatsu
sahasra-vadanaḥ svarāṭ
nivārayan dīrgha-phāṇair
āsāraṁ śaurim anvagāt 1.11.46

“As the clouds in the sky rained, thousant—headed Ananta Śeṣa, His great hoods giving protection from the torrents of rain, followed Vasudeva.”

ūrmy-āvartākulāvegaiḥ
siṁha-sarpādi-vāhinī
sadyo mārgaṁ dadau tasmai
kālindī saritāṁ varā 1.11.47

“Filled with great waves and whirlpools and (its shores) filled with lions, snakes, and other wild creatures, the Yamunā, the best of rivers, gave a path to Vasudeva.”

nanda-vrajaṁ sametyāsau
prasuptaṁ sarvataḥ param
śiśuṁ yāsodā-śayane
nidhāyāśu dadarśa tām 1.11.48

“Entering Nanda’s Vraja, he placed the sleeping infant on Yaśodā’s bed and glanced at Yogamāyā.”

tat-sutāṁ samupādāya
punar gehāj jagāma saḥ
tīrtvā śrī-yamunāṁ śauriḥ
svāgāre pūrvavat sthitaḥ 1.11.49

“Picking up the infant Yogamāyā, Vasudeva left the house, crossed the Yamunā, entered his prison cell, and became as he had been before.”

sutaṁ sutāṁ vā jātaṁ cā-
jñātvā gopī yaśomatī
pariśrāntā sva-śayane
suṣvāpānanda-nidrayā 1.11.50

“Not knowing whether a son or daughter had been born, exhausted Yaśodā-gopī slept in bed with a sleep of bliss.”

atha bāla-dhvaniṁ śrutvā
rakṣakāḥ samupasthitāḥ
ūcuḥ kaṁsāya vīrāya
gatvā tad-rāja-mandiram 1.11.51

“Hearing the sounds of an infant, the guards went to the royal palace and told the warrior Kaṁsa.”

sūti-gṛhaṁ tvaraṁ prāgāt
kaṁso vai bhayakāturaḥ
svasātha bhrātaraṁ prāha
rudatī dīnavat satī 1.11.52

“Overcome with fear, Kaṁsa rushed to the maternity room. Weeping like the most wretched person, the chaste sister (Devakī) spoke to her brother (Kaṁsa).”

śrī-devaky uvāca
sutām ekaṁ dehi me tvaṁ
putreṣu pramṛteṣu ca
striyaṁ hantuṁ na yogyo ’si
bhrātas tvaṁ dīna-vatsalaḥ 1.11.53

Śrī Devakī said: “Now that my sons are dead, please give me this one daughter. O brother, it is not right for you to kill a girl. You are kind to they who are miserable.”

te ’nujāhaṁ hata-sutā
kārāgāre nipātitā
dātum arhasi kalyāṇa
kalyāṇīṁ tanujāṁ ca me 1.11.54

“I am your younger sister. You killed my sons and threw me in a prison cell. O noble one, please give this beautiful daughter to me.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
aśru-mukhyā mohitayā
samācchādyātmajaṁ bahu
prārthito ’ṅkād vinirbhartsya
tāṁ sa acicchide khalaḥ 1.11.55

Śrī Nārada said: “Bewildered, and her face covered with tears, Devakī pleased and tried to hold the child in her lap. The demon Kaṁsa rebuked her and grabbed the child.”

kusaṅga-nitaraḥ pāpaḥ
khalo yadu-kulādhamaḥ
svasuḥ sutāṁ śilā-pṛṣṭhe
gṛhītvāṅghryor nyapātayat 1.11.56

“Then the sinful demon Kaṁsa, the lowest of the Yādavas, his friends all demons, holding his sister’s daughter by her feet, threw her against a stone slab.”

kaṁsa-hastāt samutpatya
tvaraṁ sā cāmbare gatā
śata-patre rathe divye
sahasra-haya-sevite 1.11.57

“Form Kaṁsa’s hand the girl flew into the sky and entered a splendid hundred-petal-lotus chariot pulled by a thousand horses.”

cāmarāndolite śubhre
sthitādṛśyata divya-dṛk
sāyudhāṣṭa-bhujā māyā
pārṣadaiḥ parisevitā
śata-sūrya-pratīkāśā
kaṁsam āha ghana-svanā 1.11.58

“He saw her standing on a splendid chariot with moving cāmaras. Then Goddess Yogamāyā, served by many attendants, her glorious form splendid as a hundred suns, her eight arms holding weapons, and her voice like thunder, spoke to Kaṁsa.”

śrī-yogamāyovāca
paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān svayam
jātaḥ kva vā tu te hantā
vṛthā dīnāṁ dunoṣi vai 1.11.59

Śrī Yogamāyā said: “Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, is already born. Where is He born? He will kill you. You torture this poor girl in vain.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā taṁ tato devī
gatā vindhyācale girau
yogamāyā bhagavatī
bahu-nāmā babhūva ha 1.11.60

Śrī Nārada said: “After saying this, the goddess went to the Vindhya hills. There goddess Yogamāyā assumed many names.”

atha kaṁso vismito ’bhūc
chrutvā mātā-vacaḥ param
devakīṁ vasudevaṁ ca
mocayām āsa bandhanāt 1.11.61

“At this Kaṁsa became astonished. Listening to his mother’s words, he released Vasudeva and Devakī from their bonds.”

kaṁsa uvāca
pāpo ’haṁ pāpa-karmāhaṁ
khalo yadu-kulādhamaḥ
yuṣmat-putra-prahantāraṁ
kṣamadhvaṁ me kṛtaṁ bhuvi 1.11.62

Kaṁsa said: “I am a sinner. I have done many sins. I am a demon. I am the lowest of the Yādavas. Please forgive what I have done in this world. (Please forgive) the killer of your sons.”

he svasaḥ śṛnu me śaure
manye kāla-kṛtaṁ tv idam
yena niścalyamāno vā
vāyuneva ghanāvaliḥ 1.11.63

“O sister, hear me. O Vasudeva, I think this was all done by time, which, as the wind moves the clouds, moves what cannot be moved.”

viśvasto ’haṁ deva-vākye
devās te ’pi mṛṣā-giraḥ
na jānāmi kva me śatrur
jātaḥ kau kathito ’nayā 1.11.64

“I believed the words of the demigods. The demigods also lie. She said my enemy is already born on the earth. Where? I don’t know.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
itthaṁ kaṁsas tad-aṅghryoś ca
patito ’śru-mukho rudan
cakāra sevāṁ paramāṁ
sauhṛdaṁ darśayaṁs tayoḥ 1.11.65

Śrī Nārada said: “Weeping, and his face covered with tears, Kaṁsa fell at their feet. He rendered great service to them and he showed friendship to them.”

aho śrī-kṛṣṇacandrasya
paripūrṇatama-prabhoḥ
dāna-dakṣaiḥ kaṭākṣaiś ca
kiṁ na syād bhūmi-maṇḍale 1.11.66

“Ah, will the charitable glances of Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, not come to the circle of the earth?”

prātaḥ-kāle tadā kaṁsaḥ
pralambādīn mahāsurān
samāhūya khalas tebhyo
’vadad uktaṁ ca māyayā 1.11.67

“Early in the morning the demon Kaṁsa called together the great demons headed by Pralambāsura and told them what Yogamāyā said.”

kaṁsa uvāca
jāto me hy anta-kṛd bhūmau
kathito yogamāyayā
anirdaśān nidarśāṁs ca
śiśūn yūyaṁ haniṣyatha 1.11.68

Kaṁsa said: “Yogamāyā said the person that will bring my end is already born on the earth. Therefore all of you please kill all infants, whether they are younger or older than ten days.”

daityā ūcuḥ
sajjasya dhanuṣo yuddhe
bhavatā dvandva-yodhinā
tāṅkārenodgatā devā
manyase taiḥ kathaṁ bhayam 1.11.69

The demons said: “When you ready your bow for a fight, the twang of its string makes the demigods flee. Why do you fear them?”

go-vipra-sādhu-śrutayo
devā dharmādayaḥ pare
viṣṇoś ca tanavo hy eṣāṁ
nāśe daitya-balaṁ smṛtam 1.11.70

“The cows, brāhmaṇas, devotees, Vedas, demigods, and others headed by the principles of religion, are the limbs of Lord Viṣṇu. When they are destroyed, the power of the demons will be remembered.”

jāto yadi mahā-viṣṇus
te śatrur yo mahī-tale
ayaṁ caitad vadhopāyo
gavādīnāṁ vihiṁsanam 1.11.71

“If your enemy Lord Mahā-Viṣṇu is indeed born on the earth then the way to kill Him is to attack the cows and these others.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
itthaṁ mahodbhaṭā duṣṭā
daiteyāḥ kaṁsa-noditāḥ
dudruvuḥ khaṁ gavādibhyo
jaghnur jātāṁs ca bālakān 1.11.72

Śrī Nārada said: “Sent by Kaṁsa, these powerful and sinful demons flew in the sky. They killed many newborn infants and many cows and other pious beings.”

ā-samudrād bhūmi-tale
viśantaś ca gṛhe gṛhe
kāma-rūpa-dhara-daityāś
ceruḥ sarpa ivābhavan 1.11.73

“From sea to sea traveling over the earth and entering house after house, these demons, who could assume any form they wished, moved about like so many snakes.”

utpathā udbhaṭā daityās
tatrāpi kaṁsa-noditāḥ
kapiḥ surāpo ’li-hato
bhūta-grasta ivābhavan 1.11.74

“The great demons sent by Kaṁsa were far from the path (of religion). They were like a monkey, or a drunkard destroyed by drink, or a man possessed by a ghost.”

Chapter 12 - Description of Śrī Nanda’s Festival

atha putrotsavaṁ jātaṁ
śrutvā nanda uṣaḥ-kṣaṇe
brāhmaṇāṁś ca samāhūya
kārayām āsa maṅgalam 1.12.1

“Hearing the happy news of his son’s birth, Nanda called the brāhmaṇas at dawn and had them perform the auspicious ceremonies.”

sa-vidhi jātakaṁ kṛtvā
nanda-rājo mahā-manāḥ
viprebhyo dakṣiṇābhiś ca
mudā lakṣaṁ gavāṁ dadau 1.12.2

“When the traditional birth-ceremony was performed, noble-hearted King Nanda happily gave the brāhmaṇas a hundred thousand cows as dakṣiṇā.”

krośa-mātraṁ rātna-sānūn
suvarṇa-śikharān girīn
sa-rasān sapta-dhanyānāṁ
dadau viprebhya ānataḥ 1.12.3

“With bowed head he gave many hills of seven grains with peaks of jewels and gold to the brāhmaṇas.”

mṛdaṅga-vīṇā-śaṅkhādyā
nedur dundubhayo muhuḥ
gāyakāś ca jagur dvāre
nanṛtur vāra-yoṣitaḥ 1.12.4

“Mṛdaṅgas, vīṇās, śaṅkhas, dundubhis, and other instruments sounded again and again, singers sang, and at the entrance vārāaganās danced.”

patākair hema-kalaśair
vitānais toraṇaiḥ śubhaiḥ
aneka-varṇaiś citraiś ca
babhau śrī-nanda-mandiram 1.12.5

“Decorated with beautiful and wonderfully colorful flags, awnings, gateways, and golden pots, Śrī Nanda’s palace was glorious.”

rathyā vīthyāś ca dehālyo
bhitti-praṅgana-vedikāḥ
tolikā-maṇḍapa-sabhā
rejur gandha-jalāmbaraiḥ 1.12.6

“The highways, footpaths, entanceways, walls, courtyards, platforms, gazebos, and assembly-halls glistened with drops of fragrant water.”

gāvaḥ suvarṇa-śṛagyaś ca
hema-mālā-lasad-galāḥ
ghaṇṭā-mañjīra-jhaṅkārā
rakta-kambala-maṇḍitāḥ 1.12.7

“The cows were decorated with red cloths and their horns were covered with gold. Their necks glistened with golden necklaces and they tinkled with many bells and ornaments.”

pīta-pucchā sa-vatsāś ca
taruṇī-kara-cihnikāḥ
haridra-kuakumair yuktās
citra-dhātu-vicitritāḥ 1.12.8

“Their tails were yellow, their calves were by their sides, they were decorated with taruṇī-karas, they were anointed with turmeric and kuakuma, and they were decorated with designs and pictures drawn in mineral colors.”

barhi-pucchair gandha-jalair
vṛṣā dharma-dhurandharāḥ
itas tato virejuḥ śrī-
nanda-dvāri manoharāḥ 1.12.9

“Decorated with peacock feathers and sprinkled with scented water, many bulls bearing the principles of religion shone gloriously at Śrī Nanda’s door.”

go-vatsā hema-mālāḍhyā
muktā-hāra-virājitāḥ
itas tato vilaṅghanto
mañjīra-caraṇāḥ sitāḥ 1.12.10

“Opulent with golden necklaces, spkendid with strands of pearls, and their hooves decorated with tinkling ornaments, white calves jumped here and there.”

śrutvā putrotsavaṁ tasya
vṛṣabhānu-varas tathā
kalāvatyā gajārūḍho
nanda-mandiram āyayau 1.12.11

“Hearing of the festival celebrating a son’s birth, King Vṛṣabhānu and Kalāvati came, riding on an elephant, to Nanda’s palace.”

nandā navopanandāś ca
tathā ṣaḍ vṛṣabhānavaḥ
nānopāyana-samyuktāḥ
sarve te ’pi samāyayuḥ 1.12.12

“The nine Nandas and Upanandas and the six Vṛṣabhānus all came, bringing many gifts.”

uṣṇīśopari mālāḍhyāḥ
pīta-kañcuka-śobhitāḥ
barha-guñja-baddha-keśā
vana-mālā-vibhūṣaṇāḥ 1.12.13

“Their turbans opulent with flower garlands and their hair tied with guñā and peacock feathers, they were decorated with forest garlands and handsome with yellow garments.”

vaṁsī-dhārā vetra-hastāḥ
su-pātra-tilakārcitāḥ
baddha-varṇāḥ parīkārā
gopās te ’pi samāyayuḥ 1.12.14

“Carrying flutes, sticks in hand, and decorated with peacock feathers and tilaka decorations, many cowherd men came.”

nṛtyantaḥ parigāyanto
dhunvanto vasanāni ca
nānopāyana-samyuktāḥ
smaśrulāḥ śiśavaḥ pare 1.12.15

“Dancing, singing, and shaking their garments, many bearded men, accompanied by their sons, brought many different gifts.”

haiyaṅgavina-dugdhānāṁ
dadhy-ajyānāṁ balīn bahūn
nītvā vṛddhā yaṣṭi-hastā
nanda-mandiram āyayuḥ 1.12.16

“Sticks in hand, and bringing many offerings of butter, ghee, milk, and yogurt, many elder cowherd men came to Nanda’s palace.”

putrotsavaṁ vrajeśasya
kathayantaḥ parasparam
prema-vihvala-bhāvaiḥ svair
ānandāśru-samākulāḥ 1.12.17

“Overcome with love and their eyes filled with tears of bliss, they talked about the birth-festival of the king of Vraja’s son.”

jāte putrotsave nandaḥ
svānandāśrv-ākulekṣaṇaḥ
pujayām āsa tān sarvāṁs
tilakādyair vidhānataḥ 1.12.18

“His eyes filled with tears of bliss, Nanda worshiped everyone with tilaka and other gifts during the festival celebrating his son’s birth.”

śrī-gopā ūcuḥ
he vrajeśvara he nanda
jātaḥ putrotsavas tathā
anapatyasyecchato ’lam
ataḥ kiṁ maṅgalaṁ param 1.12.19

The cowherd men said: “O king of Vraja, O Nanda, for a chuldless man who desires a son, what can be more auspicious than this festival of a son’s birth?”

daivena darśitaṁ cedaṁ
dinaṁ vo bahubhir dinaiḥ
kṛta-kṛtyāś ca bhūtāḥ smo
dṛṣṭvā śrī-nanda-nandanam 1.12.20

“After many days, destiny shows this day to us. As we gaze at Nanda’s son, our lives have attained success.”

he mohaneti dūrāt tam
aṅkaṁ nītvā gadiṣyasi
yadā lālana-bhāvena
bhavitā nas tadā sukham 1.12.21

“When, placing Him on your lap, you fondle Him and say, O my charming one,” then we will be happy.”

śrī-nanda uvāca
bhavatām āśiṣaḥ puṇyāj
jātaṁ saukhyam idaṁ śubham
ājñā-vārtī hy ahaṁ gopā
gopānāṁ vraja-vāsinām 1.12.22

Śrī Nanda said: “Your pious blessings make me very happy. O cowherd men, I am a servant obedient to the orders of the cowherd men of Vraja.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
śrī-nanda-rāja-suta-sambhavam adbhutaṁ ca
śrutvā visṛjya gṛha-karma tadaiva gopyaḥ
tūrṇaṁ yayuḥ sa-balayo vraja-rāja-gehān
udyat-pramoda-paripūrita-hṛn-mano-’agāḥ 1.12.23

Śrī Nārada said: “Hearing of the wonderful birth of King Nanda’s son, the gopīs at once abandoned their household duties. Their hearts, minds, and limbs flooded with rising happiness, they rushed with gifts to the palace of Vraja’s king.”

ā-nanda-mandira-purāt sva-gṛhād vrajantyaḥ
sarvā itas tata uta tvaram āvrajantyaḥ
yāna-ślathad-vasana-bhūṣaṇa-keśa-bandhā
rejur narendra pathi bhūpari mukta-muktāḥ 1.12.24

“O king, running from their own homes to Nanda’s palace, their garments, ornaments, and hair becoming loose as they went, and dropping pearls on the ground, they all were splendidly beautiful.”

jhaṅkāra-nūpura-navāagada-hema-cīra-
mañjīra-hāra-maṇi-kuṇḍala-mekhalābhiḥ
śrī-kaṇṭha-sūtra-bhuja-kaṅkana-bindukābhiḥ
pūrṇendu-maṇḍala-nava-dyutibhir virejuḥ 1.12.25

“Decorated with tinkling anklets, armlets, gold cīras, anklebells, necklaces, jewel earrings, sashes, kaṇṭha-sūtras, bracelets, and bindukās, they shone with the glory of a host of full moons.”

śrī-rājika-lavaṇa-rātri-viśeṣa-cūrṇair
go-dhūma-sarṣapa-yavaiḥ kara-lālanaiś ca
uttārya bālaka-mukhopari cāśiṣās tāḥ
sarvā dadur nṛpa jagur jagadur yaśodām 1.12.26

“With gentle strokes they anointed the child’s face with a paste of wheat and barley flours, black mustard, and salt. They all gave many blessings. O king, they sang, and then they spoke to Yaśodā.”

śrī-gopya ūcuḥ
sādhu sādhu yaśode te
diṣṭyā diṣṭyā vrajeśvari
dhanyā dhanyā parā kukṣir
yayāyāṁ janitaḥ sutaḥ 1.12.27

The gopīs said: “O Yaśodā, well done! Well done! O queen of Vraja, your fortune is very great! Your fortune is very great! Your noble womb, which gave birth to such a son, is very fortunate, very fortunate!”

icchā-yuktaṁ kṛtaṁ te vai
devena bahu-kālataḥ
rakṣa bālaṁ padma-netraṁ
su-smitaṁ śyāma-sundaram 1.12.28

“After a long time the Lord fulfilled your desire. (O Lord,) please protect this lotus-eyed, sweetly smiling, handsome, dark boy.”

śrī-yaśodovāca
bhavadīya-dayāśīrbhir
jātaṁ saukhyaṁ paraṁ ca me
bhavatīnām api paraṁ
diṣṭyā bhūyād ataḥ param 1.12.29

Śrī Yaśodā said: “Your kind blessings bring me great happiness. May you always be very fortunate.”

he rohiṇi mahā-buddhe
pūjanaṁ tu vrajaukasām
āgatānāṁ sat-kulānāṁ
yatheṣṭaṁ hīpsitaṁ kuru 1.12.30

“O very intelligent Rohiṇī, please worship the noble people of Vraja who have come here. Please fulfill their desires.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
rohiṇī rāja-kanyāpi
tat-karau dāna-śīlinau
tatrāpi noditā dāne
dadāv ati-mahā-manāḥ 1.12.31

Śrī Nārada said: “Even though she was a princess, when asked in this way noble-hearted Rohiṇī gave charity with two generous hands.”

garua-varṇā divya-vāsā
ratnābharaṇa-bhūṣitā
vyacarad rohiṇī sākṣāt
pūjayantī vrajaukasaḥ 1.12.32

“Then fair-complexioned, splendidly dressed, jewel decorated Rohiṇī worshiped the people of Vraja.”

paripūrṇatame sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇe vrajam āgate
nadatsu nara-tūryeṣu
jaya-dhvanir abhūn mahān 1.12.33

“When Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, came to Vraja Village, many tūryas sounded and a great cry of “Victory!” rose up.”

dadhi-kṣīra-ghṛtair gopā
gopyo haiyaṅgavair navaiḥ
śiśicur harṣitās tatra
jagur uccaiḥ parasparam 1.12.34

“Then the happy gopas and gopīs sprinkled each other with fresh butter and loudly sang.”

bahir antaḥ-pure jāte
sarvato dadhi-kardame
vṛddhāś ca sthūla-dehāś ca
petur hāsyaṁ kṛtaṁ paraiḥ 1.12.35

“When a large elder gopa fell in the yogurt-mud that was everywhere, inside and outside the homes, the others laughed.”

sūtāḥ pauraṇikāḥ proktā
māgadhā vaṁsa-saṁśakāḥ
vandinas tv amala-prajñāḥ
prastāva-sadṛśoktayaḥ 1.12.36

“Then many intelligent and pure sūtas, paurāṇikas, māgadhas, vaṁśa-śaṁsakas, and vandīs recited many prayers.”

tebhyo nando mahā-rājo
sahasraṁ gāḥ pṛthak pṛthak
vaso-’laṅkāra-ratnāni
hayebhān akhilān dadau 1.12.37

“To each one Nanda Mahārāja gave a thousand cows and many garments, ornaments, jewels, horses, and elephants.”

vandibhyo māgadhebhyaś ca
sarvebhyo bahulaṁ dhanam
vavarṣa dhanavad-gopo
nanda-rājo vrajeśvaraḥ 1.12.38

“King Nanda, the wealthy gopa ruler of Vraja, showered great wealth on the vandīs, māgadhas, and everyone else.”

nidhiḥ siddhiś ca vṛddhiś ca
bhuktir muktir gṛhe gṛhe
vīthyāṁ vīthyāṁ luṭhantīva
tad-icchā kasyacin na hi 1.12.39

“Wealth, mystic powers, glory, sense gratification, and liberation rolled about on the ground in every home and on every pathway. No one had any desires left to be fulfilled.”

sanat-kumāra-kapila-
śuka-vyāsādibhiḥ saha
haṁsa-datta-pulastyādyair
mayā brahma jagāma ha 1.12.40

“Then, accompanied by me and by the great sages headed by Sanat-kumāra, Kapila, Śukadeva, Vyāsa, Haṁsa, Datta, and Pulastya, the demigod Brahmā went there.”

haṁsārūḍho hema-varṇo
mukuṭī kuṇḍalī sphuran
catur-mukho veda-kartā
dyotayan maṇḍalaṁ diśam 1.12.41

“Wearing a crown and earings, his complexion the color of gold, and filling the directions with light, four-faced Brahmā, the author of the Vedas, came, riding on a swan.”

tathā tam anu bhūtāḍhyo
vṛṣārūḍho maheśvaraḥ
rathārūḍho raviḥ sākṣād
gajārūḍhaḥ purandaraḥ 1.12.42

“Accompanied by many ghosts and demons, Lord Śiva came, riding on a bull. The sun-god came riding on a chariot. Indra came riding on an elephant.”

vāyuś ca khañjanārūḍho
yamo mahiṣa-vāhanaḥ
dhanadaḥ puṣpakārūḍho
mṛgārūḍho kṣapeśvaraḥ 1.12.43

“Vāyu came riding on a khañjana bird. Yamarāja came riding on a buffalo. Kuvera came riding on a flower chariot. The moon-god came riding on a deer.”

ajārūḍho vītihotro
varuṇo makara-sthitaḥ
mayūra-sthaḥ kārttikeyo
bhāratī haṁsa-vahinī 1.12.44

“Agni came riding on a ram. Varuṇa came riding on a shark. Kārttikeya came riding on a peacock. Sarasvatī came riding on a swan.”

lakṣmīś ca garuḍārūḍhā
durgākhyā siṁha-vāhinī
go-rūpa-dhāriṇī pṛthvī
vimāna-sthā samāyayau 1.12.45

“Lakṣmī came riding on Garuḍa. Durgā came riding on a lion. Manifesting the form of a cow, the earth-goddess came riding in an airplane.”

dolārūḍhā divya-varṇā
mukhyāḥ ṣoḍaśa-mātṛkāḥ
ṣaṣṭhī ca śibikārūḍhā
khaḍginī yaṣṭi-dhāriṇī 1.12.46

“The sixteen Mātṛkās came riding on a swing, and Goddess Ṣaṣṭhī, holding a sword and club, came riding on a palanquin.”

maṅgalo vanarārūḍho
bhāsārūḍho budhaḥ smṛtaḥ
gīṣpatiḥ kṛṣṇasāra-sthaḥ
śukro gavaya-vāhanaḥ 1.12.47

“The ruler of the planet Mars came riding on a gorilla. The ruler of the planet Mercury came riding on a rooster. The ruler of the planet Jupiter came riding on a black stag. The ruler of the planet Venus came riding on an ox.”

śaniś ca makarārūḍha
uṣṭra-sthaḥ siṁhika-sutaḥ
koṭi-bālārka-saṅkāśa
āyayau nanda-mandiram 1.12.48

“The ruler of the planet Saturn came riding on a shark. The ruler of the planet Rāhu came riding on a camel. Splendid as millions of suns, they came to Nanda’s palace.”

kolāhala-samāyuktaṁ
gopa-gopī-gaṇākulam
nanda-mandiram abhyetya
kṣaṇaṁ sthitvā yayuḥ surāḥ 1.12.49

“Finally coming to Nanda’s palace, which was filled with many gopas and gopīs making a great tumult, the demigods paused for a moment, and then entered.”

paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ bāla-rūpiṇam
natvā dṛṣṭvā tadā devās
cakrus tasya stutiṁ parām 1.12.50

“After offering obeisances to Him and gazing at Him, the demigods offered many eloquent prayers to Śrī Kṛṣṇa who, although manifesting the form of a child, was the original Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

Chapter 13 - The Liberation of Pūtanā

śrī-nārada uvāca
śaury-anāmaya-pṛcchārthaṁ
karaṁ dātuṁ nṛpasya ca
putrotsavaṁ kathayituṁ
nande śrī-mathuraṁ gate
kaṁsena preṣitā duṣṭā
pūtanā ghāta-kāriṇī
pureṣu grāma-ghoṣeṣu
carantī gharghara-svanā 1.13.1-2

“While, to ask about Vasudeva’s welfare, to pay his tax to the king, and to describe his son’s birth-festival, Nanda was in Śrī Mathurā, the murderess Pūtanā, sent by Kaṁsa, was traveling among cities, towns, and cowherd villages, making a menacing rumbling sound.”

atha gokulam āsādya
gopa-gopī-gaṇākulam
rūpaṁ dadhāra sā divyaṁ
vapuḥ ṣoḍaśa-varṣikam 1.13.3

“Coming to Gokula, which was filled with many gopas and gopīs, the demoness assumed the form of a splendid sixteen-year old girl.”

na ke ’pi rurudhur gopāḥ
sundarīṁ tāṁ ca gopikāḥ
śacīṁ vāṇīṁ ramāṁ rambhāṁ
ratiṁ ca kṣipatīm iva 1.13.4

“No gopa or gopī stopped this beautiful girl, who seemed to eclipse the goddesses Śacī, Sarasvatī, Lakṣmī, Rambhā, and Rati.”

rohiṇyāṁ ca yaśodāyāṁ
dharṣitāyāṁ sphurat-kucā
aṅkam ādāya taṁ bālaṁ
lālayantī punaḥ punaḥ 1.13.5

“Ignoring Rohiṇī and Yaśodā, she took the infant on her lap, fondled Him again and again, and then exposed her breasts.”

dadau śiśor mahā-ghorā
kālākūtāvṛtaṁ stanam
prāṇaiḥ sārdhaṁ papau dugdhaṁ
kaṭuṁ roṣāvṛto hariḥ 1.13.6

“When the very horrible demoness gave her poison-covered breast to Him, infant Lord Hari became angry and drank her life-breath along with the bitter milk.”

muñca muñca vadantītthaṁ
dhavantī piḍita-stanā
nītvā bahir gatā taṁ vai
gata-māyā babhūva ha 1.13.7

“Screaming “Leave me! Leave me!”, and her breast filled with pain, she ran outside, bringing the infant with her and her magical illusion now gone.”

patan-netrā śveta-gātrā
rudantī patitā bhuvi
nanāda tena brahmāṇḍaṁ
sapta lokair bilaiḥ saha 1.13.8

“Screaming, her eyes bulging and limbs perspiring, she fell to the ground, making a sound that filled the universe with its seven planetary systems and empty regions of outer space.”

cacāla vasudhā dvīpais
tad adbhutam ivābhavat
ṣaṭ-krośaṁ sā dṛḍhān dīrghān
vṛkṣān pṛṣṭha-tale gatān
cūrṇī-cakāra vapuṣā
vajrāagena nṛpeśvara
vadantas te gopa-gaṇā
vīkṣya ghoraṁ vapur mahat 1.13.9-10

“A great wonder happened. The earth and all its continents moved. She became twelve mils long and her thunderbolt body broke many tall and strong trees and made them fall to the ground. O king of kings, when the gopas saw her horrible gigantic body, they said:

asyā aṅguli-go bālo
na jīvati kadācana
tasyā urasi sānandaṁ
krīḍantaṁ su-smitaṁ śiśum
dugdhaṁ pitvā jṛmbhamānaṁ
taṁ dṛṣṭvā jagṛhuḥ striyaḥ
yaśodayā ca rohiṇyā
nidhāyorasi vismitāḥ 1.13.11-12

The child was crushed by her fingers. He could not have remained alive.” Then, seeing the smiling infant yawn after drinking the demoness’ milk, and then play on her breast, the astonished women, along with Yaśodā and Rohiṇia, grabbed Him, and pulled Him down from the breast.”

sarvato bālakaṁ nītvā
rakṣāṁ cakrur vidhānataḥ
kālindī-puṇya-mṛt-toyair
go-puccha-bhramaṇādibhiḥ 1.13.13

“Then they took the child and acted to give Him protection by anointing Him with the Yamunā’s sacred water and clay, by having a cow wave its tail before Him, and by doing other things also.”

go-mūtra-go-rajobhiś ca
snāpayitvā tv idaṁ jaguḥ 1.13.14

“Bathing Him with cow dust and cow urine, they chanted:”

śrī-gopya ūcuḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇas te śiraḥ pātu
vaikuṇṭhaḥ kaṇṭham eva hi
śvetadvīpa-patiḥ karṇau
nāsikaṁ yajña-rūpa-dhṛk 1.13.15

The gopīs said: “May Śrī Kṛṣṇa protect Your head. May Lord Vaikuṇṭha protect Your neck. May the Lord of Śvetadvīpa protect Your ears. May Lord Yajña protect Your nose.”

nṛsiṁho netra-yugmaṁ ca
jihvāṁ daśarathātmajaḥ
adharāv avatāṁ te tu
nara-nārāyanāv ṛṣī 1.13.16

“May Lord Nṛsiṁha protect Your two eyes. May Lord Rāma protect Your tongue. May Lord Nara-Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi protect Your lips.”

kapolau pāntu te sākṣāt
sanakādyāḥ kalā hareḥ
bhalaṁ te sveta-vārāho
nārado bhrū-late ’vatu 1.13.17

“May the four Kumāras. who are Lord Hari’s kalā expansions, protect Your cheeks. May Lord Śveta Varāha protect Your forehead. May Lord Nārada protect the two vines of Your eyebrows.”

cibukaṁ kapilaḥ pātu
dattātreya uro ’vatu
skandhau dvāv ṛṣabhaḥ pātu
karau matsyaḥ prapātu te 1.13.18

“May Lord Kapila protect Your chin. May Lord Dattātreya protect Your chest. May Lord Ṛṣabha protect both Your shoulders. May Lord Matsya protect Your hands.”

dor-daṇḍaṁ satataṁ rakṣet
pṛthuḥ pṛthula-vikramaḥ
udaraṁ kamaṭhaḥ pātu
nābhiṁ dhanvantariś ca te 1.13.19

“May powerful Lord Pṛthu always protect Your arms. May Lord Kūrma protect Your abdomen. May Lord Dhanvantari protect Your navel.”

mohinī guhya-deśaṁ ca
kaṭiṁ te vāmano ’vatu
pṛṣṭhaṁ paraśurāmaś ca
tavorū bādarāyaṇaḥ 1.13.20

“May Śrīmatī Mohinī protect Your private parts. My Lord Vāmana protect Your hips. May Lord Paraśurāma protect Your back. May Lord Vyāsa protect Your thighs.”

balo jānu-dvayaṁ pātu
jaṅghe buddhaḥ prapātu te
padau pātu sa-gulphau ca
kalkir dharma-patiḥ prabhuḥ 1.13.21

“May Lord Balarāma protect Your knees. May Lord Buddha protect Your shins. May Lord Kalki, the master of religion, protect Your ankles and feet.”

sarva-rakṣā-karaṁ divyaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇa-kavacaṁ param
idaṁ bhagavatā dattaṁ
brahmaṇe nābhi-paṅkaje 1.13.22

“The Supreme Personality of Godhead gave this glorious and transcendental Śrī Kṛṣṇa-kavaca (armor of Lord Kṛṣṇa), which gives all protection, to the demigod Brahmā on the lotus flower of His navel.”

brahmaṇā śambhave dattaṁ
śambhur durvāsase dadau
durvāsāḥ śrī-yaśomatyai
prādāc chrī-nanda-mandire 1.13.23

“Then Brahmā gave it to Lord Śiva. Lord Śiva gave it to Durvāsā Muni. Durvāsā Muni gave it to Śrī Yaśodā in Śrī Nanda’s palace.”

anena rakṣāṁ kṛtvāsya
gopībhiḥ śrī-yaśomatī
pāyayitvā stanaṁ dānaṁ
viprebhyaḥ pradadau mahat 1.13.24

“In this way Śrī Yaśodā and the gopīs gave protection to (infant Kṛṣṇa). Then Śrī Yaśodā gave Him her breast milk to drink, and then she gave great charity to the brāhmaṇas.”

tadā nandādayo gopā
āgatā mathurā-purāt
dṛṣṭvā ghoraṁ pūtanākhyaṁ
babhūvur bhaya-vihvalāḥ 1.13.25

“When, just returned from Mathurā City, the gopas headed by Nanda saw horrible Pūtanā, they were overcome with fear.”

chitvā kuṭhārais tad-dehaṁ
gopāḥ śrī-yamunā-taṭe
anekāś ca citāḥ kṛtvā
dahayām āsur eva tam 1.13.26

“Cutting it up with axes, and making many pyres by Śrī Yamunā’s shore, the gopas burned her body.”

elā-lavaṅga-śrīkhaṇḍa-
tagarāguru-gandhi-bhṛt
dhūmo dagdhasya dehasya
pavitrasya samutthitaḥ 1.13.27

“The smoke that rose from her now purified burning body was fragrant as elā, lavaṅga, śrīkhaṇḍa, tagra, and aguru.”

aho kṛṣṇam ṛte kaṁ vā
vrajāma śaraṇaṁ tv iha
pūtanāyai mokṣa-gatiṁ
dadau patita-pāvanaḥ 1.13.28

“Of whom else but Kṛṣṇa will we take shelter? The purifier of the fallen, He gave liberation to Pūtanā.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
keyaṁ vā rakṣasī pūrvaṁ
pūtanā bāla-ghātinī
viṣa-stanā duṣṭa-bhāvā
paraṁ mokṣaṁ kathaṁ gatā 1.13.29

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “Who was this demoness in her previous birth? Why did evil Pūtanā, who murdered children by giving them her poisoned breast, attain ultimate liberation?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
bali-yajñe vāmanasya
dṛṣṭvā rūpam ataḥ param
bali-kanyā ratnamālā
putra-snehaṁ cakāra ha 1.13.30

“Seeing the form of Lord Vāmana in Bali Mahāraja’s yajña, Bali’s daughter Ratnamālā felt for the Lord (a mother’s) love for her son.”

etādṛśo yadi bhaved
bālas taṁ hi śuci-smitam
pāyayāmi stanaṁ tena
prasannaṁ me manas tadā 1.13.31

(She thought:) “If I had a son like Him, then I would give that gloriously smiling son the milk from my breast to drink. In that way my heart would become happy. . .”

baleḥ parama-bhaktasya
sutāyai vāmano hariḥ
manorathas tu te bhuyān
manasy api varaṁ dadau 1.13.32

“. . .may your desire be fulfilled.” In this way the Personality of Godhead, Lord Vāmana, gave a blessing in his heart to the daughter of His great devotee Bali Mahārāja.”

sābhavad dvāparānte vai
pūtanā nāma viśrutā
śrī-kṛṣṇa-sparśa-sambhūtā
paraṁ prāpta-manorathā 1.13.33

“At the end of Dvāpara-yuga she became known as Pūtanā. Then, by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s touch, she attained her desire.”

Chapter 14 - The Liberation of Śakaṭāsura and Tṛṇāvarta

śrī-garga uvāca
ity evaṁ kathitaṁ divyaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇa-caritaṁ varam
yaḥ śṛṇoti naro bhaktyā
sa kṛtārtho na saṁśayaḥ 1.14.1

Śrī Garga Muni said: “A person who with devotion hears Lord Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental pastimes becomes perfect and successful. Of this there is no doubt.”

śrī-śaunaka uvāca
sudhā-kāṇḍāt paraṁ miṣṭaṁ
